# Changelog

All notable changes to hypergumbo are documented in this file. The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

- Released **tool** is at: v6.1.0
- Released **schema** is at: v0.14.2

This changelog tracks the **tool version** (package releases). The **schema version** is tracked separately in `schema.py` as `SCHEMA_VERSION`. The schema version changes when `docs/schema.json` has significant updates: breaking changes to the behavior map output format (minor bump) or additions like new type definitions for YAML validation (patch bump).

> **Looking for the reader-friendly summary?** See **[docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md)** for the audience-organized release notes (CLI users, JSON consumers, per language/framework). This file is the **implementer log**: structured, mechanism-level entries per release.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **A strict substrate loader hardens every `--input` consumer at one chokepoint (INV-sozop, WI-jukah, INV-gapib, WI-marul — cli-input substrate-loader keystone).** The single behavior-map read helper (`behavior_map_io.load_behavior_map`) was a bare `json.load` with zero validation, so a bad `--input` failed four different silent/ugly ways across `symbols`/`slice`/`explain`/`io-boundaries`/`routes`/`test-coverage`/`config`/`dead-code-maybe`/`verify-claims`/`sketch`: malformed/empty JSON escaped as an unhandled Python **traceback** (INV-sozop); a wrong-shape dict (no `nodes` key) was silently accepted and printed **"No X found" with `rc=0`** (WI-jukah); a wrong-`view` projection (a `slice`/`tiered` envelope) was consumed **as if it were a behavior map** (INV-gapib); and a future/older/absent `schema_version` **read silently** with no compatibility signal (WI-marul). New `behavior_map_io.load_substrate(path, *, expected_view="behavior_map")` + typed `SubstrateError` centralize four guards: a `json.JSONDecodeError` or non-object root → `SubstrateError`; a dict lacking the structural `nodes` key → `SubstrateError`; a `view` field **present and different** from the expected view → `SubstrateError` (a *missing* `view` is accepted — legacy/minimal maps omit it, and the concern is a *wrong* view, not an absent one); and `schema_version` **warn-first** (absent or mismatched → a stderr `Warning:` but the map still loads, since an older/newer map is usually still readable — rejecting would be over-strict). The reconciliation is deliberate: WI-jukah lists `schema_version`/`nodes` as "required keys", but the co-bundled WI-marul governs `schema_version` as a *warn* signal, so `nodes` is the hard-required structural discriminator (the silent-empty root cause) while `schema_version` warns. The CLI's top-level `main()` dispatch gains a narrow `except SubstrateError` that converts the typed failure to a clean `rc=2` + message for every consumer at once (the general top-level exception handler remains the separate WI-himas item); all 11 `load_behavior_map` call sites in `cli.py` now route through `load_substrate` (`load_behavior_map` stays as the permissive low-level reader for auxiliary artifacts like compact outputs). Behavioral evidence (live): a malformed `--input` → `rc=2` `not valid JSON`; a `nodes`-less dict → `rc=2` `missing required key(s): nodes` (was `rc=0` "No symbols found"); a `view: tiered` map → `rc=2` `wrong view — expected 'behavior_map', got 'tiered'`; a `schema_version: 0.0.1-ancient` map → stderr `Warning:` + `rc=0` with symbols still emitted; a current-version map → clean (`rc=0`, empty stderr). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`explain` gains disambiguation/bounding flags and field-drift guards (WI-nanut, WI-dazob — cli-output F3).** `explain` previously accepted only `--path/--input/-x/--with-source/-t/--provenance`, so an ambiguous name (e.g. `main` on a multi-binary repo) dumped every match — 15,713 stdout lines on the self-corpus. New flags bring it to parity with `symbols`: `--language L` and `--file SUFFIX` narrow the match pool, `--first` accepts the top match, and `--limit N` caps sections for non-ambiguous same-file duplicates. Separately (WI-dazob), the renderer now (a) flags an edge type registered nowhere as `unrecognized edge type '<type>'` instead of presenting it as a legitimate relationship, (b) surfaces the `EdgeTypeSpec.description` for a registered-but-unstyled edge type (F80.A1 — the registry descriptions existed but were never rendered), and (c) prints a field-presence drift warning to stderr when the substrate's `schema_version` differs from this build's, so a consumer diffing `explain` output can tell "code changed" from "substrate schema changed". Additive flags (flag-matrix baseline regenerated); no schema bump. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`cache-status` and `catalog` gain `--format json` (WI-dulul, WI-soroz — cli-output sub-A / INV-fivar).** Two read subcommands were text-only, so programmatic consumers couldn't inspect the cache or the analyzer/framework catalog. `cache-status --format json` emits `{cache_dir, total_entries, total_size_bytes, entries:[{fingerprint, size_bytes, entry_count, age_days}], honk_threshold_bytes}` (covering the empty / populated / per-repo paths; `--quiet` still suppresses all output); `catalog --format json` emits `{passes:[{id, availability, description, available}], framework_patterns:[{name, path}], suggested, large_directory}`. Both flow through the `add_schema_envelope` gateway (WI-gogif F1). Additive `--format` flag, default `text` (existing text output unchanged). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`io-boundaries` and `verify-claims` gain the canonical `--format text|json` spelling; `--json` kept as a back-compat alias (WI-kitud — cli-output, closes the flag-spelling gap under WI-gogif).** These two read subcommands used a `--json` boolean while the other six read views (`cache-status` / `catalog` / `config` / `dead-code-maybe` / `routes` / `test-coverage`) had already standardized on `--format text|json` — so a consumer driving multiple subcommands had to learn two flag spellings (the fragmentation WI-gogif's own "one consistent convention" expectation names as the defect). Both now expose `--format {text,json}` (default `text`), routed through a shared `_read_view_wants_json(args)` helper that treats `--json` as an alias (`--json` wins when both are given — it has always meant JSON). Wire output is byte-identical; the `io-boundaries` (`IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION`) and `verify-claims` (`VERIFY_CLAIMS_SCHEMA_VERSION`) envelopes are unchanged. Additive `--format` flag (flag-matrix baseline regenerated); no schema bump. This is the last piece of the cli-output block: with it, all JSON-emitting text-defaulting read views share one flag spelling, unblocking the WI-gogif grandparent (`slice`/`compact` are a separate JSON-only category — no text mode, so out of scope). Core package 100% coverage.
- **Manifest-silent web apps now surface routes: allowlisted route frameworks promote on a bare exact import (WI-tosul Phase 2 — the dead-code route-monoculture root).** A repo that imports a route framework at top level (`from flask import Flask`, `const app = express()`) but declares no dependency manifest previously got **zero routes** — `refine_frameworks`'s import-promotion bare-name arm required a *compound-submodule* import (`flask.blueprints`; WI-pusad's FP guard), and a bare exact import (`python:flask`) was rejected, so the framework's YAML never loaded and its routes stayed dark. This is the single upstream cause of the "dead-code 99% Python" monoculture (real Python repos reliably declare flask/django in `requirements.txt`; other-language / manifest-silent apps went dark). A curated `_BARE_EXACT_PROMOTE_ROUTE_FRAMEWORKS` allowlist now lets a **high-confidence set of dedicated web frameworks** promote on a bare exact import (Python: flask/fastapi/sanic/litestar/quart/starlette/bottle/tornado/pyramid/django/flask-appbuilder; JS/TS: express/koa/fastify; Ruby: sinatra/padrino). The FP guard is intact for every other bare name — `graphql` (type defs — WI-rofiz), `react`/`solid` (frontend UI), `plug` (Elixir middleware spec), `aiohttp` (HTTP *client*), `next`/`nex` (generic tokens) all stay gated — and the pre-existing `allowed_langs` gate keeps residual risk within-language. Seeded conservatively per the Phase-2 scout (lab notebook `phase2_manifest_gating_scout_07072026.md`); the NEEDS-BAKEOFF middle set (aiohttp/falcon/grape/vapor/…) expands only after bakeoff FP-validation. Verified live: a Flask route with no `requirements.txt` goes 0 → 1 route marker + 2 route concepts. Two independent latent blockers the scout surfaced are filed separately (INV-fosam: `_FRAMEWORK_ALIASES` name-mismatch drops next/adonis/aspnet/vertx/zio routes even *with* a manifest; WI-nizuv: JVM/Scala/PHP coord-vs-namespace import patterns). Core package 100% coverage.
- **Canonical `route_of()` / `is_route()` accessor consolidates route detection (WI-tosul, target-D).** Route-ness is surfaced two ways at the analyzer/enrichment layer — the ADR-0027 route marker (`meta.framework_role == 'route'` + `route_path`/`http_method`, analyzer-level, fires with no manifest) and framework-YAML concept enrichment (a `meta.concepts` entry with `concept == 'route'` carrying path/method/framework, manifest-gated) — and ~15 consumer sites (cmd_routes, handler/reverse slices, supply-chain tier promotion, entrypoint detection, the http linker) each re-implemented the OR of the two, including a verbatim duplicate accessor pair inside `linkers/http.py`. New `hypergumbo_core.routes` module: `route_of(symbol) -> {path, method, framework, protocol} | None` (**marker-first** precedence per the decision-register — the ADR-0027 marker is authoritative, the concept is the manifest-gated projection; the `WS` sentinel is normalized out of `http_method` into a dedicated `protocol` field) + `is_route(symbol)`. First consumers folded onto it: cli's `_is_route_symbol` and `_extract_route_info` (behavior-preserving — the raw `WS` method reconstructed for the latter's established `{method, path}` contract; 195 route/slice/parity tests unchanged). The foundation for WI-tosul's route-surfacing unification; the http.py duplicate fold, entrypoint delegation, and additive `route_framework`/`route_protocol` payload keys follow, ahead of the higher-leverage manifest-gating relaxation + per-framework detection ratchet.
- **WI-zipis producer-sweep drain: 7 language-construct `Symbol.kind` values registered + 1 folded, draining the ratchet backlog to zero (audit-findings 0015; INV-numat).** The 8 values the producer-side ratchet baselined — all surfaced by positional-helper / nested-closure emit sites the literal-grep diagnostics missed — got per-value ADR-0027 verdicts (four-leakage-test, one agent per value). **REGISTERED** as canonical language constructs on `AXIS_LANGUAGE_CONSTRUCT`: `dart` `extension`, `powershell` `filter`/`workflow`, `tlaplus` `operator`/`assumption`, `smithy` `list`/`map` — each a genuine distinct construct with no canonical equivalent (smithy `list`/`map` are peer aggregate shapes to the already-registered `union`/`enum`; there is no generic collection/associative kind to fold to). **FOLDED**: `structure`→`struct` at both producers (Smithy `structure_statement` + Lean 4 `structure`) — a pure synonym of the record/product-type `struct` (property-derivability fires: `structure` = `struct` + `language`). With all 8 drained, `.ci/producer-axis-coherence-baseline.json`'s `Symbol.kind` list is empty and the producer-side ratchet is green across all three vocab axes with a **zero backlog** — the INV-numat producer-side closure state. Verdicts filed as `docs/audits/0015`; `docs/schema.json` (open `Symbol.kind` enum) + the schema-coverage baseline synced. Core + lang-extended1 100% coverage.
- **Producer-side axis-coherence linter now descends module-local emission helpers and binds positional arguments (WI-zipis).** The L3 producer-coherence check (`producer_coherence.py`) previously matched only direct `Symbol(kind="…")` / `Edge.create(evidence_type="…")` calls carrying the axis value as a *keyword literal*, so a value routed through a module-local helper and passed *positionally* — the `_make_proto_symbol(…, "service")` shape where the helper does `Symbol(kind=kind)` — was invisible, and the axis reported a false clean bill (the INV-numat "cohort-counting undercounts" keystone; audit-0013's false-RESOLVED `rpc`/`service`). `find_producer_coherence_violations(..., descend_helpers=True)` now discovers, by a module-local fixpoint, every helper whose parameter flows into a known sink (multi-hop, e.g. `_outer`→`_inner`→`Symbol(kind=)`) and binds positional **and** keyword arguments at each helper call site (skipping `*args` splats and default-omitted params). Opt-in: the established direct-path gate is unchanged by default. Running the extended sweep on the tree surfaces **8** previously-invisible unregistered `Symbol.kind` emissions the blind gate rated clean — `proto` (`service`/`rpc`), `smithy` (`service`/`structure`/`list`/`map`), `powershell` (`filter`/`workflow`) — the producer-side census that replaces per-cohort violation-counting as INV-numat's closure gate.
- **Producer-side axis-coherence descent now also covers nested-closure emit helpers (WI-zipis, cont.).** The `descend_helpers` fixpoint above scanned only module-level functions, but the dominant emit-helper shape across analyzers is a **nested closure** — `def make_symbol(...): … Symbol(kind=kind)` defined *inside* the analyzer's `analyze(...)` (thrift, ocaml, dart, haskell, lean, solidity, tlaplus, and more). Discovery now walks every function def (`ast.walk`), not just top-level ones. Not cosmetic: on the live tree the helper-descent `Symbol.kind` census rises **5→9** — four unregistered kinds were hidden *entirely* in nested closures (`dart` `extension`, `lean` `structure`, `tlaplus` `operator`/`assumption`), on top of the module-level ones; draining only the module-level set would have produced a false zero (the INV-numat trap the sweep exists to prevent). Cross-*module* helper sinks are **verified-absent** for this axis — every Symbol-emitting helper is analyzer-local, and the only shared `kind`-taking helper (`make_symbol_id`) builds the id string, not `Symbol.kind`. Known residual: a value passed *positionally to the direct constructor* (`Symbol("id","name",…,"kind")`) is still unbound (analyzers use keyword form there); dynamic/unresolvable emits (`node.type`, dict lookups) remain advisory, not strict.
- **The producer-side axis-coherence gate is now a shrink-only ratchet — INV-numat's producer-side closure mechanism, replacing per-cohort violation-counting (WI-zipis).** `scripts/check-producer-axis-coherence` (and the CI test `test_live_tree_producer_axis_ratchet`) now runs the full descend-aware sweep — every unregistered `Symbol.kind` / `Edge.evidence_type` / `Edge.edge_type` value emitted through the direct constructor, module-level helpers, **or** nested-closure helpers — and diffs the live value set against a committed baseline (`.ci/producer-axis-coherence-baseline.json`, keyed by value not file:line). A **new** unregistered value fails the gate (a regression); a baselined value that has since been drained must be removed (the baseline can only shrink). This is the trustworthy producer-side check the INV-numat methodology ruling demands: per-cohort `validation_report` counting provably undercounts because it never sees positional-helper or nested-closure producers. New importable surface: `unregistered_emitted_values` + `unregistered_symbol_kinds`/`unregistered_evidence_types`/`unregistered_edge_types` (descend-aware, registry-filtered) and `ratchet_diff`; `find_emitted_literal_values` gains `descend_helpers`. Initial baseline: **8** `Symbol.kind` values pending drain (`dart` `extension`; `powershell` `filter`/`workflow`; `smithy`+`lean` `structure`; `smithy` `list`/`map`; `tlaplus` `operator`/`assumption`) — all *language constructs* to register per ADR-0027; `evidence_type`/`edge_type` clean. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Bash external-program launches now surface as a disclosed `command_launch` io-boundary cohort (WI-javoh).** A call inside a bash function to a name that is neither a defined shell function, nor a resolver-resolved symbol, nor a shell builtin (`echo`/`cd`/`local`/`export`/`read`/… — a comprehensive `SHELL_BUILTINS` denylist) is an **external program launch** — a subprocess crossing (arbitrary code execution). The bash analyzer now emits a `calls` edge for each such launch, `is_resolved=False` with an `ExternalRef` dst (`bash:<cmd>:0-0:<cmd>:unresolved`), deduped per `(caller, command)` so a function calling `git` five times contributes one chain. Per ADR-0016's impl-note ruling A (command-mediated languages populate the subprocess boundary by emitting unresolved external-command edges, *not* via a data-I/O catalog), the producer **prestamps** `meta["io_boundary"] = "command_launch"` on the edge — the first and only producer-persisted `io_boundary`, an intentional exception to the consumer-time-derivation contract, because only the shell analyzer can tell a launch from a builtin and there is no catalog to match at consumer time. **Classification is separated from counting:** every launch *is* a subprocess crossing (classification, never gated by an I/O-relevance allowlist — that would re-open the hand-curation WI-tijos retired), but the `command_launch` chains are a **disclosed-only** cohort — surfaced in the new `command_launch_edges` envelope field and **excluded** from the `total_io_edges` headline, mirroring the `external_potential` count-vs-disclose doctrine (WI-huhit/WI-foduh, INV-pubom), so invocation-count multiplicity does not swamp the curated stdlib subprocess entries. `io_boundary.py` gains `_DISCLOSED_ONLY_BOUNDARIES` (single source of truth for headline hold-outs), `BoundaryMap.command_launch_edges`, and `command_launch` in `KNOWN_IO_BOUNDARIES`; `IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumps `2.0`→`2.1`. Behavioral evidence: `deploy() { curl …; git pull; rm -rf … }` emits `command_launch` chains for `curl`/`git`/`rm` (deduped), while `echo`/`cd`/`local`/`export`/`read`/`printf` and an in-tree `helper()` call emit none; end-to-end a `command_launch`-tagged edge aggregates into its own bucket, counts in `command_launch_edges`, and stays out of `total_io_edges`. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Elixir analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for `defstruct` fields (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Elixir tail-language slice).** Elixir has no field/variable DECLARATION nodes — a struct's shape is defined by the `defstruct` MACRO CALL — so the analyzer previously emitted module/function/macro symbols but nothing for struct fields, leaving a module's data shape without an anchor (invisible to search, centrality, io-boundaries). A new `defstruct` branch emits `kind="field"` per field, owner = the enclosing module (an Elixir struct IS its module, `%User{}`). Field names are extracted (`_extract_defstruct_field_names`) from the macro's single argument in either syntactic form — parenthesized or not (via `args.named_children[0]`, skipping the `(`/`)` tokens): a `list` of `atom`/`quoted_atom` (`[:name]`/`[:"weird"]`); a `keywords` or in-list keyword pair's KEY (`x: 0`/`"k": 0` → `x`/`k`, never the default value); or a tuple-literal pair (`[{:name, 0}]` — what `[name: 0]` desugars to) whose first atom is the field. Value-atoms and nested keyword/map defaults (`opts: [a: 1]`, `m: %{k: 1}`) are never mistaken for fields. A `@fields` module-attribute reference (dynamic) yields no static fields (fails-safe). Two fails-safe owner guards: a `defstruct` inside a `quote` block (an `__using__` macro template injected into a DIFFERENT module via `use`) emits nothing (`_has_quote_ancestor`); and one whose nearest enclosing module is atom-named (`defmodule :"x"`, which the owner walk cannot resolve) or absent is dropped rather than mis-attributed to a resolvable ancestor. Duplicate keys are de-duped. **Call-graph integrity: `ElixirAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`** — a qualified field `User.name` would otherwise let the `NameResolver` suffix index mint a wrong `calls` edge for a bare `name()` call, and there is no existing edge to preserve; fields reach output via `analysis.symbols` and are excluded from `symbol_by_name`/`global_symbols`/the `__multi__` multi-clause index, so a `value()` call resolves to a same-named function, never the field. Additive symbols only. **Adversarially reviewed across three rounds (GO_WITH_CHANGE → GO_WITH_CHANGE → GO):** round 1 caught a BLOCKING 100%-recall-loss on the parenthesized `defstruct([...])`/`defstruct(k: v)` form (the arguments node's first child is the `(` token, not the container — fixed with `named_children`) plus tuple-literal and quoted-key recall gaps; round 2 caught the `quote`/`__using__` phantom-owner defect. **Scope-out (documented follow-up):** module `@attributes` (`@timeout 5000`) as `kind="variable"` — separating data attributes from the open-ended set of reserved/framework meta attributes (`@moduledoc`/`@doc`/`@spec`/`@behaviour`/`@derive`/Ecto's `@primary_key`/…) needs a design decision and is deferred. **Filed follow-up (INV-sinah):** the analyzer-wide quote-attribution gap (`def`/`defmacro` inside a `quote` are mis-attributed to the defining module identically) is cross-cutting and tracked separately. Behavioral evidence (`analyze_elixir`): `defmodule App.User do defstruct [:name, :email, age: 0, role: :guest] end` → fields `App.User.name`/`App.User.email`/`App.User.age`/`App.User.role` (defaults `0`/`:guest` not emitted); a `defstruct` in an `__using__` `quote` emits no field on the defining module; a field `Store.value` never captures a `value()` call from a same-named function. WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail's tractable languages — fortran, julia, elixir — are now covered; Haskell remains unfit; `@attributes` a documented follow-up); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Julia analyzer now emits `kind="variable"` symbols for non-const module-level assignments (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Julia module-variable tail slice, extending the earlier struct-field slice).** Julia already emitted struct fields (`kind="field"`) and module `const` (`kind="const"`), but a non-const module binding (`x = 5`, `handler = () -> …`, `config = Dict()`) had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new `assignment` branch classifies each binding by its EFFECTIVE scope via a walk (`_julia_variable_scope`) that ascends through scope-TRANSPARENT wrappers — a module-level `begin…end`, `if…elseif…else`, a `global x = …` statement, and a chained `x = y = 5` link — to the enclosing `module_definition`/`source_file`, and STOPS (excludes) at the first scope-introducing ancestor (`function_definition` incl. short-form `f(x) = begin … end`, `let`/`for`/`while`/`try`/`do`/`local`). A `const` assignment's parent is `const_statement`, so it is not double-emitted. Target name(s) are read from the LHS: `identifier`; the PRE-`::` target of a `typed_expression` (`y::Int` → `y`, `(a,b)::Point` → `a`,`b` — never the type); every element of an `open_tuple`/`tuple`/`parenthesized` incl. a `splat_expression` (`rest...` → `rest`); a `call_expression` LHS (possibly wrapped in a `typed_expression` for a return type, `f(x)::Int = …`) is a short-form FUNCTION, not a variable; a `field_expression`/`index_expression` is a MUTATION, not a declaration. A rebound name is de-duped once per (scope, name). **Call-graph integrity: `JuliaAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips BOTH `field` and `variable`.** The load-bearing lesson from the review: unlike the F# slice — where value bindings were ALREADY resolvable, so excluding them would drop existing edges (a regression) — Julia variables were UNEMITTED before this slice, so excluding them from resolution adds no edge and drops none (no regression); meanwhile a module variable's name is bare/unqualified, so registering it would let it EXACT-match a bare `foo()` call and beat a same-named function's module-QUALIFIED suffix-match, minting a wrong cross-module `calls` edge. So the per-language skip-set turns on **"regression vs new feature," not "functional vs imperative"** — for Julia the safe, no-regression choice is `{field, variable}`. Variables still reach output/search/centrality via `analysis.symbols`. Additive symbols only. **Adversarially reviewed across five rounds (NO_GO → NO_GO → GO_WITH_CHANGE → GO_WITH_CHANGE → GO), each catching a real defect hidden by 100% line coverage:** a `typed_expression` LHS emitting the TYPE as a phantom variable that clobbered the struct (`(a,b)::Point` → phantom `variable:Point`; `f(x)::Int = x` dropping the function); a scope gate that over-excluded valid module variables behind `begin`/`if`/`global`/chained wrappers; a short-form-function body-local leak introduced by making `assignment` transparent (fixed by stopping the walk at a `call_expression`-LHS assignment); a dropped splat binding; and finally the cross-module clobber that drove the corrected `{field, variable}` skip. Documented fails-safe residuals (a miss, never a wrong symbol): a `global x = …` INSIDE a function, a macro-wrapped `@show x = 5`, and a `g = (q = 5)` parenthesized inner binding. Behavioral evidence (`analyze_julia`): a `Config` module with `timeout = 30`, `retry::Int = 3`, `host, port = …`, `handler = () -> 200`, and `const MAX = 100` → variables `timeout`/`retry`/`host`/`port`/`handler` (const `MAX` and struct field `Server.name` unchanged), with a module-level `begin`/`if`/`global`/`x = y = 5` all emitting, a function-body local and a short-form-function body-local excluded, and no `calls` edge ever terminating on a variable (a cross-module `handler()` resolves to the function, not the variable). WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail — elixir — continues; Haskell remains unfit); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Fortran analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for derived-type components and `kind="variable"` symbols for module/program/submodule/block-data-level declarations (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Fortran tail-language slice).** Fortran previously emitted module/program/function/subroutine/type symbols but zero field/variable symbols, so derived-type structure, module state, and module constants had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new `variable_declaration` branch classifies each declaration by its **effective scope** (`_effective_scope` walks past transparent `preproc_*` conditionals, so a `#ifdef`-guarded declaration in a `.F90` file is still classified correctly): a `derived_type_definition` scope → **field** (owner = the type, e.g. `point.x`); a `module`/`program`/`submodule`/`block_data` scope → **variable**; a `subroutine`/`function`/`block_construct`/interface-body scope → a local/parameter-type declaration → skipped. Declarator names are harvested by `_declarator_name` across all five tree-sitter shapes (bare `identifier`; `sized_declarator` for inline arrays `grid(nx,ny)`/`w(:)`; `coarray_declarator` for `field(10)[*]`; `pointer_init_declarator` for `q(:,:) => tgt` — binding the *name*, never the association target; `init_declarator` for `arr(3) = […]`) via leftmost-child recursion, so array dimensions, coarray codimensions, pointer targets, and initializer values are never mistaken for the name. Parameterized-derived-type KIND/LEN parameters (`integer, kind :: k`) are excluded (a type slot, not a data component). **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`FortranAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable` — Fortran is imperative, so **both** are skipped): a field `box.label` would let the `NameResolver` suffix index mint a wrong `calls` edge for a bare `call label()`, and a module `variable` `compute` would EXACT-match a bare `call compute()` and clobber a same-named subroutine — neither is ever a call target; the symbols still reach output/search/centrality because the base orchestrator builds the output set from `analysis.symbols` independently of the resolution registry. Additive symbols only (no new edges — taint- and coherence-neutral). **Adversarially reviewed across seven rounds, each of which caught a real defect hidden by 100% line coverage** — the strongest demonstration in this campaign of "line coverage is not correctness; the diverse-lens review is the gate." The first four rounds fixed a recall loss on the dominant Fortran data idiom (inline-dimensioned arrays, pointer-init, initialized arrays were silently dropped), phantom PDT KIND/LEN fields (`matrix.k`/`matrix.n` emitted while the real component was dropped), coarrays, preprocessor-guarded declarations, and a wrong-owner nested-type phantom. Round 5 exposed the load-bearing lesson: the bundled `tree_sitter_fortran` grammar sets `has_error` on **valid** F2003 it cannot parse (`asynchronous`, ubiquitous in MPI-3), so any `has_error`-based deferral silently drops valid declarations — forcing a redesign onto an explicit **firm contract** (NEVER drop a valid symbol on compilable code; NEVER emit a wrong edge; best-effort suppress phantoms on malformed input using only signals that cannot fire on compilable code). Rounds 6–7 removed the last over-eager guards (a prev-sibling and a next-sibling ERROR guard) that each dropped a valid declaration — a standalone `asynchronous ::`/`codimension ::` statement's follower, an `asynchronous`-as-last-statement whole-unit drop (fixed by an ERROR-wrapped-unit scope recovery), and a fixed-form (`.f`) column-6 continuation's clean leading name — converging on minimal, provably relaxation-biased logic (harvest names, stopping at the first in-vardecl ERROR child; a grammar-parseable declaration has no ERROR child, so no valid symbol on compilable code is ever dropped). **Documented accepted residuals** (spurious EXTRA, never a wrong edge; validated to have no safe suppression signal): DEC/VAX `STRUCTURE`/`UNION`/`MAP` (non-standard legacy the grammar cannot parse) may leak a phantom component variable; a name the grammar buries inside an ERROR node (a fixed-form *continued* name, the inline-`asynchronous` name) is an irreducible grammar recall limit. Behavioral evidence (`analyze_fortran_files`): a `geometry` module with `integer :: module_counter`, `real, parameter :: PI`, `real :: grid(nx,ny)`, a `type :: point` with `real :: x`/`real :: y`, and subroutines → fields `point.x`/`point.y` + variables `module_counter`/`pi`/`grid`, with a `call reset()` resolving to the subroutine (no anchor steals it); a valid module var after an `asynchronous` interface, a standalone `asynchronous` statement's follower, and a fixed-form `INTEGER COUNT,` continuation all preserve their valid names. WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail — elixir, julia non-const module variables — continues; Haskell remains unfit); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Zig analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for struct/union members and enum variants, and `kind="variable"` symbols for module-level `const`/`var` (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Zig tail-language slice).** Zig previously emitted functions/methods/structs/enums/unions but zero field/variable symbols, so struct members, enum variants, and module-level constants/state had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new branch handles `container_field` → **field** (the shared node for struct fields, tagged-union fields, and enum variants; owner = the enclosing type name threaded down when its declaration pushed the aggregate body onto the traversal stack, so a nested `Inner.x` and an `Outer.y` are attributed correctly; the declared type is the signature); and a `variable_declaration` that is **not** a type declaration (`const X = struct/enum/union/error_set`) → **variable**, gated on `node.parent.type == "source_file"` so function-body locals (parented by a `block`) and struct-associated `const`s (parented by an aggregate body) are structurally excluded — no scope walk needed. An `@import(...)` module-alias `const` is represented as an import edge, not a variable. **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`ZigAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable`): a data anchor is never a call/instantiate target, so a bare-named module `var` can neither clobber a same-named function's registry slot (a false-negative an edge-site kind-gate cannot recover) nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target; the symbols still reach output/search/centrality because the base orchestrator builds the output set from `analysis.symbols` independently of the resolution registry. Additive symbols only (no new edges — taint- and coherence-neutral). Enum variants emit as fields, consistent with the dart enum-body-value precedent (they are type members accessed via `.`). Struct-associated `const`s (an associated constant inside an aggregate body) are a deferred fail-safe (miss, never mis-emit). Behavioral evidence (`analyze_zig`): `const Point = struct { x: f32, y: f32 }` → fields `Point.x`/`Point.y`; `const Color = enum { red, green }` → fields `Color.red`/`Color.green`; `const MAX_SIZE = 100; var counter = 0;` → variables; a function-local `const`/`var`, an `@import` alias, a `const Point = struct{}` type decl, and a struct-body `const origin` all emit no variable; a module `var value` coexisting with `fn value()` leaves the `value()` call resolving to the function (no anchor steals it). WI-jusus stays open (the non-gated analyzer tail continues); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Nim analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for object/enum members and `kind="variable"` symbols for module-level `const`/`var`/`let` (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Nim tail-language slice).** Nim previously emitted proc/func/method/type symbols but zero field/variable symbols, so object fields, enum variants, and module-level constants/state had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. New branches handle `field_declaration` → **field** (owner = the enclosing `type_declaration`'s name, so `Person.name`; a multi-name `age, weight: int` lists one `symbol_declaration` per name and emits both), `enum_field_declaration` → **field** (enum variants are type members accessed via `.` — `Color.red` — consistent with the dart/zig enum-body-value precedent), and a module-level `variable_declaration` → **variable**. Unlike the swift INV-lanaz / go INV-sidab local-leak class, object/enum fields use **distinct grammar nodes** no local construct ever produces, so the field half is structurally leak-proof (no scope walk); the module-variable half is gated on its section (`const_section`/`var_section`/`let_section`) sitting **directly under `source_file`** — a proc-body `var`/`let` reuses the same `variable_declaration` node but its section lives under a `statement_list`, so locals are structurally excluded. The `*` export marker (`name*`/`Pi*`, which wraps the identifier in an `exported_symbol` node) is resolved by the existing `_declared_name_node` helper (the INV-bisom fix, reused), so exported fields/variables carry `is_exported=True`. **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`NimAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable`, and they are never added to `symbol_by_name`): a data anchor is never a call/instantiate target, so a bare-named module `variable` can neither clobber a same-named `proc`'s flat registry key (a false-negative an edge-site kind-gate cannot recover) nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target; the symbols still reach output/search/centrality because the base orchestrator builds the output set from `analysis.symbols` independently of the resolution registry. Additive symbols only (no new edges — taint- and coherence-neutral). **Adversarially reviewed (4 refuters, NO-GO → fixed → GO):** all four converged on a confidently-wrong emission hidden by 100% line coverage — an inline anonymous tuple used as a field *type* (`coord: tuple[x, y: int]`) is modelled by the bundled grammar with the tuple's *own* nested `field_declaration` nodes, so the tuple's members leaked onto the enclosing object as phantom, wrong-owner fields (`Outer.x`/`Outer.y`, which don't exist — you access `o.coord.x`), and on a member-name collision (`meta: tuple[name: …]` on an object with a real `name` field) two distinct `Owner.name` symbols. Fixed structurally: `_enclosing_type_name` aborts the owner walk (a fails-safe skip) if it crosses an **intervening `field_declaration`** before the enclosing `type_declaration` — a discriminator verified against the grammar to drop exactly the nested-tuple/object phantoms while preserving real object fields, object-variant (`case`) branch fields, and a **standalone** named tuple's own members (`type Coord = tuple[x, y: int]` → `Coord.x`/`Coord.y`, which the rejected "intervening `tuple_type`" alternative would have wrongly dropped). Other fails-safe behaviors: a nested anonymous *object* type mis-parses to an `ERROR`/`call` subtree (inner field skipped, outer emits); documented recall misses (skip, never mis-emit) are the object-variant *discriminator* field (`case kind: Kind`, a `variant_discriminator_declaration`) and a `when defined(...): var x` module variable (its section is not directly under `source_file`). Behavioral evidence (`analyze_nim`): `type Person = object { name: string; age: int }` → fields `Person.name`/`Person.age`; `type Color = enum { red; green = 2; blue }` → fields `Color.red`/`Color.green`/`Color.blue`; `const Pi = 3.14; var counter = 0; let MAX = 100` → variables; `type Outer = object { coord: tuple[x, y: int] }` → field `Outer.coord` only (no `Outer.x`/`Outer.y`); a proc-body `var`/`let`, a `type` declaration, a proc-return tuple's members, and the nested-object inner field all emit no variable/phantom; a module `var value` coexisting with `proc value()` leaves the `value()` call resolving to the proc (no anchor steals it). WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail — d_lang/fortran/elixir/fsharp/… — continues); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The D analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for struct/class/interface members and enum members, and `kind="variable"` symbols for module-level globals (WI-jusus emission-parity; the D tail-language slice).** D previously emitted module/function/method/struct/class/interface symbols but zero field/variable symbols, so aggregate members, enum variants, and module globals had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. The load-bearing piece is scope discrimination: a struct/class field, a module global, and a function-body local are all the SAME tree-sitter `variable_declaration` node (an `auto x = …` is a distinct `auto_declaration`), so the classifier keys on the IMMEDIATE parent — `aggregate_body` → **field** (owner = the enclosing struct/class/interface via the existing `_find_parent_container`, which walks to the *nearest* type, so a nested struct's member is `Inner.x` not `Outer.x`); module scope (`module_def` when a `module X;` declaration is present, else `source_file` directly) → **variable**; anything else (`block_statement`/`function_body`) → skipped local. Enum members (`enum_member`) emit as **field** owned by the enclosing `enum_declaration`'s name (`Color.red` — the dart/zig/nim enum-body-value precedent); an **anonymous** enum parses to a distinct `anonymous_enum_declaration` with no owner name, so its members are skipped (a fails-safe recall miss, never a wrong-owner phantom). Multi-name declarations (`int a, b;`) emit one symbol per `declarator`. **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`DAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable` before its module-qualified + bare-name registrations, and they are never added to `symbol_by_name`): a data anchor is never a call/instantiate target, so a bare-named module `variable` can neither clobber a same-named `function`'s registry key nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target; the symbols still reach output/search/centrality because the output set is built from `analysis.symbols` independently of the resolution registry. Additive symbols only (no new edges — taint- and coherence-neutral). **Adversarially reviewed (4 refuters, NO-GO → fixed → GO):** all four converged on a wrong-owner phantom hidden by 100% line coverage (the D analogue of the Nim inline-tuple leak) — a **named** union nested in a struct/class (`struct Outer { union U { int a; } }`) declares a nested *type*, so its members belong to `U` (`U.a`), but `_find_parent_container` walked past the union (absent from `_CONTAINER_NODE_TYPES`) and mis-attributed them to `Outer` (phantom `Outer.a`). Fixed by adding `union_declaration` to `_CONTAINER_NODE_TYPES`: a named union now owns its members (`U.a`), while an **anonymous** union — which has no `identifier` child, so the owner walk's `if name_node` guard skips it — correctly keeps hoisting its members into the enclosing struct (`Anon.x`), and a top-level named union's members are now recalled (`TopU.p`); the same fix also correctly qualifies methods declared inside a named union. Behavioral evidence (`analyze_d`): `struct Point { float x; float y; }` → fields `Point.x`/`Point.y`; `struct Outer { struct Inner { int x; } int y; }` → `Inner.x`/`Outer.y` (not `Outer.x`); `struct Outer { union U { int a; float b; } int c; }` → `U.a`/`U.b`/`Outer.c` (no `Outer.a`); a `struct { union { int x; } }` anonymous union → the enclosing struct's `.x`; `enum Color { red, green = 2, blue }` → `Color.red`/`Color.green`/`Color.blue`; `int globalVar = 5; auto answer = 42;` → variables; a function-body `int localInt`/`auto autoLocal` and an anonymous `enum { A, B }`'s members emit no symbol; a module `int value` coexisting with `int value_fn()` leaves `value_fn()` resolving to the function (no anchor steals it). WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail — fortran/elixir/fsharp/… — continues); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The F# analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for record members and reclassifies module `let` value bindings to `kind="variable"` (WI-jusus emission-parity; the F# tail-language slice), fixing a local-leak bug on the way.** F# previously emitted record *types* but none of their fields, and emitted module `let` bindings as an F#-only `kind="value"` — which also **leaked function-body locals** (the emission had no scope guard). This slice: (1) emits each `record_field` as **field** owned by the record type (`Rec.name`; a `mutable` modifier is skipped); (2) reclassifies module-level `let` values from `value` → **variable**, the cross-language convention every sibling analyzer uses (`value` was an F#-only registered kind with no cross-package consumer and no test asserting it); (3) scope-guards the value emission via `_fsharp_value_is_module_level` so only module-scoped bindings emit — a value's `function_or_value_defn` must sit under `declaration_expression > named_module`/`module_defn`/**`file`** (the last covers headerless `.fsx` scripts / implicit-module `.fs` files, which the grammar wraps directly in `file`), while a function-body local — whose grandparent is the enclosing `function_or_value_defn` — is excluded even in a headerless file. **Call-graph integrity is handled with an F#-specific nuance surfaced by adversarial review** (`FSharpAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips **only `field`**, not `variable`): a record field must stay out of the resolution registry because the `NameResolver` suffix index would let a qualified `Rec.name` suffix-match a bare call `name(…)` and mint a wrong `calls` edge — a load-bearing precision guard — but a `variable` is an F# `let` binding that is *legitimately callable* (point-free `let inc = (+) 1`, lambda `let add = fun a b -> …`, composition, factory results), and F# forbids a same-name value+function within a module (so there is no clobber to prevent), so variables stay resolvable. **Adversarially reviewed (4 refuters, GO-with-change): the review caught two regressions vs. the first cut** — (a) a phantom `anonymous_module` in the scope allowlist (never produced by the bundled grammar) that omitted the real `file` root, silently dropping every top-level value in headerless `.fsx` scripts; and (b) excluding `variable` from resolution (the initial cut) dropped `calls` edges to callable value bindings that resolved before — both fixed and locked with regression tests (headerless top-level value emits + resolves; headerless local excluded; `caller → add`/`inc` value-binding call edges restored). Additive fields + one reclassification (no schema break; `value` becomes a registered-but-unemitted kind). Behavioral evidence (`analyze_fsharp`): `type Rec = { name: string; mutable age: int }` → fields `Rec.name`/`Rec.age`; `module M` + `let topLevelVal = 30` → variable (no `value`-kind symbol); a headerless `script.fsx` with `let apiKey = "secret"` → variable `apiKey`; a function-body `let localBinding` and a nested-module `let nestedVal` emit correctly (local excluded, nested-module value emitted); `let add = fun a b -> a + b` with `let caller x = add x 1` resolves the `caller → add` call edge. WI-jusus stays open (the analyzer tail — fortran/elixir/… — continues); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Scala analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for class/object/trait/enum/given-body `val`/`var` and `kind="variable"` symbols for top-level `val`/`var` (WI-jusus emission-parity F5; the Scala tail-language slice).** Scala previously emitted zero field/variable symbols (`_extract_symbols_from_file` had no `val`/`var` branch), so class fields, module state, and top-level constants had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new branch handles `val_definition`/`var_definition`/`val_declaration`/`var_declaration` (the last two are abstract members like a trait's `val label: String`). The load-bearing piece is scope discrimination (`_scala_property_scope`), which walks to the nearest scope-defining ancestor: a **named** class/object/trait `template_body`, `enum_body`, or `given … with` `with_template_body` → `field` (owner = that type's name); `compilation_unit` → `variable`; and any **local** scope → **skipped**. The local set is exhaustive against the swift INV-lanaz / go INV-sidab function-local-leak regression class: `block`/`function`/`lambda`, plus `case_clause` (a `case _ => val w = …` inside a partial-function literal, `match`, or `try`/`catch` that *initializes* a field or top-level val — it would otherwise climb to the body and leak) and Scala-3 `indented_block` (a braceless nested initializer). An anonymous `new Foo { val inner = … }` (or anonymous `given`) member has a body with no named owner, so it is skipped rather than mis-attributed; a tuple-pattern `val (a, b) = …` and a multi-name `val a, b = …` (no direct identifier child) are skipped. To protect call-graph integrity the new field/variable symbols are **not** registered under their bare short name in the resolution index (mirroring commit `67b2e14788`), and the edge pass's resolver call-resolution now refuses a `field`/`variable` target — so a bare `compute()` never mis-resolves to a same-named `compute` field. Fields carry `make_typed_stable_id` (type annotation as signature, `private`/`protected` → not `is_exported`, annotations → `meta['decorators']`); top-level variables carry `make_variable_stable_id` (same-name vals in different files hash distinctly). Additive symbols only (no new edges — taint- and coherence-neutral). Documented fails-safe deferrals (miss the symbol, never emit a wrong one): primary-constructor `val`/`var` params, multi-name bindings, and package-object members. Behavioral evidence: `object Config { val timeout = 30; private val secret = "x" }` → fields `Config.timeout`/`Config.secret` (secret not exported); `enum Color: … val rgb = 0` → field `Color.rgb`; `val topLevelVal = 100` → variable; method-/case-/indented-local vals, an anonymous-instance member, and a tuple-pattern val emit nothing. WI-jusus stays open (the non-gated analyzer tail — dart/haskell/ocaml/… — continues); tagged for bakeoff validation (blast-radius emission change across the Scala corpus).
- **The Dart analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for class/mixin/extension/enum-body value declarations and `kind="variable"` symbols for top-level declarations (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Dart tail-language slice).** Dart previously emitted zero field/variable symbols, so class fields, module constants, and module state had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new branch handles the two tree-sitter value-list nodes — `initialized_identifier_list` (var/typed) and `static_final_declaration_list` (const/static/final-inferred) — classifying by immediate structural context: wrapped in a `declaration` under a `class_body` (class **or** mixin), `extension_body`, or `enum_body` → **field** (owner = the enclosing type); a direct child of `program` → **variable**. Unlike the swift INV-lanaz / go INV-sidab local-leak class, Dart function-/method-/for-/lambda-/if-block **locals use a distinct node type** (`local_variable_declaration` → `initialized_variable_definition`) and can never match, so the leak is structurally impossible (verified by AST probe across method/for/lambda/if/while/try/switch bodies). Multi-name declarations (`int x = 1, y = 2;`) emit one symbol per name; a bare `type_identifier` immediately preceding the list is the signature (generics/nullable/inferred → no signature, never a wrong type). **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`DartAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable`): a data anchor is never a call/instantiate target, so a bare-named top-level `variable` can neither clobber a same-named `function`'s flat registry key (a false-negative kind-gates cannot recover) nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target, and instantiation edges are untouched — the symbols still reach output/search/centrality since the output set is built independently of the resolution registry. Fields carry `make_typed_stable_id`; top-level variables carry `make_variable_stable_id`; underscore-prefixed names → `private`/not `is_exported`. Additive symbols only (no new edges). Adversarially reviewed (2 refuters, both NO-GO-as-written → GO): the review caught the registry-clobber vector (→ the chokepoint fix) and a Dart-3 record/list/map **pattern-declaration** mis-parse (`var (a, b) = pair;` salvages the RHS as a spurious list under this grammar) — guarded by skipping any value list with an `ERROR` preceding sibling (fails-safe; also drops the rare `abstract`/`external` field the grammar mis-parses, a documented deferral). Behavioral evidence (live `hypergumbo` analyze): a file with `const int maxDelay`, `var currentItems`, `class Counter { int count; final String _label; void tick(){ var local; } }` → fields `Counter.count`/`Counter._label` + variables `maxDelay`/`currentItems`, with the method-local `local` excluded. WI-jusus stays open (haskell/ocaml/… tail continues); tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Solidity analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for contract state variables and struct members, and `kind="variable"` symbols for file-level constants (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Solidity tail-language slice).** Solidity previously emitted zero field/variable symbols, so contract **state variables** — the security-critical persistent storage that functions read/write and that taint/io-boundary analysis cares about — had no anchor, invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new branch handles three tree-sitter nodes: `state_variable_declaration` under a `contract_body` → **field** (owner = enclosing contract/interface/library, visibility from the `visibility` child, `public` → `is_exported`); `struct_member` → **field** (owner = the enclosing struct); and `constant_variable_declaration` directly under `source_file` → **variable** (contract-level constants parse as `state_variable_declaration`, handled as fields). Function-body locals use a distinct grammar node (`variable_declaration` inside `variable_declaration_statement`) and can never match, so the swift INV-lanaz / go INV-sidab local-leak class is structurally impossible (adversarially confirmed to hold even under parse-error recovery). Name extraction reads the declaration's direct `identifier` child (never the `type_name`, verified for array/mapping/user-defined-type declarations); the `type_name` text is the signature. **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`SolidityAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`/`variable`, and `add_value_symbol` writes only to `analysis.symbols`, never `symbol_by_name`): a state variable is never a call target — a `public` state variable's auto-generated getter is a synthesized function, not this symbol — so a bare-named value can neither clobber a same-named function's registry key nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target. Fields carry `make_typed_stable_id`; file-level constants carry `make_variable_stable_id`. Additive symbols only (no new edges). Adversarially reviewed (GO): an end-to-end proof confirmed a coexisting field `Store.value` + function `Store.value` resolve the call to the function with zero spurious edges. Behavioral evidence (live analyze): a `Vault` contract with `address public owner`, `mapping(...) private balances`, `uint256 public totalDeposits`, a `struct Deposit { uint256 amount; uint64 timestamp; }`, and a file-level `uint256 constant VERSION` → 5 fields (`Vault.owner`/`Vault.balances`/`Vault.totalDeposits` + `Deposit.amount`/`Deposit.timestamp`) + 1 variable (`VERSION`), with `owner` exported / `balances` not, and a function-body local `amt` excluded. WI-jusus stays open; tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Julia analyzer now emits `kind="field"` symbols for struct members (WI-jusus emission-parity; the Julia tail-language slice).** Julia previously emitted the struct *type* symbol but none of its member fields, so a struct's data shape had no anchor — invisible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. A new branch, running inside the `struct_definition` handler, emits a field per struct member: a `typed_expression` (`x::Float64`) → field with the (possibly parametric, e.g. `Vector{Int}`) type as signature, and a bare `identifier` (untyped field) → field with no signature; owner = the struct name, underscore-prefixed → `private`/not `is_exported`. Inner constructors (`Widget(s) = new(s)`) and function-body locals are not fields (distinct grammar nodes) and never leak. **A tree-sitter grammar divergence surfaced during TDD** and is handled: the bundled `tree_sitter_julia` grammar lists members as *direct children* of `struct_definition`, while the language-pack grammar wraps them in a `block` — the extractor handles both. Julia-1.8 `const x::T` struct fields parse as an `ERROR` node under the bundled grammar (which predates the feature) and are deferred fails-safe (miss, never emit a wrong symbol); the sibling non-const fields still emit. Julia module-level `const` is already emitted (`kind="const"`); non-const module globals (which need scope discrimination) are a deferred follow-up. **Call-graph integrity is closed at one chokepoint** (`JuliaAnalyzer.register_symbol` skips `field`, and the field emitter writes only to `analysis.symbols`, never `symbol_by_name`): a struct field is never a call target, so a bare-named field can neither clobber a same-named function's registry key nor be a suffix-matched spurious `calls` target. Fields carry `make_typed_stable_id`. Additive symbols only (no new edges). Behavioral evidence (live analyze): `struct Circle <: Shape` with `radius::Float64`, `center::Vector{Float64}`, bare `label`, and an inner constructor → fields `Circle.radius`/`Circle.center`/`Circle.label` (constructor excluded, parametric type captured), and `mutable struct Counter` with `count::Int`/`_internal::Bool` → `Counter.count` (exported) + `Counter._internal` (not), with a function-body `local` excluded. WI-jusus stays open; tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The subprocess→CLI linker now joins `subprocess.run(['tool', 'method'])` calls to the public methods of a class exposed via `fire.Fire(<Class>)` (WI-sigam; the python-fire tail of WI-lubap).** WI-lubap made the linker argparse-aware (`add_parser`/`set_defaults` → handler); python-fire was deferred because `fire.Fire(obj)` exposes `obj`'s public **methods** as subcommands *reflectively* — there is no `add_parser`/`set_defaults` idiom to scan, and no per-subcommand Symbol. A new `_scan_fire_commands` (mirroring `_scan_argparse_commands`) detects `fire.Fire(<Name>)` calls, and for every `<Name>` that resolves to a real `kind="class"` Symbol, registers each `<Name>.<method>` method whose name is **public** (not underscore-prefixed, exactly how python-fire hides members) as the subcommand `<method>` in the shared `command_by_name` index — so it inherits the INV-zuhub multi-candidate collision fallback for free, and coexists with argparse and `concept="command"` symbols. The fire map is counted by the linker's requirement diagnostic so a fire-only project is not reported as zero command sources. INV-fahub-guarded: the class-existence check means a `fire.Fire(local_var)` sharing a name with an unrelated class does not join, and the join keys on the fired class only. Behavioral evidence (end-to-end): a `fire.Fire(Calc)` whose `Calc` has `add`/`multiply`/`_private` methods, with `subprocess.run(['tool', 'add'])` / `subprocess.run(['tool', 'multiply'])`, emits `subprocess_calls` edges to `Calc.add` and `Calc.multiply` (was 0 edges); `Calc._private` is never exposed. Additive only (new `subprocess_calls` edges to existing method symbols — taint- and coherence-neutral). Deferred broader fire forms (a module / instance / dict target, and the `from fire import Fire; Fire(Calc)` bare-import idiom) are follow-ups.
- **Python nested-function call/reference resolution now walks the full enclosing (LEGB) scope chain — the py.py scope-stack rewrite (identity:F1/F4a).** The single-level `inner_scope` dict (only the caller's *immediate* nested helpers) is replaced by a materialized `ScopeStack` (new `_pyscope.py`) built per caller from a precomputed `enclosing_func_id` ancestry map (reusing the existing `parent_map` walk). A bare call or reference to a helper defined in a **grandparent-or-higher enclosing function** — or, for a method, its enclosing function (the class body is transparent per Python's LEGB rule) — now resolves to that helper, where before it produced no edge. Additive and behavior-preserving: the new enclosing lookup is strictly last-resort (fires only after `local_symbols`/imports miss, so no existing edge is re-targeted), returns only nested *functions*, and every new edge is `is_resolved=True` to a real in-repo symbol; the unresolved-emit path (`is_resolved`/`dst_ref`/`:unresolved`) is untouched (taint-safe). This is the shared substrate the dispatch:F3 `f=g` alias (WI-gulot), method-receiver short-name (WI-noham), and vocab/io follow-ups rebase onto as additive patches rather than re-migrating the flat-dict resolver. Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **Python route materializer symbol ids use the `function` kind-slot, not `route` — ADR-0036 Ruling 2 tail (WI-zugob, python producer).** The django/starlette/flask route materializers in `py.py` minted `{lang}:{path}:{span}:{name}:route` while `Symbol.kind="function"` and `meta['framework_role']='route'` was already stamped — a fossil id-slot left over from the ADR-0027 route→function fold. All three now build the id with the node's own kind (`:function`). The `_is_route_symbol` consumer already identifies these via `framework_role` / `route_path`+`http_method` meta (its id-slot check is a third fallback that still covers the not-yet-migrated `:route` fleet — WI-zugob's ruby/elixir/swift/php/grpc analyzers, which emit `kind="route"` directly). No identity churn (`stable_id` from `make_route_stable_id`). Clears the last 8 residual `id_format|warning` nodes on self-analysis (the Starlette routes), leaving only the pre-existing name-slot `id_format|error` (a Maven-dependency colon — the Ruling-1 follow-up).
- **Event-sourcing publisher/subscriber symbol ids drop the role from their kind-slot — ADR-0036 Ruling 2 tail (WI-kugaj, event half).** The event-sourcing linker minted `{lang}:{path}:{line}-{line}:{event}:event_publisher` / `:event_subscriber` while `Symbol.kind="function"` and the role already lived on `meta['framework_role']` — the id-slot was smuggling the role as a disambiguator. The id now uses the node's own kind (`:function`); the role stays on `meta['framework_role']`. Cross-run identity is unaffected (`stable_id` derives from `make_protocol_stable_id`, not the id-slot), and ids stay unique — `(path, line, event_name)` is unique per pattern on all measured corpora (a line publishes XOR subscribes a given event; a per-file id-uniqueness validator backstops the rare pub+sub-same-line case). Clears 10 of the residual `id_format|warning` nodes on self-analysis; the `:route` kind-slot fleet (WI-zugob — 10 producers across route linkers + py/js/go/swift analyzers) is the remaining route/event cohort.
- **External boundary node ids now carry `external_symbol` in their kind slot instead of the use-site reference syntax — ADR-0036 Ruling 2 kind-slot purity (WI-pubiv).** A boundary Symbol's `Symbol.kind` is `external_symbol`, but its id kind-slot used to keep the *reference syntax* the dangling edge was minted with (`go:fmt:0-0:Errorf:unresolved`, `python:mod:0-0:attr:attribute`, …) — so one node answered "what kind are you?" two different ways, the impurity ADR-0036 Ruling 2 forbids (the id kind-slot is a denormalized copy of `Symbol.kind`, nothing else). This was the dominant `validation_report id_format|warning` cohort (1,399 external nodes / ~2,550 advisory violations on self-analysis — the `unresolved` majority plus the `attribute`/`module`/`namespace`/`package`/`function`/`file`/`script` tails). The single `create_boundary_nodes` chokepoint (`_canonical_external_id`) now builds the id with a uniform `external_symbol` kind-slot and moves the reference syntax to a new registered `Symbol.meta['reference_syntax']` key (`AXIS_SYMBOL_META`), so no information is lost. Boundary identity (and `stable_id`) is now `(language, path, name)` — the kind slot no longer participates — and the dedupe key drops kind accordingly; two references to the same external via different syntaxes collapse to one node (measured lossless: no external is reached via more than one syntax on any corpus, guarded by a boundary-id-uniqueness property test). The existing `id_remap` rewrites inbound edges onto the new canonical id, so the final graph's node ids and edge dsts stay consistent. **Node-id string values change for these ~1,399 external nodes; no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump** (the schema *structure* is unchanged — `reference_syntax` is an open-dict meta key, and node ids are run-local location identity, not a stability contract; `stable_id` is the cross-run identity). The `:unresolved` suffix retained on pre-remap *edge dsts* (the cgo/taint/taint_refine internal convention) is ADR-0037's separate concern and unchanged here (WI-jopil follow-up); the route/event, `npm_package`, and `tsconfig` kind-slot cohorts come from other producers and are separate fast-follows.
- **`npm_package` and `tsconfig` symbol ids drop their fold-source kind-slot to match the node's real kind — ADR-0036 Ruling 2 tail (WI-pubiv fast-follow).** Two producers still stamped a *fold-source* kind in the id kind-slot even though `Symbol.kind` had already been folded: the js-module linker minted `{lang}:npm:0-0:{name}:npm_package` (kind already `package`, ecosystem already on `meta.package_ecosystem='npm'`), and the JSON analyzer minted `json:{path}:{span}:{path}:tsconfig` (kind already `file`, format already on `meta.config_format='tsconfig'`). Both now build the id with the node's own kind (`:package` / `:file`); no identity churn (the npm `stable_id` derives from `make_dependency_stable_id`, not the id) and no schema change. Clears 5 of the 46 residual `id_format|warning` nodes on self-analysis; the route/event kind-slot cohort and the WI-jopil edge-dst fold remain.
- **The subprocess→CLI linker now joins argparse subcommands to their handlers, not just decorator-based (Click/Typer) commands (WI-lubap).** The linker matched a subprocess subcommand (`subprocess.run(["myapp", "serve"])`) to a CLI command *by symbol name* — which works for Click (`@click.command def serve` → a symbol named `serve` with `concept="command"`) but silently produced **zero edges** for argparse/`argparse`-style CLIs, because argparse exposes its command surface as `<p> = sub.add_parser("serve")` + `<p>.set_defaults(func=handler)` — the subcommand name is a *string literal* and the parser is not a symbol, so there was no `concept="command"` symbol to join (and the `cli.yaml` argparse pattern was keyed under `decorator:`, which never fires on a constructor call). hypergumbo's own CLI is argparse-based, so its ~20 subcommands were dark to the linker. The linker now additionally scans each Python file's AST for the argparse idiom (`_scan_argparse_commands`), pairs `add_parser`/`set_defaults` by parser-variable, resolves the `func=` handler to its module symbol, and merges `{subcommand: handler}` into its command index — so `subprocess.run(["myapp","serve"])` joins to the `serve` handler function. The linker's requirement diagnostic counts both command sources (concept=command symbols + argparse handlers), so an argparse-only project is no longer falsely reported as having zero (the machine-checkable prerequisite the item asked for). Fire's reflective command surface (`fire.Fire(obj)`) is a separate, harder case (deferred). Behavioral evidence: a `subprocess.run(["myapp","serve"])` against an argparse `add_parser("serve")`+`set_defaults(func=cmd_serve)` now emits a `subprocess_calls` edge to `cmd_serve` (was: no edge).
- **`Symbol.visibility` — one computed canonical visibility level, replacing the asymmetric per-language `modifiers` polarity (INV-jusot, additive first half; SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.3 → 0.14.4).** "Is this symbol publicly visible?" was encoded incompatibly: a per-language `modifiers` list (Python emits only the negative `private`; C#/Java `public`; Go `exported`/`unexported`; Rust `pub`; Solidity `external`; Apex/Clojure/Hack via a separate `meta['visibility']`) *and* a path-heuristic `supply_chain.is_exported` boolean — which disagreed on 7 self-corpus symbols. A new `visibility.py` declares the canonical vocabulary (`public` / `private` / `protected` / `internal` / `package`) + the per-language modifier→level mapping, exposed as `all_known_visibility_levels()` and wired into `_known_axes()` as the `visibility` axis. A finalize pass (`_finalize_compute_visibility`) computes `Symbol.visibility` once for every symbol from the highest-priority signal — **a language modifier wins over the Python leading-underscore name convention, which wins over the public default** (language syntax is authoritative for visibility; the path heuristic conflated publishedness/test-penalty with it) — and records the deciding signal in `meta['visibility_signal']`. The legacy `meta['visibility']` key (Apex/Clojure/Hack) is folded into the field and removed (it collided with the new typed field). **This half is additive**: `is_exported` and the visibility terms still in `modifiers` are reconciled to `visibility` in a follow-up (which flips the 7 conflicts and is bakeoff-validation-gated).
- **A general YAML analyzer emits a file-anchor node per generic `.yaml`/`.yml` file (INV-babuj).** YAML was recognised by file classification (174 files on the self-corpus) but only `yaml_ansible` ran — and that pass matches *Ansible-shaped* YAML only — so 173 of 174 YAML files had zero nodes in the behavior map: CI workflows, framework-pattern catalogs (`packages/*/frameworks/*.yaml`), the self-catalog, and config were all invisible to search / file-anchor centrality / supply-chain classification / the orphan ratchet. The new `hypergumbo_lang_mainstream.yaml` analyzer (`@register_analyzer("yaml", languages=["yaml"])`, which auto-registers `"yaml"` into the catalog-derived language axis) emits exactly one `kind="file"` anchor per file — **file-anchor-only**, no per-key/per-document nodes (YAML's key space is open-ended and consumers don't branch on it). It coexists with `yaml_ansible` by subtracting `find_ansible_files(root)` from its candidate set, because `make_file_id` is language-keyed (a `language="yaml"` and a `language="ansible"` anchor for the same path would be two distinct file Symbols — the finalize external-dedup only collapses dangling references). Self-analysis: **173 YAML file anchors now present (was 0)**. (Markdown / CSS / Just / Mermaid also lack content analyzers — same INV-nanon "no-analyzer-registered" sub-pattern — but are out of scope for this YAML-specific item.)
- **`EntrypointKind` becomes a lightweight catalog-derived axis — `all_known_entrypoint_kinds()`, wired into `_known_axes()` as `entrypoint-kind` (WI-pupiz).** The entrypoint-kind vocabulary lived only as the `EntrypointKind` enum with no catalog resolver, so consumers had no canonical "list every entrypoint kind we recognise" function and the schema `Entrypoint.kind` description hardcoded a partial example list that included a phantom value (`framework_handler`, never a real kind). A new `entrypoints.all_known_entrypoint_kinds()` (single source = the enum) is registered as the `entrypoint-kind` axis in `multi_value_field_axis._known_axes()` (mirroring the `language` / `pass-id` catalog-derived axes per ADR-0024 §4), and `generate-schema` now generates the `Entrypoint.kind` enumeration from it (the phantom is gone; the full sorted catalog is documented). `kind` (what the entrypoint IS) and the WI-rukam `meta.evidence_type` (how it was detected) are now explicitly the two distinct entrypoint axes. **Premise note:** the originating symptom (cli_command/background_task/connectivity_based/websocket_handler absent on the self-corpus) was re-verified — 3 of the 4 are *dark-for-good-reason* (no triggering construct, or the connectivity fallback only firing when no concept entrypoints exist), and the genuine `cli_command`-for-argparse detection gap is tracked as the sibling WI-lubap; this item delivers the catalog axis, not the argparse detector. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (the `kind` change is a description refinement, not a structural change).
- **Entrypoint records grow a `meta` provenance dict mirroring `Edge.meta` — `id` + `source` + `evidence_type` (WI-rukam; SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.2 → 0.14.3).** Entrypoint records carried only `{symbol_id, kind, confidence, label}`, so a consumer could not tell whether a `confidence=0.6` entry came from a framework route detector, a manifest target, or a library-export heuristic — the entrypoint analog of the `Edge` provenance shape (`Edge.id`/`origin`/`evidence_type`) was simply absent. Each record now nests, under `meta`: `id` (a stable `entrypoint:sha256:<16hex>` content hash of kind+symbol_id+label, **auto-stamped in `__post_init__` on every record** — identity excludes confidence, which is a ranking value), `source` (the producer pass — `concept_detector` / `connectivity_fallback` / `script_module_detector`), and `evidence_type` (the inference pathway — `manifest_declared` / `framework_pattern` / `structural` / `language_convention` / `naming_heuristic` / `connectivity_heuristic`, aligned 1:1 with the spec §8/§9 confidence tiers). A new `Entrypoint.create(...)` factory sets and validates `source`/`evidence_type` against the single-source `ENTRYPOINT_SOURCES` / `ENTRYPOINT_EVIDENCE_TYPES` frozensets (a typo'd pathway is a construction-time `ValueError`, mirroring `Edge.create`'s access-mode guard); all 38 producer sites in `entrypoints.py` were migrated to it. The three keys are registered on a **new `entrypoint_meta` axis** in `axis_meta_keys.py` (the registry's third axis alongside `symbol_meta`/`edge_meta`), and the cross-axis collision test is now axis-aware (an `entrypoint_meta` key is checked against `Entrypoint` fields, not the union of `Symbol`+`Edge` fields — so `meta["id"]` is legal even though `Edge` has a typed `id`). Schema `Entrypoint` `$def` gains a documented `meta` object; the field is additive (no `additionalProperties:false`), so existing consumers (which read only `symbol_id`) are unaffected.
- **`hypergumbo slice --entry <X> --io-boundary <category>` filters a slice to the edges that reach a named I/O boundary, classified ephemerally (WI-fakuv, filter half).** This is the slice-scoped twin of `hypergumbo io-boundaries`: after building the entry's slice, `compute_boundary_map` stamps `meta['io_boundary']` on the slice's edges *in memory* (the consumer-time derivation the REFRAME made canonical — no persisted field), then the slice keeps only edges whose stamp equals the requested category, plus their endpoint nodes and the entry. An unknown category exits 2 with the valid `KNOWN_IO_BOUNDARIES` list (mirroring the WI-furop slice/search filter-validation discipline). Resolves the prior spec gap where `slice --io-boundary` was documented-then-unimplemented (the verbatim spec example exited with "unrecognized arguments").
- **Advisory range-validation guards that analyzer edge confidence stays within its derived band (WI-nurun step 4, ADR-0039).** `spec_validator` now flags any `Edge.confidence` outside the `[low, base_confidence]` band for its `evidence_type` — `low` being the `is_resolved`-conditioned floor (`base_confidence_unresolved`) for the multimodal call types, else the 0.30 analyzer floor — as an advisory `cross_field`/`info` violation. It's a *forward* regression guard: the post-migration corpus is fully in-band (0 violations across all 75 seeded pathways), so this catches a future producer re-hardcoding an off-band value or over-claiming the reserved-ceiling 1.0. Unregistered/not-yet-seeded pathways carry no band (in-band by default, so the deferred 1.0 ceiling-breach pathways are not flagged here — they're a separate producer-side fix). New `confidence.confidence_within_band` reader. This completes WI-nurun (the confidence:F1 derivation work).
- **confidence:F1 derivation now conditions on `Edge.is_resolved` for the multimodal call types (ADR-0039).** Following the reader's first slice, the two high-volume call pathways whose detection reliability splits on resolution gained a `base_confidence_unresolved`: `ast_call_direct` (resolved 0.85 / unresolved 0.50) and `ast_call` (resolved 0.85 / unresolved 0.40), each verified against the live `is_resolved`×confidence cross-tab (94–99% bimodal). `derive_confidence(evidence_type, *, is_resolved=True)` returns the unresolved variant for unresolved edges on conditioned pathways and the base otherwise; single-valued pathways ignore `is_resolved`. Still **not wired** into `Edge.create` (no published value changes) — this completes the derivation *table* for the highest-volume edge types ahead of the default-flip step.
- **A central evidence→confidence derivation reader lands (confidence:F1 first slice; ADR-0039, INV-suvil).** Per ADR-0039, `Edge.confidence` is *detection reliability* and should be derived from the inference pathway (`Edge.evidence_type`), not hardcoded at each of the ~575 emit sites (the per-emitter accident INV-suvil names). This first slice introduces the layer **without changing any published value**: `EvidenceTypeSpec` gains a `base_confidence: float | None` field (seeded for the 8 single-valued, in-band inference pathways whose confidence is invariant on the live corpus — `ast_import`/`ast_new` 0.95, `span_overlap`/`import_to_manifest` 0.90, `ast_name_read`/`module_attribute_reference` 0.85, `module_export_heuristic`/`callback_argument_reference` 0.75), and a new `hypergumbo_core.confidence.derive_confidence(evidence_type)` reads it — returning `None` for unregistered or not-yet-seeded types so callers keep their own literal (no blanket fallback, honoring the withdrawn 0.30 unknown-evidence MUST). Deliberately scoped: the reader is **not yet wired** into `Edge.create` (the ~44k-edge default flip) or any producer; multimodal pathways (`ast_call`/`ast_call_direct`, driven by `is_resolved`), ranking-contaminated `type_hierarchy`, and the `naming_convention`=1.0 ceiling-breach are deferred to later F1/F2/producer-fix slices. A property test enforces the documented analyzer/linker confidence band ([0.30, 0.95]) on every seeded value. Advances INV-suvil (confidence hardcoded rather than derived).
- **`hypergumbo routes --format json` adds machine-readable output (INV-jutuj).** The `routes` subcommand was text-only while `test-coverage` and `dead-code-maybe` already supported `--format {text,json}`. It now emits a `{schema_version, view: "routes", routes: [...]}` envelope to stdout (each record carries `id`, `name`, `path`, `language`, `span`, `method`, `route_path`, `controller_action` — extracted from both concept-enriched and `kind="route"`/`framework_role` symbols), with the run summary sent to stderr so stdout stays parseable; an empty result emits `{"routes": []}` rather than the human-readable "No API routes found" text.
- **`hypergumbo sketch --no-comparison-sketches` opts out of the 4x/16x comparison sketches and the representativeness table (WI-fufop).** `sketch` unconditionally generated three sketches (the requested budget plus 4x and 16x) and printed the "How Representative Is This Sketch?" table whenever a token budget was set — useful interactively, but 3x the generation cost and noise for batch/scripted single-budget runs (the default 8000-token budget made the comparison always fire). The new opt-out flag (following the `--no-secret-scan` pattern) skips both the extra generation and the table; default behavior is unchanged.
- **Kotlin `val`/`var` properties now emit `field`- and `variable`-kind symbols (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — Kotlin slice, the first non-gated tail-language slice).** The Kotlin analyzer emitted functions/classes/objects but no symbol for a `val`/`var` `property_declaration`, so module constants (`const val`), module state, and class/object/interface fields had no node in the symbol graph. A `property_declaration` in a class/object/interface/enum body now emits `kind="field"` (`Class.field` name, class-scoped canonical `make_typed_stable_id`), a top-level one emits `kind="variable"` (canonical `make_variable_stable_id`), and a function-/block-/lambda-local `val`/`var` is excluded — a new `_kotlin_property_scope` walk classifies by the nearest body-defining ancestor. That scope guard is the discipline whose absence produced the Swift/Go `INV-lanaz`/`INV-sidab` local-leak regressions: the analyzer's bundled tree-sitter-kotlin grammar wraps `init { }` bodies in a `block` node, so an init-block `val` is correctly treated as a local (a dedicated regression test locks this in). The type annotation becomes `signature`, `private`/`internal`/`protected` → not `is_exported`, and property annotations (`@JvmField`) flow into `meta["decorators"]`. Kotlin lives in `hypergumbo-lang-mainstream` but is not one of the 8 `FIXTURE_ANALYZER` languages the emission-parity matrix gates, so this slice is guarded by a dedicated test file rather than a matrix xfail-strip. Blast-radius (adds field + variable symbols across the Kotlin corpus, shifting symbol/centrality/dead-code counts) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
