- **Python class attributes (incl. dataclass fields) now emit `field`-kind symbols — the final emission-parity F5 strip, fully closing the cross-language field/variable gate (WI-jusus).** py.py emitted module-level variables but no `field`-kind symbols, so class attributes — including dataclass field declarations (`x: int`) in `ir.py`/`datamodels.py` and config constants — had no node in the symbol graph. Class-body `Assign`/`AnnAssign` with `Name` targets now emit `kind="field"` (`Class.attr` name; class-scoped canonical `assemble_stable_id` via the class's file-anchored stable_id; the annotation as `signature`); instance attributes (`self.x = ...` set in methods) are out of scope. **Strips the last `(python, emits_field)` strict-xfail hole** — every `emits_variable`/`emits_field` cell in the emission-parity gate is now a hard lock (the gate's F5 columns are fully closed; eight per-language slices ratcheted them away). Blast-radius: Python is hypergumbo's own self-analysis substrate, so its dataclasses now contribute field nodes. Bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **Swift stored properties and top-level `let`/`var` now emit `field`- and `variable`-kind symbols (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — Swift slice, a double strip).** The Swift analyzer emitted computed properties (`kind="property"`) but not stored properties or top-level value bindings — both are bare `property_declaration` nodes (no `computed_property` child). A stored property inside a type body now emits `kind="field"`; a top-level (`source_file`-scope) `let`/`var` emits `kind="variable"` (Swift uses one node type for both — the enclosing type discriminates; function-/closure-local bindings are excluded). Both mirror the func/method path: a canonical `make_typed_stable_id`, the type annotation as `signature`, and `public`/`open` → `is_exported`. Computed properties remain `kind="property"`. **Strips both the `(swift, emits_field)` and `(swift, emits_variable)` strict-xfail holes** — and since Swift was the last `emits_variable` hole, **every `emits_variable` cell in the emission-parity gate is now a hard lock**. Blast-radius (adds field + variable symbols across the Swift corpus) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **Rust struct fields and module-level `const`/`static` now emit `field`- and `variable`-kind symbols (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — Rust slice, a double strip).** The Rust analyzer emitted neither: struct fields (Rust's primary data construct) and module constants were both absent from the symbol graph. Named struct fields now emit `kind="field"` (`Struct::field` name, Rust's `::` member convention with the node-id segment collapsing `::`→`.`; tuple structs have positional/unnamed fields, so none); module-level `const`/`static` (top-level or inside a `mod` block — not function-body locals or impl-/trait-associated consts) emit `kind="variable"`. Both mirror the func/method path: a canonical `make_typed_stable_id`, the declared type as `signature`, and `pub` → `is_exported`. **Strips both the `(rust, emits_field)` and `(rust, emits_variable)` strict-xfail holes** — the emission-parity gate's first double strip. Blast-radius (adds field + variable symbols across the Rust corpus) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **C# fields now emit `field`-kind symbols, and field attributes (DI `[Inject]`, EF Core `[Column]`/`[Key]`) gain a `decorated_by` anchor (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — C# slice).** The C# analyzer emitted properties (`kind="property"`) but no `field`-kind symbols, so plain fields and their attributes had no node in the symbol graph. Each `field_declaration` declarator now emits a `kind="field"` Symbol mirroring the `method_declaration` path: class-scoped `qualified_name`, a canonical `make_typed_stable_id`, the declared type as `signature`, and `public` → `is_exported`; multi-declarator fields (`int a, b;`) emit one symbol per name. The field's C# attribute metadata flows through the existing `_extract_attribute_edges` path (C# stores attributes under `meta["annotations"]`), so `[Inject]` / `[Column]` now emit `decorated_by` edges anchored on the field. Properties remain a separate `kind="property"`; the existing `class_field_types` chained-call resolution is preserved. **Strips the `(csharp, emits_field)` strict-xfail hole** from the emission-parity gate. Blast-radius (adds field symbols across the C# corpus) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **Go struct fields now emit `field`-kind symbols (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — Go slice).** The Go analyzer emitted package-level variables but no `field`-kind symbols, so struct fields — Go's primary data-modeling construct — had no node in the symbol graph. Each NAMED struct field now emits a `kind="field"` Symbol mirroring the func/method path: struct-scoped `qualified_name` (`Struct.field`), a canonical `make_typed_stable_id`, the declared type as `signature`, and capitalization-derived `is_exported`. A `field_declaration` naming several fields (`a, b int`) emits one symbol per name; embedded fields carry no field name and remain `base_classes` (not field symbols). **Strips the `(go, emits_field)` strict-xfail hole** from the emission-parity gate (now a hard lock). Blast-radius (adds field symbols across the Go corpus, shifting symbol/centrality/dead-code counts) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **Java class fields now emit `field`-kind symbols, and field annotations (Spring `@Autowired`/`@Inject` DI, JPA `@Column` ORM) gain a `decorated_by` anchor (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — Java slice).** The Java analyzer emitted no `field`-kind symbols, so class fields and constants had no node in the symbol graph and field-level DI/ORM annotations had nothing to attach to. Each `field_declaration` declarator now emits a `kind="field"` Symbol mirroring the `method_declaration` path: class-scoped `qualified_name` (`Class.field`), a canonical `make_typed_stable_id` (same-named fields in different classes/files stay distinct), the declared type as `signature`, and access / `static` / `final` `modifiers` (`public` → `is_exported`). Multi-declarator fields (`int a, b;`) emit one symbol per name. The field's annotation metadata flows through the existing `_extract_annotation_edges` path (kind-agnostic on the decorated side), so `@Autowired` / `@Column` now emit `decorated_by` edges anchored on the field symbol (unresolved-external for non-standard annotations). This **strips the `(java, emits_field)` strict-xfail hole** from the emission-parity gate (now a hard lock — the ratchet's first cross-language strip). Blast-radius (adds field symbols across the Java corpus, shifting symbol/centrality/dead-code counts) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **The emission-parity gate now locks variable/field-kind emission across languages (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — the F1 gate-before-sweep).** `test_emission_parity_matrix.py` (guardrail G2) gained two columns — `emits_variable` and `emits_field` — measuring whether each language analyzer emits a `kind="variable"` / `kind="field"` Symbol for a module-level variable and a class/struct field now present in every per-language fixture. Measured-then-locked: JS/TS emit both (slices 1+2); Python and Go emit the module variable. The remaining `(language, kind)` cells are strict-xfail holes a per-language Wave-3 emitter fix strips — `emits_variable` holes: rust, swift; `emits_field` holes: python, go, java, csharp, rust, swift. A new `COLUMN_APPLICABILITY` map skips inapplicable cells so the gate never asserts a non-existent construct: `emits_variable` is not parametrized for Java/C# (no module-level variables — every value binding is a class member). Establishes the gate that the cross-language variable/field emission sweep ratchets against.
- **TypeScript/JavaScript module-level variable symbols now emit (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — slice 2 of 2).** Following slice 1 (class fields), top-level `const`/`let`/`var` declarations now emit `kind="variable"` symbols, so module constants and module state (`const MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY = …`, `let ws`) — previously absent from the symbol graph for every non-Python/Go language — become visible to search, centrality, and io-boundaries. Mirrors the Python analyzer's **module-level-only** contract: bindings emit at program scope (including `export const …`, flagged `is_exported`) but **not** inside function bodies or blocks (`_is_module_level_declaration` gates on the declaration's parent being `program` or a top-level `export_statement`). Identity is the canonical `make_variable_stable_id(language, repo-relative-path, name)` (a same-named const in two files stays distinct; a rare module-scope `var` redeclaration is disambiguated by the within-file collision post-pass, ADR-0035 §3); a TS type annotation becomes the `signature`. Function-valued declarations (`const f = () => …`, incl. the `catchAsync(…)` wrapper) keep their existing `kind="function"` emission — the variable path runs only when the function path did not, so there is no double-emission. **Out of scope (follow-ups):** object/array destructuring patterns (`const { a, b } = …` — the declarator's `name` field is a pattern, not an identifier, so they are skipped), function-body / bare-block locals, and TS ambient `declare const` (type-only). This is the biggest-blast-radius half (adds a node per module binding across the JS/TS corpus) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-jusus).
- **TypeScript/JavaScript class fields now emit `field`-kind symbols, and field decorators (lit `@property`/`@state`) gain a `decorated_by` anchor (WI-jusus, emission-parity F5 — slice 1 of 2).** The JS/TS analyzer emitted **zero** `variable`/`field` symbols (as did every non-Python/Go analyzer), so class fields — `public_field_definition` in TS, `field_definition` in JS — had no node in the symbol graph, and lit's field-level reactive-property decorators had nothing to attach to (only the class-level `@customElement` was captured; a component's `@property`/`@state` reactive contract was entirely absent). Class fields now emit `kind="field"` symbols mirroring the `method_definition` path: class-scoped `qualified_name` (`Class.field`), a canonical `make_typed_stable_id` (so same-named fields in different classes/files stay distinct even when untyped — the identity folds `name` + `qualified_name` + file), the type annotation as `signature`, and `static`/`readonly`/accessibility (`public`/`private`/`protected`) `modifiers`. The field's `meta["decorators"]` flows through the existing `_extract_decorator_edges` path, so `@property`/`@state` now emit `decorated_by` edges anchored on the field symbol (resolving unresolved-external, as the lit decorators are imported). Computed field names (`[Symbol.iterator]`) are skipped — they carry no stable string identity. **Scope:** this slice is JS/TS class fields only; module-level variable symbols (`const`/`let`/`var` bindings → `kind="variable"`) are WI-jusus slice 2, and the remaining non-JS/TS analyzers (Java/Rust/etc. fields) are the broader WI-jusus epic. This is a blast-radius change (adds field symbols across the JS/TS corpus, shifting symbol/centrality/dead-code counts) — bakeoff validation pending.

### Changed

- **`explain` now errors on an ambiguous symbol name instead of silently picking every match (INV-nogof — cli-output F3; behavioral).** `slice` raised `AmbiguousEntryError` when a name resolved to symbols in more than one file, but `explain` iterated all matches with no gate — identical input, different outcomes across two read subcommands. The ambiguity policy is now one shared `slice.raise_if_ambiguous(entry_spec, candidates)` (extracted from `slice_graph`, consumed by both `cmd_slice` and `cmd_explain`): a name matching >1 file is ambiguous → `explain` prints the candidates to **stderr** and exits **1** (was: exit 0, every section on stdout). Exact node-ID matches and same-file duplicates are not ambiguous. Escapes: `--first` (top match), `--language`/`--file` (narrow the pool). `explain` keeps its own case-insensitive matcher (only the ambiguity *decision* is shared, not the resolver — avoiding a case-insensitivity regression). Core package 100% coverage.
- **Read-view JSON output now flows through one `add_schema_envelope()` gateway (WI-gogif — cli-output F1 keystone; internal, byte-identical).** The `{schema_version, view, …payload}` envelope idiom was inlined at ~6 sites (`slice`, `routes`, `verify-claims`, `test-coverage`, handler-slice, handler-slice-index) with no single source, inviting drift. It's now single-sourced through `add_schema_envelope(payload, view=…, schema_version=…)`, which produces the canonical **spread** envelope per spec Appendix C (schema_version + view + payload at top level, **not** nested under a `data` key, no `query` field). `schema_version` stays **per-view** (a substrate echo, a wire constant like `IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION`, or a placeholder — deliberately not unified; that is a separate version-policy change, WI-bobog). Output is byte-identical — a pure refactor establishing the gateway that the missing-flag / envelope leaves retrofit onto. (`io-boundaries` keeps its own view-less frozen contract.) Core package 100% coverage.
- **Read-view envelope versions are single-sourced and the three version axes are documented (WI-bobog, WI-romup — cli-output F4 / schema:F4).** The six CLI read-view envelopes (`routes`, `test-coverage`, `config`, `catalog`, `cache-status`, `dead-code-maybe`) each hardcoded a bare `schema_version="0.1.0"` placeholder with no shared owner — the exact "version-field naming inconsistent across surfaces" WI-bobog flagged. They now single-source `schema.READ_VIEW_SCHEMA_VERSION` (value unchanged, byte-identical output; a view promotes to its own named constant when it needs to evolve independently, as `io-boundaries`/`verify-claims` already have). The `schema.py` "Version Distinction" docstring and spec Appendix C now name the **three deliberately-independent version axes**: (1) top-level format version `schema_version`; (2) tool version `__version__` (aliased as `hypergumbo_version` / `analyzer_version` — *not* schema versions); (3) per-view / per-sub-schema versions (`READ_VIEW_SCHEMA_VERSION`, `IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION`, `VERIFY_CLAIMS_SCHEMA_VERSION`, `VALIDATION_REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION`). WI-romup: this documents why `validation_report.schema_version` (`0.3`) legitimately differs from the enclosing map's `schema_version` — they are independent sub-schemas — rather than consolidating them (which would discard real independent changelogs) or renaming the wire fields (a covert break). No behavior change, no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact`'s default selection flips from connectivity-aware to centrality-ranked; connectivity is now opt-in via `--connectivity` (WI-zulij / WI-kolal / WI-fumut; D12; awaits bakeoff validation).** Per the 2026-06-10 design ruling (D12) and spec §"Output reproducibility" (which already declared the compact/tiered default centrality-ranked), the `compact` view — and `run --compact` — now default to a **global centrality-ranked prefix** (most-important-first, matching the sketch) instead of connectivity-aware selection. The `--no-connectivity` flag is replaced by **`--connectivity`** ("connected core"), which opts into the connectivity-aware selection that bridges disconnected entrypoints. Consequences: **(WI-kolal)** the default prefix runs with `language_proportional` disabled, so a smaller `--max-symbols` budget selects a strict **subset** of a larger budget's selection — containment monotonicity now holds *by construction*; the language-stratified allocation's remainder redistribution could otherwise drop a previously-included node as the global budget grew (a non-monotonic step in `allocate_language_budget`). **(WI-fumut)** `--coverage` is now effective in the default path — it was structurally dead-code under the old connectivity-aware default (which ignored `target_coverage`). Tagged for bakeoff validation (the default compact output shape changes across the corpus). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`high_risk` retired as a net/fs risk axis — it is now a display-only marker scoped to `subprocess`, with net/fs risk carried by the taint source/sink model (WI-tijos Part B / ruling 1b; reverts WI-tijos Part A).** The `io-boundaries` `high_risk`/`has_high_risk` flag was an undocumented, code-only, display-only construct (0 occurrences in any ADR / `spec.md` / `schema.json`; consumed only by the CLI `*** HIGH RISK ***` markers + JSON keys, never by `verify-claims` or taint) that shadowed — and disagreed with — hypergumbo's actual, ADR-backed net/fs risk taxonomy: the taint source/sink model (`AUTO_SINK_ZONE_MAP`/`AUTO_SOURCE_LABEL_MAP`, ADR-0017 §2b), where every write-side/egress boundary is an untrusted sink and network-egress risk is additionally graded by supply-chain `dst_tier`. Extending `high_risk` to net/fs (Part B) would have duplicated that taxonomy and cascaded false positives — net/fs has no clean "always risky" invariant (`mkdir`/`os.stat` are benign), unlike `subprocess == arbitrary exec`. Per 1b, the **destructive-filesystem** (`shutil.rmtree`, `os.remove`/`unlink`/`rmdir`, `pathlib.Path.unlink`/`rmdir`) and **network-outbound** (`urllib.request.urlopen`/`Request`/`urlretrieve`, `socket.socket.connect`/`send`/`sendall`) entries are removed from `HIGH_RISK_PRIMITIVES` (`is_high_risk("shutil.rmtree")` and `("urllib.request.urlopen")` are now `False`; this **reverts WI-tijos Part A's `urlretrieve` addition** — the `net_send`→`network` taint sink carries it). The deliberate **`subprocess`** ratchet is unchanged (clean invariant; launch-vs-manage precision the taint model can't express, `HIGH_RISK_EXEMPTIONS_SUBPROCESS`). Display-only value narrowing: the `io-boundaries` envelope keys are unchanged so `IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `2.0`, and `verify-claims` (which never read `high_risk`) is unaffected. **Documentation hardening (the anti-recurrence fix):** the `HIGH_RISK_PRIMITIVES`/`is_high_risk` docstrings, a reciprocal `taint.py` cross-reference, ADR-0016 §1 (a new "Risk classification" clause + a transparency-tier/`is_resolved` implementation note that also records ruling A — command-mediated invocation such as bash shelling out to `curl`/`rm` classifies as `subprocess`/opaque, WI-javoh implementation pending), and spec §"IO-boundary reporting" now all state that `high_risk` is subprocess-scoped and cross-reference the taint model as the canonical net/fs risk taxonomy — closing the shadow-axis confusion whose root cause was `is_high_risk`'s own docstring teaching a flat "high-risk = fs + subprocess + net" model. Both packages stay at 100% coverage.
- **`data_direction` replaces the FFI-bridge `access_mode="write"`/`dest_access_mode="read"` direction smuggling, and `dest_access_mode` is removed — ADR-0038 ruling 3, vocab F4 PR 2 (awaits bakeoff validation).** ~25 FFI-bridge and protocol linkers stamped `access_mode="write"` + `dest_access_mode="read"` to mean *dataflow direction* (the caller passes data to the callee — the cgo docstring said it outright: "Go caller passes data to C"), NOT *access* (how src touches dst's state). Per the one-field-one-axis discipline (ADR-0023/0027/0028), that direction semantic moves to a new registered `Edge.meta` key **`data_direction`** (closed vocabulary `src_to_dst` / `dst_to_src` / `bidirectional`, `ir.VALID_DATA_DIRECTIONS`, threaded through an `Edge.create(data_direction=…)` kwarg mirroring `access_mode`), and **`dest_access_mode` is removed entirely** (it was observed zero-entropy — `"read"` on every populated record — so it measured nothing). 20 bridge/FFI/IPC stamps across cgo/jni/napi/pyffi/ruby_ffi/lua_ffi/wasm_bindgen/tauri_ipc/crypto_flow/ipc/websocket now emit `data_direction="src_to_dst"` and no longer carry `access_mode`/`dest_access_mode`. **`event_publishes` edges are the deliberate carve-out — they KEEP `access_mode="write"`** (publishing an event IS a genuine write to a channel; ADR-0038 §Neutral, and `event_publishes` is in the `access_mode` applicability matrix) and drop only `dest_access_mode`. The sole `dest_access_mode` consumer — slice.py's predictive `would_admit_dst_reader` admission counter — is removed with it. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (a meta-value/key change, not a structural one; `docs/schema.json` never referenced `dest_access_mode`). Tagged for bakeoff validation (bridge edges' dataflow meta changes shape across the polyglot corpus).
- **`access_mode` gains a declared per-edge-type applicability matrix, and the dataflow classifier stops stamping N/A edge types (`instantiates` + structural) — ADR-0038 ruling 2, vocab F4 PR 1 (INV-tibob / WI-hapab; awaits bakeoff validation).** `Edge.meta["access_mode"]` was populated on some edge types and absent on others with no declared policy, so a consumer reading `None` could not tell "the question doesn't arise" from "missing data" (INV-tibob), and the line-granular Tier-1 classifier stamped a spurious `write`/`mutate`/`read` on **~6,006 self-corpus edges where access is meaningless** — 5,992 `instantiates` (a constructor call is not an *access* of the class — WI-hapab) plus 14 `dispatches_to`. `axis_meta_keys.MetaKeySpec` now carries an optional `applicable_edge_types` / `na_edge_types` declaration, populated for `access_mode` from INV-tibob's 17-type census: **applicable** = `calls` / `references` / `module_attr_ref` / `event_publishes` (a `None` here means "fix the emitter"); **Declared-N/A** = 11 structural edge types + `instantiates` (a `None` here means "does not arise"; `script_src` — a census structural type but on the mid-fold `endpoint_shape` axis, ADR-0023 — is deferred with the 37, and is behaviorally moot since a `<script src>` include never reaches the dataflow classifier). Both `dataflow.annotate_dataflow` and `annotate_dataflow_ast` gate on the new `is_access_mode_not_applicable(edge_type)` resolver, so N/A edge types are never stamped (behavioral evidence: an `instantiates` edge now carries `access_mode=None`, a `calls` edge still derives `read`). The `access_mode` registry description is de-staled (`'read_write'` → the four-cell `read`/`write`/`mutate`/`delete` vocabulary). **Scope (ADR-faithful):** only the ADR's decided 17 census types are classified; the ~37 remaining canonical edge types stay UNCLASSIFIED, deferred to a polyglot-census follow-up — and the dataflow-DIRECTION family (`crypto_flow` / `data_flows_to`) is deliberately left out, as it is PR 2's `data_direction` territory (ADR-0038 ruling 3), not access. Tagged for bakeoff validation (access_mode drops from ~6,006 edges, shifting any taint / io-boundary consumer that read it on `instantiates`).
- **`verify-claims --json` now emits a versioned top-level envelope instead of a bare array, exposing the taint-coverage signal to machines (WI-nulot, INV-gatog; breaking for array-parsing consumers).** The `--json` output was a flat JSON array of verdict objects with no room for metadata — and the INV-javam "no taint-flow catalog for language(s): …" warning (which flags that a `confirmed` verdict on an uncovered language is really *inconclusive* because taint-flow had nothing to trace) went **only to stderr**, invisible to any CI gate parsing the JSON. Output is now `{"schema_version": "1.0", "view": "verify-claims", "verdicts": [...], "unsupported_taint_languages": [...]}` (mirroring the `io-boundaries --json` envelope, which already exposed its analogous `unsupported_languages`). `unsupported_taint_languages` is empty when there are no taint claims or every touched language has a catalog. This is a breaking change for consumers indexing the top-level array (`result[0]`) — read `result["verdicts"]` instead; the new `schema_version` lets consumers detect the shape. Behavioral evidence: `verify-claims --json` on hypergumbo's own map is now a dict with those four keys, `schema_version=1.0`, 18 verdicts, and `unsupported_taint_languages=[ansible, bash, csharp, gitignore, html, json, markdown, solidity, sql, toml, wgsl, yaml]` (previously stderr-only).
- **The synthesis/import values in `Symbol.origin` are declared legitimate synthetic pass-ids, not a separate axis — WI-kadop's proposed `synthesis_mechanism` field-split is withdrawn (ADR-0044).** WI-kadop was filed to split a `Symbol.synthesis_mechanism` field off `origin` (mirroring the ADR-0031/0032 `language`/`canonical_name` splits) because `origin` carried values naming *how* a symbol was synthesized (`inheritance`, `orchestrator_file_symbol_synthesis`, `boundary_external_symbol_synthesis`, `scip`) rather than *which registered pass*. Re-verifying the premise showed the ground truth had since shifted: the intervening **synthetic:F1** work gave the two high-volume synthesizers (1620 + 518 self-corpus symbols) real `AnalysisRun`s whose `pass_id` *is* the origin value, with the nodes' `origin_run_id` joining to it — so a synthesized symbol's "how" *is* "which pass". A field-split would then store the same string in two fields (a *new* redundancy) rather than remove a leak; and of the other two values, `inheritance` is a **phantom** (zero producers — the inheritance-linker stamps `make_pass_id("inheritance-linker")`, not the bare string) and `scip` is off-self-corpus. Per ADR-0044: no new field; `catalog._SYNTHESIS_MECHANISMS` is renamed `_SYNTHETIC_PASS_IDS` and re-documented as legitimate synthetic pass-ids, the `inheritance` phantom is dropped, and the SCIP-symbol `AnalysisRun`-join gap is split to WI-zabus (cross-package, rust-analyzer). No IR shape change, no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Precedent: a filed field-split can be correctly *withdrawn* when intervening work resolves the axis conflation it targeted.
- **`is_exported` now requires public `visibility`, and `modifiers` drops its visibility terms (INV-jusot second half; awaits bakeoff validation).** Completing INV-jusot, the finalize visibility pass reconciles the two remaining redundant visibility encodings against the canonical `Symbol.visibility` field (landed in the additive half): (1) **`is_exported` is downgraded to `False` for any non-public symbol** — language visibility is *necessary but not sufficient* for being a public-API member. This deliberately is **not** a pure `is_exported = visibility=='public'` alias: that would flip **22,319 of 38,243 self-corpus symbols (58%) from not-exported to exported — 19,151 of them in test files** — redefining `is_exported` from "public API member" (29% true) to "language-public" (87% true) and turning every public test function into an exported dead-code root. The `and`-with-visibility keeps `is_exported`'s public-API-membership meaning; on the self-corpus only **5 symbols move** (the `src/_foo` privates the path heuristic wrongly marked exported). Publishedness/test-penalty inputs stay on `supply_chain` (`is_test_file` / `tier`). (2) **`modifiers` keeps only non-visibility terms** — the `private`/`public`/`pub`/`exported`/`external`/`re_exported`/`unexported`/`protected`/… terms now live solely on `visibility`, leaving `modifiers` with `static`/`native`/`abstract`/… A consumer can now ask "is this symbol exported / public" from one field each (`is_exported`, `visibility`) with no per-language polarity logic and no silent cross-encoding disagreement. Tagged for bakeoff validation (5 symbols change `is_exported`, shifting dead-code/reachability for them + dependents).
- **`io-boundaries` headline `total_io_edges` redefined to the real/verified I/O surface (excludes `external_potential`); new `external_potential_edges` discloses the bucket separately; text & JSON now report the same canonical count (WI-huhit/WI-foduh; `IO_BOUNDARIES_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1.0→2.0).** The old headline summed *every* boundary chain including `external_potential` — which on self-analysis is **8,134 / 8,428 = 96.5% receiver-unresolved builtin method calls** (`append`/`get`/`split`/…), not real I/O — so a consumer reading `total_io_edges` as "I/O surface" over-counted **~28×**. `total_io_edges` is now `sum(chains) for boundary categories != external_potential` (294 real edges on the self-corpus); `external_potential_edges` carries the unverified bucket count (8,134). The text headline already excluded external_potential post-suppression but *varied* with `--show-external-potential` and disagreed with JSON ~9–28×; both modes now compute the same canonical real-categories count regardless of flags, and the text suppression line states the bucket is excluded from the total. **INV-pubom canonical definition amended (2026-06-30)**: the unfiltered (`BoundaryMap.to_dict`), filtered (`cmd_io_boundaries`), and text paths agree on the real-categories total + the separately-disclosed `external_potential_edges`. `verify-claims`' INV-bitig coverage gate ("did the analysis see *any* I/O?") now counts both fields, so a repo with only external_potential edges is still treated as analyzed (not a false "inconclusive"). Wire-format change → schema bumped 1.0→2.0; the envelope property tests are updated. Tagged for bakeoff validation (the headline number moves ~28×).
- **`Edge.create` now derives `confidence` from the inference pathway when no explicit value is passed (confidence:F1, ADR-0039).** The flat `confidence=0.85` default became a sentinel (`None`): when a producer omits `confidence`, `Edge.create` calls `derive_confidence(evidence_type, is_resolved=…)` and uses the registry-seeded value, falling back to 0.85 for not-yet-seeded pathways. **No published values change yet** — the seeded pathways' producers still pass explicit values that match the registry, and the dominant omit-confidence case (`ast_call_direct` resolved) derives 0.85 = the old default; the broad suite (15,486 tests) confirmed zero fallout. This is the enabling plumbing for the per-producer migration that drops explicit `confidence=` (the step that actually closes INV-suvil). Direct `Edge(...)` construction and `Edge.from_dict` are unaffected (create()-only).
- **Confidence derivation table extended from 10 to 75 inference pathways via a centralized `_CONFIDENCE_SEEDS` overlay (WI-nurun PR1, ADR-0039).** `evidence_types.py` now carries one authoritative table of per-pathway `base_confidence` seeds (65 new single-valued types overlaid onto the raw registry, disjoint from the inline-seeded specs) so that the upcoming producer migration — which drops explicit `confidence=` and lets `Edge.create` derive — lands the right value instead of the 0.85 fallback. Each seed is the **edge-weighted modal** confidence its producers currently hardcode (corpus-dominant value, AST-scan site modal as fallback for pathways absent from the self-corpus), so the dominant cohort is value-preserving and only per-emitter outliers will collapse to the canonical value. Seeds are constrained to `inference_pathway`-axis types within the 0.30–0.95 analyzer/linker band; **four 1.0-modal pathways (`naming_convention`, `build_dependency`, `subdir_include`, `tree_sitter`) are deliberately *not* seeded** — 1.0 is a reserved ceiling, so their producers' confidence over-claim is a separate producer-side fix, not a table seed. **No published values change** (seeds only affect edges that omit `confidence`, dominated by the already-seeded `ast_call_direct`); the producer migration that moves values rides later WI-nurun PRs (with the `confidence_model` v1→v2 bump).
- **Analyzer edge `confidence` is now *derived* from the inference pathway, not hardcoded per emitter (WI-nurun PR2; closes INV-suvil; ADR-0039).** The producer migration dropped the literal `confidence=` at **218 analyzer `Edge.create` sites** whose `evidence_type` is a seeded inference pathway, so those edges derive their confidence via `derive_confidence(evidence_type, is_resolved=…)` from the PR1 registry table. Self-analysis confirms it: **all 9,638 analyzer (non-linker) seeded edges now carry `confidence == derive_confidence(...)`**, with the per-emitter outliers collapsed to the canonical value (e.g. resolved `ast_call` 0.95→0.85, unresolved 0.5→0.40). **Kept explicit (NOT derived), by design:** (1) **linker** edges — a separate *match-quality* confidence model (spec §12); all 27 linker producer files were left untouched; (2) dynamically-**computed** confidence sites (linker match-strength, the `type_hierarchy` ranking dampener); (3) three **context-encoding** analyzer sites where a low hardcoded value signals ambiguity/unresolved-ness that single-valued derivation cannot express — Go ambiguous method call (0.50), Rust unresolved trait impl (0.70), Haskell external call (0.50). `confidence_model` bumped `hypergumbo-evidence-v1`→`v2`; SCHEMA_VERSION unchanged (a value change, not a structural one). Spec §12 "Edge confidence: analyzer evidence" rewritten from "planned, not implemented" to the implemented model.
- **`sketch_precomputed.centrality_scores` renamed to `additional_file_centrality_scores`, and the whole `sketch_precomputed` block formally marked `x-internal` in the schema (INV-napap, INV-jifus centrality face).** The field measured *additional-file symbol-mention frequency* (how often a config/doc file mentions graph-central symbols) — NOT graph centrality, which is a distinct, already-shipped concept (`hypergumbo symbols`' In/Out/Deg columns via `ranking.compute_centrality()`). The bare name `centrality_scores` conflated the two. The rename disambiguates at every producer/consumer site (`cli.py`, `sketch.py`). Because `sketch_precomputed` is an internal pre-computation cache — the spec already states consumers "should not depend on its presence" — `docs/schema.json` now annotates the block `x-internal: true` (the `generate-schema` comment that mis-described it as a stability contract was corrected to match the spec). No public-schema change, so SCHEMA_VERSION stays 0.14.2.
- **`auto-pr` / `do_merge` now auto-recover from Codeberg's DB desync instead of just printing a hint (WI-hubod).** The desync breaks AGit's `refs/for/` proc-receive hook — which does the DB writes that *create* the PR — producing 504 / "fail to run proc-receive hook" on push, and HTTP 405 ("Please try again later") on the merge endpoint; a *plain* branch push (`refs/heads/<branch>`, post-receive only) is resilient and its post-receive recomputes the DB representation, clearing the desync. Two additive, human-approved recoveries build on last entry's self-documenting hint: (1) **push fallback** — when `do_pr`'s AGit push fails, it now falls back to a plain (non-force) branch push + an API PR-create (new `create_pr` in `forgejo-api.sh`), reusing any PR a partial AGit push already created (and re-checking after a non-2xx `create_pr` so a server-side-created PR is reused rather than duplicated by a vPR), before giving up to a vPR; (2) **merge resync-retry** — when the merge endpoint fails on the final attempt with a desync signature (405 / 000 / 5xx / "proc-receive" / "try again later"), `do_merge` does one plain branch push to resync the DB and retries the merge once, verifying success by *either* the PR record *or* git ground truth (`_pr_landed_in_base` — `git merge-base --is-ancestor`), so the "reports-failure-but-succeeds" FF-landed case (where the ref lands but the PR `merged` flag lags) is recognized rather than mis-reported as a failure. The branch pushes are non-force and never target a protected branch. New test seams (`AUTO_PR_SIMULATE_DESYNC`, `AUTO_PR_SIMULATE_DESYNC_FALLBACK`, `AUTOPR_TEST_CREATE_PR`, `AUTOPR_TEST_RESYNC_PUSH`) make every path exercisable offline.
- **Self-documenting Codeberg desync workaround in CI/forge scripts.** When `auto-pr` / `merge-pr` / `ci-debug` hit the failure signatures of Codeberg's recurring "database representation out of synchronization" desync (504 / HTTP 000 / 405 / "remote rejected: same commit" / "fail to run proc-receive hook"), they now print a terse recovery hint via a shared `print_desync_recovery_hint` helper in `forgejo-api.sh`: do one plain branch push (its `post-receive` recomputes the DB representation and clears the desync), then retry. The hint explicitly directs the agent to surface the problem to the human and **never** contact Codeberg admins. Keeps the recipe at the point of failure instead of bloating AGENTS.md.

### Fixed

- **An unexpected error now exits cleanly instead of dumping a raw traceback (WI-himas — cli-input top-level handler).** The CLI dispatch caught only the typed `SubstrateError` (rc=2) and `BrokenPipeError`; any *other* uncaught exception in a command escaped as a raw Python traceback dumped at the user. `main()` gains a top-level `except Exception` safety net that converts an unanticipated error into a clean `hypergumbo: internal error: <Type>: <msg>` + "re-run with --debug for a full traceback" on stderr and `rc=1`. Under `--debug` the original exception is re-raised so the full traceback is preserved for diagnosis. It deliberately catches `Exception`, not `BaseException`, so argparse's `SystemExit` (`--help` / parse errors) and `KeyboardInterrupt` (Ctrl-C) propagate normally; the typed `SubstrateError`/`BrokenPipeError` handlers still run first. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`search` case-sensitivity is now uniform and an empty pattern is rejected (WI-runos, WI-kopon — cli-input standalones).** Two `search` input inconsistencies: (WI-runos) the positional pattern was case-insensitive (documented) but `--language`/`--kind` were case-sensitive (undocumented), so `--language PYTHON` rejected as "not a known language" while `--language python` worked — the flags now fold to the registry's canonical lowercase, making them case-insensitive and uniform with the pattern (`--language PYTHON` / `--kind FUNCTION` match). (WI-kopon) an empty or whitespace-only pattern (e.g. a shell-eaten unquoted `|`) is a substring of every name, so it silently matched all symbols; it now fails fast with `rc=2` + "search pattern cannot be empty." — consistent with the cli-input validation umbrella. Behavioral evidence (live): `search load … --language PYTHON` and `--kind FUNCTION` → `rc=0` with matches; `search ""` / `search "   "` → `rc=2`. No flag/schema change. Core package 100% coverage.
- **A non-directory positional path now fails loudly instead of silently analyzing a non-repo (INV-jibof — cli-input sub-B).** `hypergumbo routes /some/file.txt` (or `symbols`/`io-boundaries`/any read consumer) resolved the positional path to a file, found no cached results, ran analysis on the non-repo, found nothing, and printed "No X found" with `rc=0` — indistinguishable from a real empty result. The shared `_get_or_run_analysis` chokepoint now raises `SubstrateError` when the resolved `repo_root` is not a directory (and there is no `--input` / cached map), so the CLI dispatch converts it to `rc=2` + `not a directory. Pass a repository directory, or pass a behavior map via --input.`. `slice`'s smart `.json`-detection is unaffected (it sets `--input` before this path, returning early). Behavioral evidence (live): `routes /tmp/notadir.txt` and `io-boundaries /tmp/notadir.txt` → `rc=2` with the guidance; a real directory still analyzes (`rc=0`). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`slice` argument-validation: negative `--hub-threshold` rejected, explicit `--out slice.json` honored (WI-bolar, INV-fapid — cli-input sub-B / type-and-range validation).** Two silent-accept bugs in `cmd_slice`'s flag handling: (1) **WI-bolar** — `--hub-threshold` used `type=int`, so a negative value passed the `hub_threshold_raw if hub_threshold_raw else None` truthiness guard (negatives are truthy) and collapsed the slice instead of erroring; it now uses the shared `_nonneg_int_arg` factory, rejecting `<0` at parse time with `rc=2` while `0` stays the documented disable sentinel. (2) **INV-fapid** — `--out` defaulted to the literal string `"slice.json"`, indistinguishable from a user *explicitly* passing `--out slice.json`, so an explicit `slice.json` was silently overridden by entry-name auto-detection (writing to the cache dir instead of the requested path); the default sentinel is now `None`, so an explicit `--out slice.json` is honored and auto-naming triggers only when `--out` is omitted. No flag names changed (flag-matrix unaffected), no schema change. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`symbols --kind file` / `--kind variable` no longer return empty (WI-sufuh — cli-output F4).** `cmd_symbols` ran the silent low-value kind exclusion (`is_excluded_kind` — file/variable/package/CSS/markdown kinds, kept out of the connectivity table by default) **before** the explicit `--kind` filter, so explicitly asking for an excluded kind matched the pre-filter and returned nothing despite hundreds of such nodes. The exclusion is now suppressed for the exact kind the user requested (`is_excluded_kind(...) and not (args.kind and kind == args.kind)`); the default/`--all` path is unchanged (regression-fenced by the existing `test_cmd_symbols_excludes_excluded_kinds`), and requesting a non-excluded kind still hides the others. The default-hidden set is now disclosed in `--kind`/`--all` help and the `symbols` epilog. No flag added, no schema change. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`config` and `dead-code-maybe` JSON output now carry the schema envelope (INV-fipol, INV-pazum — cli-output sub-B / INV-pikob).** Their `--format json` output was a bare payload with no `schema_version`/`view`, inconsistent with `slice`/`routes`/`verify-claims`. Both now flow through the `add_schema_envelope` gateway (WI-gogif F1), gaining top-level `schema_version` + `view` (`config` / `dead_code_maybe`) with the payload **spread** beside them — so `config`'s `dataflow_patterns`/`io_primitives` and `dead-code-maybe`'s `summary`/`dead_candidates` stay top-level (additive, no consumer break). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact --connectivity` and `tiered` node selection is now reproducible across runs (WI-nivuj).** Connectivity-aware selection iterated the seed **set** and the frontier **set** directly, so seed ordering — and, on a connectivity-score tie, *which* node was selected — depended on `PYTHONHASHSEED`, making the projected node set/order non-deterministic run-to-run (contradicting spec §"Output reproducibility": "deterministic given the same input graph"). The seed init, the frontier tie-break, and the coverage force-include now iterate in **sorted** order, so a score tie resolves to the lexicographically-smallest node and the output is hash-seed-independent (verified by a multi-seed subprocess test). The centrality-ranked default was already deterministic (sorted by `(-centrality, name)`). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact` output now describes its own projection: per-node centrality, symmetric edge count, and `*_summary` companions (WI-zotam, WI-kulan).** (WI-zotam) Compact/tiered nodes carried **no** per-node `centrality`, so a consumer could not rank the retained nodes or cross-check them against the summary aggregate — each retained node now carries its selection mode's own `centrality` score. And `included_edges_count` appeared only in the connectivity-mode `nodes_summary`; the coverage (`--no-connectivity`) mode now reports it too, so both compact modes are symmetric. (WI-kulan) The compact view truncated `entrypoints` (filtered to retained nodes) and `features` (dropped when all anchors were pruned) but emitted only `nodes_summary` — it now emits parallel `entrypoints_summary` / `features_summary` companions (`{included:{count}, omitted:{count}}`) so a consumer can see how much of each array was omitted. All additive under the schema's permissive `additionalProperties`, so no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Published schema accepts the projected views: `view` is now an enum, not a `const` (WI-tagaj).** `docs/schema.json` pinned the top-level `view` field to `const: "behavior_map"`, so a `compact` or `tiered` projection (which correctly stamps `view: "compact"`/`"tiered"`) failed JSON-Schema validation on that one field. `view` is now an `enum` of all projected view names, single-sourced from a new `schema.VIEW_NAMES` tuple (`behavior_map`/`compact`/`tiered`) that the schema generator enumerates — future projection views append in one place. Backward-compatible (accepts a strict superset), so no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact`/`tiered` metrics now describe the projection, not the source (WI-pizat).** A budget-limited projected view shallow-copies the source map, so its `metrics` block (`total_nodes`/`total_edges`/`total_files`/`by_supply_chain_tier`) echoed the **full-repo** totals computed before projection — a view that lied about itself. Both projections now recompute `metrics` in place from their own projected `nodes`/`edges` (shared `_recompute_view_metrics` helper). Deliberately does **not** touch `analysis_incomplete`: per spec §726 that flag is analyzer-scope (early termination / errors / resource limits), not a view-truncation signal — a budget-truncated-but-complete view leaves it `False`. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact` rejects numerically invalid flag values instead of silently accepting them (WI-vusaf).** `compact --max-symbols 0`/negative, `--min-symbols` negative, and `--coverage` outside `[0.0, 1.0]` were silently accepted and produced degenerate output; `--max-symbols < --min-symbols` was also unchecked. Added argparse `type=` validator factories (`_positive_int_arg`/`_nonneg_int_arg`/`_unit_interval_arg`, reusable by the wider cli-input-validation block) wired onto the `compact` subcommand's `--max-symbols`/`--min-symbols`/`--coverage` and (structural analogue) `run`'s `--coverage`, plus a cross-flag `max < min` guard at the top of `cmd_compact`. All fail fast with exit code 2 and a clear stderr message; `0.0` and `1.0` coverage remain valid. Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact` now strips the heavy view-irrelevant top-level blocks (WI-judun — projection payload hygiene).** The budget-limited `compact` view built its output via a shallow `dict(behavior_map)` copy, so it **retained** the large `usage_contexts` and `sketch_precomputed` blocks — the former spec-mandated stripped from compact/tiered views (spec §usage_contexts), the latter an internal cache artifact consumers must not depend on (spec §707). Both are now dropped from compact (both selection branches, via a `_COMPACT_STRIP_KEYS` constant). Unlike the more aggressive `tiered` view, compact deliberately **preserves** `analysis_runs` and `validation_report` (finalize provenance + quality signals, ADR-0033/ADR-0043; locked by `test_compact_preserves_validation_report_and_consistency`). Core package 100% coverage.
- **`compact` no longer drops parallel edges from its connectivity-aware view (WI-hakom — projection:F1 induced-subgraph closure).** When two symbols were joined by more than one edge (e.g. a `calls` and a `references` edge between the same pair), the default connectivity-aware `compact` selection kept only **one** of them: `select_by_connectivity` built its induced subgraph from a `(src, dst)`-keyed dict, which collapses parallels to the last-seen edge. The induced edges are now derived directly from the edge **list** (matching the coverage and tiered branches, which were already correct), so every edge whose both endpoints survive into the compact view is retained. A new output-closure property test asserts the compact edge array equals the induced subgraph (both directions, parallels included). Core package 100% coverage.
- **PHP framework namespaces now import-promote — a manifest-less PHP app surfaces its framework (INV-naguv).** Three compounding gaps meant PHP route frameworks (laravel/symfony/cakephp/yii/slim) never promoted from source: (A) `php.py` emitted **zero** `imports` edges — it now emits one per `use Namespace\\Class;` statement (dst carries the full backslash namespace path), mirroring the scala import emitter; (B) `profile.py`'s `_module_match_kind` / `_is_specific_pattern` recognized only `.`/`/` separators — they now handle PHP's `\\` namespace separator; (C) `PHP_FRAMEWORKS` carried only composer coordinates — each framework now also lists its import namespace (`Illuminate`→laravel, `Symfony`, `Cake`, `yii`, `Slim`, `Phalcon`, `CodeIgniter`). Together, a `use Illuminate\\Support\\Facades\\Route;` now promotes `laravel` via the same prod-importer + language-match gate every other language uses. Core + mainstream packages 100% coverage.
- **Containment linker now roots WI-jusus fields for solidity/nim/scala/D container constructs (WI-sakug, Part A).** Fields emitted by the field-emission tail (solidity `contract`/`library` state variables, nim `type` object fields, scala `object` fields, D `union` members) stayed orphaned because their enclosing type's kind was absent from the containment linker's `CONTAINER_KINDS` — so the container was never indexed and the dotted-name field (`Token.totalSupply` → `Token`) found no owner (solidity 0% rooted, nim 8%). Added `contract`, `library`, `type`, `object`, `union` to `CONTAINER_KINDS`; `_find_parent`'s same-language + same-file/unique gate bounds false positives for the widely-emitted `type` kind. The D-enum / scala enum-and-`given` remainder (needing per-analyzer owner-symbol emission) is a deferred follow-up. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Synthetic linker stand-ins no longer pollute dead-code candidates (INV-disin).** ADR-0031 Class-B synthetic symbols (linker placeholder call targets — `protocol_origin` populated, `language=None`) were entering the dead-code-maybe candidate universe and showing up ~100% "dead", because language-scoped seeds/entrypoints can never match a `language=None` node (kserve/prometheus/killbill each surfaced dozens). `production_callables` now skips any node with a truthy `protocol_origin` before adding it as a production candidate — keyed on `protocol_origin` (the canonical Class-B marker, spec-validator-enforced), **not** `framework_role` (which also marks Class-A route callables that must stay candidates) nor `language is None`. (The item's original "language-stamping producer gap" diagnosis was incorrect — the stand-ins are intentionally `language=None`; the fix is consumer-side exclusion.) Core package 100% coverage.
- **Six more route-emitting framework YAMLs are now reachable from an import detection key (INV-mupuf).** flask-restful, masonite (Python), lumen (PHP), restify (JS), scalatra (Scala), and http4k (Kotlin) emitted route patterns but had no entry in `profile.py`'s per-language framework tables (and no `_FRAMEWORK_ALIASES` entry), so a manifest/import declaring them surfaced **zero** routes. Added a dedicated detection key equal to each YAML basename (no alias needed) to the language-appropriate table, and removed the six from the `test_route_framework_yaml_reachability` exemption set — the structural reachability invariant now enforces them. `node-http` (bare-Node `http.createServer`, a generic shared built-in name that must not alias to a single YAML) remains the sole exemption. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Julia `const` emitter no longer drops multi-name / typed members, and a `const` no longer clobbers a same-named function in call resolution (INV-vopap).** The `const_statement` branch used a single-`identifier` lookup, so `const a, b = 1, 2` (multi-name) and `const K::Int = 3` (typed) emitted **zero** symbols. It now reuses the assignment-target extractor (the same helper the module-variable branch uses) to emit one `const` symbol per bound name, handling tuple and typed LHS forms; the id is qualified with the enclosing module (matching the function branch) while the display name stays bare. Separately, a `const` is a legitimate call target (`const f = () -> …`) so it stays resolvable, but it no longer overwrites a same-named function already in the resolver map — a function-preferring tie-break, so a call resolves to the function, not the clobbering const. Lang-common package 100% coverage.
- **Elixir `def`/`defp`/`defmacro`/`defmacrop` inside a `quote` block are no longer mis-attributed to the defining module (INV-sinah).** A function or macro defined inside a `quote do … end` (an `__using__` template) is injected into the module that runs `use`, not defined on the defining module. The function/macro branches attributed such symbols to the syntactic enclosing `defmodule` with no quote guard (the `defstruct` branch already had one). Extended the same fails-safe suppression (`not _has_quote_ancestor`) to the `def`/`defp` and `defmacro`/`defmacrop` branches — a quote-block definition is suppressed rather than mis-attributed. (Correct recall — attributing to the *using* module via `use`/`__using__` macro expansion — is a larger cross-module resolution, deferred.) Lang-common package 100% coverage.
- **Vendored front-end assets under a first-party root now classify as tier-3 external, not first-party (INV-kokik).** Un-minified vendored third-party JS/CSS nested under a first-party path (e.g. `src/pretix/static/pretixbase/js/vendored/jquery.js`) was classified supply-chain `first_party` — inflating JS symbol totals and leaking vendored code into top-ranked symbols — because the tier classifier's vendored-directory detection was root-anchored (`^vendor/`, `^node_modules/`) and its deep patterns only matched Go SDKs. Added three unambiguous vendored-marker directory segments to the deep (`re.search`-anywhere) pattern set — `vendored/`, `npm_mirror/`, `bower_components/` — so they demote to `EXTERNAL_DEP` even under a `src/.../static/` prefix. Deliberately **not** a blanket `static/ → external` rule (many projects keep first-party JS/CSS under `static/`); a first-party static file with no vendored-marker dir stays tier-1 (guarded by test). Minified bundles (`*.min.js`) were already demoted upstream. Residual is completeness, not correctness: vendored libs copied bare into `static/js/` with no marker segment aren't caught (a deferred content-heuristic question). Core package 100% coverage. Un-minified vendored third-party JS/CSS nested under a first-party path (e.g. `src/pretix/static/pretixbase/js/vendored/jquery.js`) was classified supply-chain `first_party` — inflating JS symbol totals and leaking vendored code into top-ranked symbols — because the tier classifier's vendored-directory detection was root-anchored (`^vendor/`, `^node_modules/`) and its deep patterns only matched Go SDKs. Added three unambiguous vendored-marker directory segments to the deep (`re.search`-anywhere) pattern set — `vendored/`, `npm_mirror/`, `bower_components/` — so they demote to `EXTERNAL_DEP` even under a `src/.../static/` prefix. Deliberately **not** a blanket `static/ → external` rule (many projects keep first-party JS/CSS under `static/`); a first-party static file with no vendored-marker dir stays tier-1 (guarded by test). Minified bundles (`*.min.js`) were already demoted upstream. Residual is completeness, not correctness: vendored libs copied bare into `static/js/` with no marker segment aren't caught (a deferred content-heuristic question). Core package 100% coverage.
- **Rails view templates (erb/haml/slim) registered as CONFIG languages, closing a language-axis-conformance leak (WI-novob).** The view-template linker stamps `Symbol.language='erb'` (also `haml`/`slim`) on view-template file anchors (`.html.erb → erb`), but these had no `@register_analyzer` and no `taxonomy.LANGUAGES` LanguageSpec, so the WI-kunut language-axis catalog (analyzer/linker ∪ taxonomy.LANGUAGES) didn't recognize them — every such file-anchor tripped `axis_conformance` (19 on a chatwoot BROAD cohort). Registered `erb`/`haml`/`slim` as LanguageSpecs with `roles=FileRole.CONFIG`, matching the `html` precedent (view templates are structural HTML markup with embedded logic, not prose — CONFIG vs DOCUMENTATION give identical LOC/Additional-Files behavior, so CONFIG is the correct semantic label). Not folded to `ruby`: an `.html.erb` is an HTML template embedding Ruby, not Ruby source. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Route markers no longer dual-carry a redundant path-less `concept=route`; the framework is single-homed on `route_framework` — closing the producer-side root of two silent route bugs (INV-vokak).** `enrich_symbols` Phase 1 stamped every framework-pattern match onto a symbol's `meta['concepts']` with no guard, so the def-based Rails/Phoenix/Sinatra/Laravel `framework_role: '^route$'` patterns appended a *path-less* `concept=route` carrying only a framework onto a symbol that **already** carried the analyzer's `framework_role=='route'` marker — one route fact in two homes, the framework orphaned from the marker. WI-tosul Phase-1b-α's `route_of` framework-UNION only *tolerated* this at the accessor (reading the framework from the co-resident concept); the incoherent data persisted and was the reproduced root of BUG-1/BUG-2. The enricher now lifts such a concept's framework onto the marker's canonical `route_framework` home (first-wins, never clobbering an existing value) and drops the redundant concept, so the route fact stays single-homed — `route_of` reads the framework from `route_framework` rather than falling through to a co-resident concept (behavior-preserving: path/method/framework unchanged; usage-based route concepts that carry a `path`, and non-route concepts, are untouched). A standing `spec_validator` predicate (`route_marker_single_home`, wired into `validate_ir` and disclosed in the `wired_checks` manifest) flags any marker that regains the dual-carry shape; it is co-ratcheted by the validation-report gate (`test_validation_report_empty.py`) as a shrink-only dimension, measured at 0 across all four substrates — so a regression here, or a new route producer re-introducing the shape, blocks CI. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Scala (Play/akka-http/zio-http) and Haskell (Scotty) route frameworks now import-promote from their real import namespace instead of a build coordinate (WI-nizuv; PHP gap filed).** `LANGUAGE_FRAMEWORKS` carried only the build-tool *coordinate* for these (`com.typesafe.play`, `com.typesafe.akka`, `dev.zio`, bare `scotty`), which appears in build.sbt/*.cabal but never in the code's import path (`play.api.*`, `akka.http.scaladsl.*`, `zio.http.*`, `Web.Scotty`) — so on a manifest-silent repo the framework went dark and even the WI-tosul Phase-2 bare-exact relaxation couldn't reach it. Added the real import namespaces (`play.api`, `akka.http`, `zio.http`, `Web.Scotty`) — the same coordinate-vs-namespace fix spring-boot got (WI-tolap). Each namespace is dotted (so `_is_specific_pattern` promotes it without a manifest) and scoped *past the base library* (`akka.http` not `akka`, `zio.http` not `zio`), guarded by tests that base-library imports (`akka.actor`, `zio.ZIO`) do NOT promote the HTTP framework. Behavioral evidence: a manifest-less `import play.api.mvc._` file goes `frameworks [] → [play]`; `import Web.Scotty` → `[scotty]`. finatra was already reachable (its `com.twitter` coordinate happens to prefix-match `com.twitter.finatra.*` imports). **PHP (laravel/symfony/cakephp/yii/slim) is blocked on a deeper gap** — the PHP analyzer emits no import edges for `use Illuminate\…` statements, so there is no module path to match; filed separately. Core package 100% coverage.
- **Five route frameworks whose detection key differs from their YAML basename now load their route patterns — closing a silent route-drop that fired even WITH a manifest (INV-fosam; INV-mupuf filed).** `load_framework_patterns` resolves a detected framework name through `_FRAMEWORK_ALIASES` and then looks for `{resolved}.yaml`; a route framework detected under a key that is neither an alias nor a same-named YAML loaded **nothing** and emitted **zero routes**. Five route-emitting YAMLs were unreachable this way — `nextjs` (key `next`), `adonisjs` (`adonis`), `aspnet` (`aspnetcore`), `vertx` (`vert.x`), `zio` (`zio-http`) — so a correctly-detected Next.js / AdonisJS / ASP.NET Core / Vert.x / ZIO-HTTP app (manifest or import) silently surfaced no routes. Added the five aliases. Behavioral evidence: an AdonisJS app whose manifest declares `@adonisjs/core` with `@Get('/users')`/`@Post` handlers goes from `http_route` entrypoints **0 → 1** across the fix. A new structural invariant (`test_route_framework_yaml_reachability`) enforces this the correct way — **not** "every `LANGUAGE_FRAMEWORKS` key resolves" (that dict also carries ~140 ML/test/ORM/UI/crypto libraries with no route YAML by design), but the reverse: every YAML that emits a `route` concept must be reachable from a detection key or be explicitly exempted, so future key/basename drift fails the test. Seven route YAMLs unreachable via *import* detection but reachable only via manifest/package-name (`flask-restful`/`lumen`/`masonite`/`restify`/`scalatra`) or a generic shared `http` key (`http4k`/`node-http`) are exempted and filed as the distinct follow-up INV-mupuf. Surfaced by the WI-tosul Phase-2 bakeoff (which established this — not Phase-2's bare-import promotion — as the higher-yield corpus lever, since it fires on manifest-bearing repos). Core package 100% coverage.
- **Route transport protocol (`WS`/`LIVE`/`RPC`) no longer smuggles into the `http_method` verb field — it folds into a dedicated `route_protocol` axis, closing the mechanism-vs-category leak the route-surfacing audit flagged (INV-tibap; WI-tosul Phase-1b-β).** WebSocket (Starlette `WebSocketRoute`), Phoenix LiveView (`live`), and gRPC route markers wrote a transport sentinel into `http_method` — a value that is not an HTTP verb (a mechanism-vs-category leak; the census confirmed the transport-token set is exactly `{WS, LIVE, RPC}`). The transport now lives in a registered `route_protocol` meta key (`websocket`/`liveview`/`grpc`) split out at emit via a `routes.transport_meta` chokepoint (producers keep the sentinel locally for name/stable_id — identity is unchanged). `route_of` derives the protocol and nulls the method for transport routes; `routes.method_token` reconstructs the sentinel for method-bucketing / slice-filename consumers, so HTTP-client→route matching, OpenAPI matching, and handler-slice filenames are behavior-preserving. All raw-sentinel consumers were migrated in lockstep off `http_method` onto the protocol: the two `entrypoints.py` WS-dispatch sites (Phase-1b-β — `_detect_from_concepts` + the Pass-2 marker path now branch on `protocol == 'websocket'` and delegate extraction to `route_of`), `route_handler.py`'s Phoenix LiveView `live` flag (now `route_protocol == 'liveview'`), and the four `cmd_routes` dict-consumer sites (JSON record / dedup / sort / text render — via a dict-level `routes.protocol_method_token`). **`route_protocol` is a distinct axis, not a reuse of `Symbol.protocol_origin`:** `protocol_origin` is a typed field under the ADR-0031 Class-A/Class-B biconditional (route markers are Class-A `language≠None`, so they cannot carry it) and encodes synthesis provenance, not wire transport (the OpenAPI route synthetic carries `protocol_origin='openapi'` but HTTP transport); the grpc route synthetic that double-encoded both now keeps `protocol_origin='grpc'` and stamps `route_protocol='grpc'`. A value-set property test guards the chokepoint (fixture-scoped, not whole-corpus — a third-party repo may legitimately carry a developer-written `method: 'ws'`). Core + mainstream + common packages 100% coverage.
- **`route_of()` now unions the route framework instead of dropping it, and the http/openapi linkers' duplicate concept-first route extractors fold onto the marker-first accessor — fixing two silent route bugs a route-surfacing concept audit reproduced (WI-tosul Phase-1b; INV-vokak/INV-tibap filed).** A 2026-07-07 fundamental-concept audit of route surfacing (four suspect pairs, four-leakage-test verdicts: concept↔marker and route_path/http_method↔concept = SAME-FACT-CONSOLIDATE; http_method=='WS'↔protocol = FOLD; the EntrypointKind route family = DISTINCT-KEEP) reproduced two bugs live. **BUG-1 (framework loss):** `route_of`'s marker branch hardcoded `framework=None`, so Rails/Phoenix/Sinatra/Laravel routes (whose framework rides a co-resident `concept=route` the framework-YAML `framework_role: '^route$'` pattern stamps onto the analyzer marker) and Starlette (`meta['framework']`) all reported `framework=None`. It now **unions** the framework: `route_framework` key → legacy `meta['framework']` → the co-resident concept — path/method stay marker-first. **BUG-2 (silent route drop):** `linkers/http.py` and `linkers/openapi.py` each carried a verbatim-duplicate *concept-first* `_get_route_info_from_concept` that returned `(None, None)` on a path-less dual-carry concept — so those framework routes were silently dropped from HTTP-client→route (and OpenAPI-operation) edge matching (openapi's copy had no meta-fallback at all, also dropping every marker-only Go/Express route). Both now delegate to the canonical marker-first `route_of` (reconstructing the raw `WS` sentinel so method-bucketing is preserved), and their `_get_route_symbols` folds to `is_route`. Also: `route_path`/`http_method` are now registered `MetaKeySpec`s (they were read as the authoritative marker payload at ~12 sites yet bypassed the axis gate) and the additive `route_framework`/`route_protocol` keys are registered (target-D(ii); producer emission deferred to INV-vokak/INV-tibap). Core package 100% coverage; 4238 tests pass.
- **proto/thrift/smithy `service` and `rpc` symbols now fold to canonical construct kinds with a framework-role residue, closing a Symbol.kind axis-conformance leak the grep diagnostics missed (WI-rilal; audit-0013; INV-numat).** Executing audit-0013's already-ratified Cluster 27D fold on the three IDL analyzers: a `service` declaration now emits `kind="interface"` and an `rpc` method `kind="method"` (a service member), each carrying `meta["framework_role"]` = `"service"` / `"rpc"` — instead of the unregistered `kind="service"` / `kind="rpc"` that reported a false-clean axis bill. proto's `_make_proto_symbol`, thrift's `make_symbol`, and smithy's `_extract_shape` gained a `framework_role` parameter threading the role into `Symbol.meta`. These producers emit via **positional helpers** (`_make_proto_symbol(..., "rpc")`) that both the literal- and assignment-form greps miss, so audit-0013 had marked both values RESOLVED on a false "zero-producer" premise (doc-RESOLVED but producer-UNRESOLVED); the WI-zipis L3 producer-coherence descend sweep surfaced them, and audit-0013's status is corrected. The `Symbol.kind` helper-descent census drops 8→5 (the residual 5 are out of audit-0013 scope: smithy `structure`/`list`/`map` IDL kinds + powershell `filter`/`workflow`). lang-common + lang-extended1 packages 100% coverage.
- **The C++ analyzer's heap-`new` instantiation edge now carries the registered `ast_new` evidence_type instead of the raw tree-sitter node-type `new_expression`, closing an evidence-type-axis-conformance leak (WI-dagih; INV-numat).** `cpp.py`'s `new_expression` branch passed the literal tree-sitter node-type string as the `evidence_type` of its `instantiates` edge, so a C++ `new X()` emitted `evidence_type="new_expression"` — a value absent from the inference-pathway catalog (ADR-0028). The sibling analyzers emit the registered canonical `ast_new` for the identical construct (js_ts `new ClassName()`, java `object_creation_expression`); C++ was the lone outlier. Now folds to `ast_new` (a pure relabel — confidence is set explicitly, so scoring is unaffected; the distinct stack-construction path keeps its own registered `stack_construction` pathway). Surfaced by a fresh language-diverse BROAD cohort (binaryen, C++); behavioral evidence: binaryen `axis_conformance` 244→0. Mainstream package 100% coverage.
- **The `language` axis catalog now recognizes taxonomy file-types (asciidoc, makefile), and two build-manifest producers stop mislabeling targets, closing the last language-axis-conformance leaks (WI-kunut; INV-numat).** Two distinct roots. **(1) catalog gap:** `all_known_languages()` (the `language` axis resolver) was built only from `@register_analyzer`/`@register_linker` registration, but the orchestrator file-anchor synthesis labels file nodes with the *taxonomy* discovery language — so a `.adoc` → `asciidoc` and a `Makefile` → `makefile` (both real `taxonomy.LANGUAGES` LanguageSpecs, but with no dedicated analyzer) tripped `axis_conformance`. The catalog now unions the taxonomy names; the two sources genuinely diverge (gitignore/make/scss are registered but not taxonomy specs; asciidoc/makefile are the reverse), so the axis catalog is their union — the complete recognized-language set. **(2) producer mislabel:** `manifest_targets._extract_gradle` / `_extract_csproj` stamped `Symbol.language` with the build-manifest FORMAT (`gradle` / `csproj`) instead of the target's language, contradicting every sibling extractor (`sbt`→scala, `cabal`→haskell, `gemspec`→ruby, `dune`→ocaml, `nimble`→nim). A Gradle `mainClass` target is a Java class (its `target_path` is `.java`) and a `.csproj` StartupObject is a C# class (`.cs`), so they now emit `java` / `csharp` — removing two invalid values rather than registering them. Behavioral evidence (full runs): openzeppelin `axis_conformance` 36→0, detekt 6→0. With WI-muzuf + WI-gobip, the entire BROAD validation cohort (pretix / phoenix / openzeppelin / detekt, ~1,654 violations) is now at `axis_conformance` = 0. Core + mainstream packages 100% coverage.
- **Two axis-conformance leaks that kept `Edge.evidence_type` / `Symbol.origin` / `Edge.origin` / `AnalysisRun.pass_id` off their registries are closed (WI-gobip; INV-numat).** Surfaced by the BROAD validation drain on a Phoenix/elixir repo (515 `axis_conformance` violations). **(1) evidence-type:** the elixir analyzer's `_handle_dot_call` (module-qualified calls like `Helper.greet()` / `App.Module.func()`, resolved by exact / alias / resolver-suffix lookup) defaulted `evidence_type="module_qualified_call"` — a value never in the `inference_pathway` catalog (ADR-0028), ~512 edges on the repo. It now emits the registered generic `ast_call` (the pathway the rest of the analyzer already uses; the call shape is evident from the qualified dst, and zero consumers read the old value). **(2) pass-id:** a linker's *emitted* pass_id is its module-level `PASS_ID`, which can diverge from its registration `name` — the view-template linker family registers under `view_template` / `view_template_phoenix` / … but all emit `view-template-linker` (via `_view_template_core.PASS_ID`), so `Symbol.origin` / `Edge.origin` / `AnalysisRun.pass_id` carried a value absent from the pass-id catalog (`all_known_pass_ids()` only unioned the registration names). `all_known_pass_ids()` now also unions each registered linker's *emitted* `PASS_ID` (via `pass_metadata._resolve_linker_pass_id`, the same keying rule the finalize pass uses) — correct-by-construction for any future divergence, and a no-op for non-divergent linkers (`make_pass_id` is identity, so their emitted pass_id equals their name). Behavioral evidence (full run on the Phoenix repo): `axis_conformance` **515 → 0**. Core + common packages 100% coverage.
- **Boundary external symbols no longer carry a file path (or a build-tool label) in `Symbol.language`, closing a language-axis-conformance leak (WI-muzuf; INV-numat).** `create_boundary_nodes` derives an external symbol's `language` from its dangling-edge id (via `_parse_dangling_id`) or its `ExternalRef.lang`, and a producer that emits a bare-path dangler leaves a non-language there: a Solidity `import "../Governor.sol"` parses through `_parse_dangling_id`'s <5-part fallback to `language="../Governor.sol"`, a JS `script_src`/asset reference to `language="priv/static/phoenix.cjs.js"`, and a manifest producer can stuff a build-tool label (`gradle`) into the lang slot. On a real Solidity repo (openzeppelin-contracts) this produced **484** boundary nodes whose `language` was a file path — every one an `axis_conformance` violation (`Symbol.language` must be a value in `all_known_languages()` or None). The boundary chokepoint now normalizes the `language` **field** to a registered language or None (the axis allows None), using the same `catalog.all_known_languages()` set the validator checks against; a bare path, an unregistered label, or an empty value becomes None. The fix is field-only — the raw parsed value still feeds the id / stable_id / display_label (the id lang-slot cleanup is a separate concern, INV-dulah / WI-zugob), so no ids churn. Safe against the ADR-0031 language/protocol_origin coherence check (which explicitly `pass`es on an external-symbol-synth node with `language=None, protocol_origin=None`) and the ADR-0032 display_label check (which exempts `kind="external_symbol"`). Behavioral evidence (full `hypergumbo run` on openzeppelin-contracts): `axis_conformance` violations **519 → 36** (the 484 path-language externals gone; 0 external symbols carry a path-language), with the residual 36 being an unrelated `asciidoc` file-anchor catalog gap tracked separately. A registered language (e.g. a `go:fmt:0-0:Errorf` dangler → `go`) is unchanged. Core package 100% coverage.
- **A Python inherited-method call whose class inherits a builtin container (`dict`/`list`/`set`/`str`/…) alongside an in-tree mixin no longer resolves to the wrong in-tree method when the builtin shadows it in the real MRO (INV-guviv; a confidently-wrong-edge false positive; scoped fix).** The `inherited_calls` C3 walker spans only in-tree `extends` edges, so an external builtin base is invisible: for `class C(dict, Base)` calling `self.pop()`, Python's real MRO dispatches `dict.pop` (dict is first), but the in-tree walk saw only `Base` and bound `Base.pop` at 0.90 — a confidently-wrong edge. A new hardcoded `_STDLIB_BASE_METHODS` catalog (builtin base short name → its named methods; a sibling of `django_orm_dispatch`'s `DJANGO_BASE_METHODS` and `_third_party_bases`' `THIRD_PARTY_BASE_METHODS`, hardcoded over YAML per ADR-0029's "static language semantics live in code, next to the walker" rule) drives `_python_stdlib_base_shadows`: when a cataloged builtin that **defines** the called method is declared **before** the first in-tree base of the class, the resolution is biased to unresolved (the honest answer — the real target is a builtin method the analyzer cannot see). Applied at Site-1 (`self.method()`) and Site-2 Step-2 (typed receiver), Python-only. **Sound by construction — never over-suppresses the dominant external-mixin-first idiom** (`class V(LoginRequiredMixin, AuditMixin)` still resolves `AuditMixin.log_action`, because a non-stdlib mixin is deliberately not cataloged — the exact over-suppression the naive "any external base ahead → suppress" gate would have caused, which the INV-guviv design workflow rejected): it fires only when a cataloged builtin that defines the method precedes any in-tree base; a class defining the method itself, a builtin declared after the in-tree base, and a builtin that doesn't define the method are all exempt. An alignment guard (every in-tree parent name must appear among the declared bases) biases to no-shadow under import-alias name divergence, so an in-tree class aliased to a builtin's name is not falsely suppressed. Adversarially reviewed (no over-suppression found in any realistic scenario). **Scope:** the builtin subset — a generic 3rd-party base (e.g. `ext.SqlBase`, an ORM base) is not cataloged (the catalog is deliberately not open-ended, to avoid the over-suppression risk) and remains a documented residual, as do Site-3 (inherited-field) and multi-level ancestor shadowing (both under-suppression, safe). INV-guviv stays `violated` on those residuals. Core package 100% coverage.
- **The Swift/Vapor route extractor now recovers the full grouped-builder surface a RouteCollection controller uses, instead of only a bare `app`/`routes`/`router` receiver + verb (INV-povit; ~236 endpoints previously undetected on VernissageServer's `EXPECTED_ROUTES_BUT_FOUND_0`).** `_extract_vapor_usage_contexts` matched a route only when the verb's receiver was a bare reserved identifier, so every route registered through Vapor's builder chain was dropped: method-chained groups (`app.grouped("api").grouped("users").get(use:)`), closure groups (`routes.group("todos") { todos in todos.get(use:) }`, nested), variable-bound builders (`let g = routes.grouped("x"); g.get(use:)` — the dominant RouteCollection idiom), and the explicit-method form (`app.on(.GET, "stream", use:)`). The flat `iter_tree` scan is replaced by a structured, scope-threaded walk: `resolve_builder(node, bindings)` resolves a receiver chain to an accumulated group-path prefix (`.grouped(<Middleware>)` links add no segment), rooted only at a reserved receiver name or a `let`/`var`-bound builder tracked forward through the block (reassignment updates or invalidates it; a differently-named `RoutesBuilder`/`Application` parameter and the `Application.routes` property are also builder roots). Correctness gates make the coverage expansion safe by construction: the pass runs only on files that `import Vapor`/`Hummingbird`; a reserved name rebound to a non-builder (`let router = CustomRouter()`) is shadowed so it stops anchoring routes; the `.on` verb is validated against the HTTP-method set so `.on(.stateChange)` is not a route; and a non-literal path segment or an un-tracked alias fails safe (a miss, never a wrong route). Both parse shapes of a trailing-closure call (combined suffix in statement position, nested call in an initializer) are normalized. Adversarial review of the implementation additionally caught a regression the design missed — a route captured in a binding RHS (`let route = app.get("hello", use:)`) was dropped where the old flat scan caught it — now fixed by descending into the initializer. Behavioral evidence (`hypergumbo routes`, full pipeline on a RouteCollection fixture): 7/7 grouped/closure/variable-bound routes recovered with correct `/api/accounts/:id`-style paths and no duplicates (was 0). Mainstream package 100% coverage.
- **Site-3 inherited-field resolution now walks Python parents in C3 MRO order instead of insertion-order BFS, so an uneven-depth diamond resolves the field on the MRO-correct ancestor (WI-rarab; inherited_calls / dispatch:F3).** `_walk_parents_for_field` (which finds the declaring ancestor of a `self.field.method()` receiver's field) used a breadth-first walk. On an uneven-depth diamond — `E(C, D)`, `C(B)`, where both `B` and `D` declare the same field name with DIVERGENT types — BFS returns `D`'s field (a direct base, hit first) while Python's real MRO `[E, C, B, D]` resolves it on `B`, so the method bound to the wrong type. The walk now follows the `_linearize_c3` MRO for `src_lang == "python"` (other languages keep their insertion-order BFS), and an un-linearizable (cyclic/too-deep) Python hierarchy biases to unresolved (matching `_walk_c3`). A documented residual of WI-hiziz (D1). Behavioral evidence: `self.dep.run()` in `E` now resolves `run` on `B`'s field type (`BDep`), not `D`'s (`DDep`). Core package 100% coverage.
- **A comprehension / lambda / nested-def binding that shadows an outer typed variable no longer inherits the stale outer type, eliminating a class of confidently-wrong `receiver_type_hint`s (INV-ruluv; dispatch:F3 / INV-fahub family).** `process_code_block` recursed into Lambda / ListComp / SetComp / DictComp / GeneratorExp and directly-body-nested FunctionDef bodies with an UNPRUNED `var_types` — only `def`/`class` DIRECT children were skipped, and only in the child-descent loop. So `[b.size for b in items]` (with `b: Box` in the enclosing scope), `lambda c: c.val` (param `c` shadowing `c: C`), and a directly-nested `def inner(svc)` (shadowing `svc: Service`) all reused the outer type and emitted a resolved edge to the wrong method/getter.
