The producer now prunes `var_types` (and `external_var_types`) of the names a sub-scope BINDS before recursing into it — comprehension for-targets (recursively unpacking tuple/starred/nested) and all lambda parameter kinds — applied at scope ENTRY (per node), so a NESTED comprehension's inner target is pruned too (it arrives as a block node, not a child — the exact case an adversarial review showed a descent-site prune would miss). Enclosing-scope positions keep the outer type: a comprehension's first iterable, lambda default exprs, and free variables the sub-scope only reads; walrus (`:=`) targets are deliberately not pruned (PEP 572 binds them in the enclosing scope). The `def`/`class` skip is hoisted to the top of the block loop (mirroring `_emit_module_attr_refs._walk`), so a directly-body-nested def is processed only by its own walk with its own params — eliminating both the mis-typed edge and a duplicate emission. Surfaced by the WI-gubar (D2) adversarial review but PRE-EXISTING and shared with the `_process_call` Case 2c and WI-noham Part A producers. Behavioral evidence: `[conn.send() for conn in socks]` with `conn: Connection` no longer stamps `receiver_type_hint=Connection` on the comp-local `send()`; `[svc.handle(x) for x in items]` still stamps `Service` on the free `svc`. Mainstream package 100% coverage.
- **Nim and D external-import edges now emit a well-formed node id with the module name in the path slot, so imported modules are visible to `refine_frameworks` / `is_test_file` again — restoring framework detection that was silently suppressed (INV-lumuh Nim; INV-fihur D; joint fix).** Both analyzers emitted a malformed 4-slot dst `{lang}:?:{module}:module` — a `?` placeholder in the path slot and no span. `refine_frameworks` reads the imported module as `edge.dst.split(":")[1]` (the path slot), so it saw `?`; and the external-symbol synthesis (`_parse_dangling_id`, which needs 5 colon-parts) fell back to `module_path`/`src` = the `<unknown>` sentinel. Net effect: every Nim/D import was invisible to framework refinement, so jester/karax (Nim) and vibe.d (D) were false-demoted out of the detected framework list on real repos (nitter, dub). Both now emit the canonical `{lang}:{module}:0-0:module:module` form (mirroring py.py's `python:{module}:0-0:module:module`), putting the module in the `parts[1]` path slot. Behavioral evidence: `import jester` → `nim:jester:0-0:module:module` (was `nim:?:jester:module`); `import vibe.vibe;` → `d:vibe.vibe:0-0:module:module` (was `d:?:vibe.vibe:module`). Extended package 100% coverage.
- **Taint sanitizer registration now applies the same resolution-/kind-aware gate as source/sink matching, so a bare-name unresolved call no longer registers a phantom barrier that silently suppresses a real taint flow (INV-finoh; a flow-suppression false-negative).** Source/sink matching gates unresolved edges through `_match_propagation_entry`/`gate_named_entry` (an untyped `x.method()` with no receiver evidence never matches; a qualified callee's exact name wins; an ambiguous bare name is filtered — ADR-0037 ruling 4 / INV-tapat / INV-maluk), but `_register_sanitizer_callers` matched purely on the bare `callee_name`. So an unresolved `x.encrypt()` method call whose leaf name collides with a sanitizer (`encrypt` from `Fernet.encrypt`) registered a barrier and marked the caller sanitized — suppressing a real source→sink violation (a false negative, which for a security tool is worse than a missed barrier). Sanitizer registration now mirrors the reliably-derivable checks: a *resolved* edge trusts its resolution (unchanged); an *unresolved* edge passes only on an exact `qualified_name` match (receiver evidence), and a bare untyped *method* call (`call_construct == "method"`) or an ambiguous bare name is rejected. Over-rejection errs toward *reporting* the flow, the safe direction. (The `kind`-filter for a free-function call matching a method-kind sanitizer is a documented follow-up — the sanitizer YAML carries no explicit `kind`.) Behavioral evidence (`propagate_taint_structural`): a plaintext flow `handler→middle→write_text` where `middle` makes an unrelated unresolved `x.encrypt()` call is now correctly REPORTED (was: silently sanitized, 0 findings); the real qualified `Fernet.encrypt` sanitizer still blocks its flow. Core package 100% coverage.
- **The Nim analyzer now extracts EXPORTED procs/funcs/methods/types (`proc greet*`) instead of silently dropping every one of them (INV-bisom; 0/246 on the filed corpus).** Nim marks a symbol public with a `*` postfix, which the tree-sitter grammar wraps in an `exported_symbol` node (`identifier` + `*`) rather than exposing a bare `identifier` child. The four declaration processors resolved the name via `find_child_by_type(node, "identifier")`, which returns nothing for the wrapped form — so `proc greet*`, `func double*`, `method speak*`, and `type Person*` all hit the `if not name_node: return None` guard and vanished, while only non-exported (`proc internal`) symbols survived. A shared `_declared_name_node` helper now resolves the inner identifier in either shape and reports whether the `*` marker was present, so exported declarations are both extracted AND marked `is_exported=True` (mirroring go.py's lexical-case export rule); non-exported ones stay `is_exported=False`. Scope: the analyzer processes proc/func/method/type declarations — `template`/`iterator`/`const`/etc. are a separate pre-existing non-extraction, not part of this fix. Behavioral evidence (`analyze_nim` on a mixed module): `greet`/`double`/`speak`/`Person` now extracted with `is_exported=True`, `internal` extracted with `is_exported=False` (was: only `internal` extracted). Package remains at 100% coverage.
- **An inherited-/typed-receiver call no longer binds to a same-short-name class (or its method) in ANOTHER language — the `inherited_calls` linker now filters every receiver / enclosing-class / field-type name→class-id lookup to the caller's own language (INV-milud; dispatch:F3 / INV-fahub umbrella).** `class_ids_by_name` is a language-AGNOSTIC short-name index, so a Java `Handler` and a Python `Handler` collide on one key. The Java legacy-permissive Site-2 (`_LEGACY_SITE2_LANGS`), which deliberately skips the same-name ambiguity guard, would then bind a Java receiver to a Python namesake's method (Step-1 direct lookup) or type symbol (Step-3 fallback) — a confidently-wrong cross-language `calls` edge; and for the strict languages a foreign-language namesake **inflated the `len(...) > 1` ambiguity count**, suppressing a legitimate same-language resolution as a false-negative. MRO / typed-receiver dispatch never crosses a language boundary (that is the FFI/bridge linkers' job), so all four name→ids lookups (Site-1 `enclosing_class`, Site-2 `receiver_type_hint`, Site-3 `enclosing_class` + field-type) now route through one `_same_language_class_ids` chokepoint that restricts candidates to `src_lang` — not a per-Site sweep. The analogous `type_hierarchy` dispatch is ID-keyed from the (within-language) inheritance graph and was already immune, so no sibling fix was needed. Production-path evidence (synthetic graphs through `link_inherited_calls`): a Java `obj.save()` with a Python `Shared.save` listed first now falls back to the Java `Shared` type instead of binding the Python method (the Site-1 bare-call and Site-3 inherited-field variants likewise stay same-language); and a Python `w.render()` with exactly one in-tree `Widget` now resolves despite a Java `Widget` namesake that previously over-suppressed it. Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **A Python non-call attribute read on an imported in-tree module constant (`import pkg.config as cfg; cfg.CONFIG`) now emits a `references` edge to the real in-tree variable instead of a phantom workspace-prefixed `module_attr_ref` external (WI-huhum; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas call/attr residual).** `_emit_module_attr_refs` minted an unconditional phantom `python:{module}:0-0:{module}.{attr}:external_symbol` (→ a `hypergumbo_*`-prefixed `external_symbol` post-remap) for every `<module-alias>.<attr>` read on an imported module — the largest chunk (52 nodes) of INV-nuzas's remaining acceptance-property failure. When the imported module is **in-tree** and the attribute resolves to a first-party module-level **`kind="variable"`**, the producer now emits a `references`/`ast_name_read` edge to the real variable (mirroring `_emit_variable_refs`' bare-name module-variable read). Three INV-fahub-critical design choices: (1) **exact** module match (`global_symbols.get((real_module, attr))`), *not* the suffix-matching `_lookup_symbol_by_module` — on this imported-module surface a suffix match would bind an external `import json as j; j.X` to a coincidentally same-suffixed in-tree `pkg/json.py` (mirrors WI-hotug CASE A); (2) edge type `references`, **not** `module_attr_ref` — the latter *is* in `TAINT_CALL_EDGE_TYPES`, so retargeting it to a live in-tree node would inject a new taint frontier; `references` is not, and an adversarial before/after diff confirmed the Python taint findings are **byte-identical** (the 52 phantoms matched zero `attributes:`-catalog stdlib IO primitives, so they carried no taint); (3) a scope-shadow guard so a param/local rebinding the alias stays phantom. The shadow guard deliberately **excludes plain `import X as Y` aliases** (`_collect_local_bindings(..., include_import_aliases=False)`): a function-local `import pkg.mod as m; m.CONST` — the dominant self-corpus shape (a test method importing then reading) — is the alias to resolve, not a shadow of it (only params, assignments, and `from`-import value rebinds shadow). The walk is also **scope-bounded** (skips nested `def`/`class` bodies): a raw `ast.walk` descends into nested scopes and, under the retarget, would mint a confidently-wrong *resolved* edge attributed to the enclosing function or over a nested param shadow — nested reads are emitted, correctly attributed, by their own passes. Behavioral evidence (self-analysis): `module_attr_ref`-reached phantom `hypergumbo_*` externals **52 → 18**; total INV-nuzas workspace phantoms **58 → 24**. Coherence-neutral (un-allow-listed offenders 28, unchanged — `references`→`variable` is an existing partition). The residual 18 are `kind=function`/dunder/re-export targets the variable-only guard correctly skips; the enclosing-scope closure-capture and module-scope-rebind shadow gaps (contrived, and shared with the sibling `_emit_variable_refs`) are tracked as a follow-up (WI-luhah). Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **A Python call to a function re-exported through a *non-package* facade module (`from facade import fn; fn()`) now resolves to the real in-tree function instead of dead-ending at a phantom workspace-prefixed `external_symbol` (WI-hotug CASE A / PR#2; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas call-edge residual).** The re-export alias pass that lets `from package import helper` resolve to the submodule's real symbol ran **only for `__init__.py`**, so a plain facade module (`compact.py` doing `from .tier import parse_tier_spec`, or `sketch.py` re-exporting `estimate_tokens`) got no alias — a cross-module `from compact import parse_tier_spec; parse_tier_spec()` missed `_lookup_symbol_by_module(compact, parse_tier_spec)` and minted a `python:hypergumbo_core.compact:0-0:parse_tier_spec:external_symbol` phantom, violating INV-nuzas's acceptance property. A new pass now generalizes re-export resolution to non-package modules, but deliberately by **EXACT module match** (`global_symbols.get((resolved_module, name))`), *not* the suffix-matching `_lookup_symbol_by_module` the `__init__` pass uses: suffix matching is the src-layout affordance a package `__init__` needs, but on the newly-opened non-package surface it would let a facade's `from json import dumps` re-export bind to a coincidentally same-suffixed in-tree `pkg/json.py` — a confidently-wrong resolution the adversarial review flagged as an **INV-fahub** violation. Exact match resolves the 10 intra-repo re-exports (all exact) and fails **safe** (to a phantom) on any external-shadowing or unresolvable case. A single `(module_name, local_name) in global_symbols` guard does double duty — a locally-**defined** same-name symbol (pre-registered by the build loop) wins over the re-export, and an alias added in a prior pass is not re-processed — inside a bounded fixed point (cap 5) that chases N-hop re-export chains; the `__init__.py` pass (including its `re_exported` modifier, a package-surface signal consumed by `visibility.py` / `library-exports.yaml`) is left **byte-identical**, so the modifier stays `__init__`-only and no existing `__init__` re-export / src-layout behavior changes. **Coherence-neutral**: the 10 redirected `calls` edges move from the `(function|method, python, external_symbol, python)` partition to `(…, function, python)` — both already carry `calls`, so the self-analysis un-allow-listed offender count is unchanged (28) and the self-tree ratchet gate stays green. Behavioral evidence (hypergumbo self-analysis): phantom `hypergumbo_*` `external_symbol` nodes **68 → 58**, `calls`-reached phantoms **16 → 6** (the 10 imported-function re-exports resolve; the residual 6 = 5 `scip_pb2` submodule-receivers + 1 `copy`, both deferred). **Scope — this is CASE A** (imported-function re-exports); the `module_attr_ref` sub-shape (WI-huhum, 52 nodes) and the scip_pb2/class-receiver cases remain separate follow-ups, so WI-hotug stays a partial INV-nuzas close. Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **A Python method call on an imported module-level *constant* (`from x import CONST; CONST.items()`) now emits a `references` edge to the in-tree variable instead of a phantom workspace-prefixed `external_symbol` (WI-hotug CASE B; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas call-edge residual).** `CONFIG_FILES_BY_LANG.items()` / `LANGUAGE_ALIASES.get(...)` — a builtin dict/list/regex method call on an in-tree constant imported via `from x import CONST` — fell to the `elif receiver_name in imports` emit in `_process_call`, which built `python:{module}:0-0:{CONST}.{method}:unresolved` (→ a `hypergumbo_*`-prefixed `external_symbol` boundary node post-WI-pubiv-remap), violating INV-nuzas's acceptance property (no `external_symbol` may carry a workspace prefix). The `.items`/`.get`/`.values` suffix is a **builtin** with no in-tree target, but the **receiver** is a real first-party symbol — so the producer now resolves the receiver via the import-anchored `_lookup_symbol_by_module(global_symbols, module, original_name)` (with `allow_ambiguous=False` → biases to unresolved on a same-name **module** collision, **INV-fahub**) and, when it is an in-tree `kind="variable"`, emits a `references`→variable edge (`evidence_type="ast_name_read"`, `is_resolved=True`) — the caller *uses* the constant. A class receiver (handled by the existing classmethod branch), a submodule receiver, or an external/None lookup all fall through to the unchanged phantom emit. The whole branch is additionally **scope-guarded** (`receiver_name not in _caller_locals`, mirroring the sibling terminal-else and `_emit_variable_refs`): `imports` is the file-level from-import map, so a function **parameter or local rebind** that shadows a same-named imported constant (`def h(settings): settings.get(...)` over `from x import settings`) would otherwise mint a confidently-wrong resolved `references`→module-constant edge (the receiver is the local, not the import — an under-determined bind the adversarial review reproduced); a shadowed receiver instead routes to the honest non-workspace `python:external:0-0:<attr>:unresolved`. **Coherence-safe** (ADR-0023 §3): `references` already dominant-carries the `(function|method, python, variable, python)` partitions, so the shrink-only ratchet gains no offender (self-analysis un-allow-listed offenders **33 → 33**). **Taint-neutral**: `references` is not in `TAINT_CALL_EDGE_TYPES`, and the removed `calls` edges targeted non-I/O workspace symbols. Behavioral evidence (hypergumbo self-analysis): phantom `hypergumbo_*` `external_symbol` nodes **107 → 68** (the 38 variable-receiver shapes resolve to `references`; the scope-shadow guard routes one more shadowed-receiver phantom to a non-workspace external). **Scope — this is CASE B** of WI-hotug (a *partial* INV-nuzas close); the imported-function re-export case (CASE A, ~10 nodes) and the `module_attr_ref`/scip_pb2/class-receiver sub-shapes are separate follow-ups. Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **Python `Class.staticmethod()`/`classmethod()` and typed-receiver `obj.method()` calls now resolve to the in-repo method via a `receiver_type_hint` the inherited_calls linker consumes — closing part of the dead-code false positives where production methods appeared uncalled (WI-noham Part A; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** A method call whose receiver type py.py could not resolve *directly* (a bare local class name like `Foo.bar()`, which had no analyzer case at all; or a `var_types`-typed receiver whose method lives cross-file / on an ancestor) fell to the terminal unresolved emit `python:external:0-0:<attr>:unresolved` with **no hint**, so the already-validated `inherited_calls` linker (which recovers 68 Java + 218 Ruby edges) recovered **zero** Python method calls. `py.py`'s final unresolved-emit `else` now *additively* stamps `meta["receiver_type_hint"]` when the receiver's type is inferred — from the per-function `var_types` map (`var_types[recv].name`; flow-insensitive last-write-wins, the same imprecision Case 2c already carries), or a bare local class name (`local_symbols[recv].kind == "class"`) — leaving the edge `is_resolved=False` with an unchanged dst; the linker is the sole minter of the resolved `calls` edge (**taint-safe by construction** — the analyzer never flips `is_resolved`, so the WI-pubiv→WI-jopil regression class cannot arm here). The local-class branch is **scope-guarded** (INV-fahub): it is suppressed when `receiver_name` is bound in the caller's own scope, because the file-global `local_symbols` table would otherwise resolve a function-local variable or unannotated **parameter shadowing a module-level class** (`def handle(raw): Response = parse(raw); return Response.get(...)`) to that class — an under-determined receiver. The guard reuses the caller's materialized `Scope.local_names` (and a module frame carries module-level rebound names for the module-scope twin), mirroring `_emit_variable_refs`'s shadow suppression. To honor **INV-fahub** (an under-determined receiver MUST NOT bind to an arbitrary same-named internal def), the linker's Site-2 resolution gains a **strict mode** for every language *except* the legacy-permissive `_LEGACY_SITE2_LANGS = {java}` (which keeps its INV-nilud-validated first-match + type-symbol fallback): the Step-3 type-symbol fallback is **gated off** (no `calls→class` edge — no new runtime_coherence partition), and a same-name-class collision (`len(type_class_ids) > 1`) **biases to unresolved** rather than binding by first match. `_LEGACY_SITE2_LANGS` is deliberately decoupled from `_MRO_WALKERS` (`{ruby, groovy, java}`), so landing a Python MRO walker (deferred) cannot silently re-enable the fallback. An untyped / duck receiver gets **no hint** (bias to unresolved). Built additively on the scope-stack substrate (identity:F1/F4a). **Scope — this is Part A**, closing the walker-independent recall (local static/classmethod + typed-receiver-direct residual, via linker Step-1); the flagship `@property` read false positives (`Symbol.end_line`, `ValidationResult.ok` — attribute *reads*, not calls), the inherited-method mass (needs a Python MRO walker), and concrete-class-id precision are **deferred follow-ups**, so WI-noham stays open. Behavioral evidence: `Foo.bar()` for a local class with `@staticmethod def bar()` now emits a resolved `calls` edge to `Foo.bar` via `ast_call_type_inferred` (was `:unresolved`); a `def f(x): x.method()` untyped receiver still emits no hint; a local var / unannotated param shadowing a class (`Response = parse(raw); Response.get(...)`) emits no hint (stays unresolved); two same-named `Foo` classes leave `r.bar()` unresolved. Both packages remain at 100% coverage. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Python `@property` attribute reads (`obj.prop`) now resolve to the in-repo getter, closing the flagship dead-code false positives where property getters appeared unread (WI-gubar; WI-noham Part B / D2; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** A `@property` access `obj.prop` is an `ast.Attribute` in **Load** context, not an `ast.Call`, so `_process_call` never saw it and no edge was emitted — the getter (`Symbol.end_line`, `ValidationResult.ok`) looked like dead code even though callers read it constantly. A new branch in `process_code_block` — where the flow-sensitive `var_types` map lives — emits, for a **`var_types`-typed instance** receiver (read inside a **function/method body**; module-scope reads are excluded so a `file`-kind src never mints a `calls` edge into the `imports`-only `(file, python, external_symbol, python)` runtime_coherence partition) whose target attribute resolves to a `@property` getter, an **unresolved `calls` edge** `python:external:0-0:<attr>:unresolved` carrying `meta["receiver_type_hint"]`, exactly mirroring the WI-noham Part A method producer; the `inherited_calls` linker then mints the resolved `calls`→getter edge via strict Site-2 Step-1 (the getter is `kind="method"` with a `Class.attr` name, so `build_method_index` already finds it — **no linker change needed**). The getter gate (`_resolve_property_getter`) resolves the target via the full-pipeline `(path, Class.attr)` index (or, for single-file `extract_nodes`, a short-name hit whose qualified name matches the class — guarding same-short-name collisions) and requires `kind=="method"` **plus** a `meta["decorators"]` entry named exactly `"property"` (a getter, not a `@x.setter` whose recorded name is the dotted `"x.setter"`), so a plain data field, a non-property method, an untyped receiver, a Store-context write (`obj.prop = x`), and a **bare-CLASS** receiver (`ClassName.prop` yields the descriptor, not the value) all emit **nothing**. Because only a per-function-scope-local `var_types` instance emits, the Part A file-global-`local_symbols` bare-class shadow hazard does not arise — **no INV-fahub scope guard is needed** (the exact defect Part A's adversarial review caught is structurally absent here). `call_construct="method"` keeps the unresolved edge suppressed at the taint **sink** gate (`gate_named_entry` method-kind), the edge stays `is_resolved=False` (the linker is the sole minter), and both endpoints land in existing runtime_coherence partitions (`…→unresolved` then `method→method`), so no new offender. A shared `_collect_call_func_attr_ids` helper (extracted from `_emit_module_attr_refs`, DRY) skips an Attribute that is a call's own callee (`obj.method()`), preventing a double-emit. **Scope — this is Part B**, clearing the flagship **read-only** `@property`-read cohort (Part A's deferred item); the inherited-method mass (needs a Python MRO walker, WI-hiziz/D1), concrete-class-id precision (WI-supat/D3), and read-write (getter + `@x.setter`) properties (the setter's last-write in `global_symbols` collapses the getter symbol — a safe no-emit, tracked follow-up) remain deferred, so WI-noham stays open until D1 lands. Behavioral evidence: `def consume(b: Box): return b.size` with `Box.size` a `@property` now emits a resolved `calls` edge to `Box.size` (was no edge); a plain method read `c.regular`, an untyped `def f(c): c.val`, and `c.val = 5` all emit nothing; `c.method()` is not double-emitted. Both packages remain at 100% coverage. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Python calls to an INHERITED method on a typed receiver now resolve to the ancestor's method via a true C3-linearization MRO walker — the dominant remaining dead-code false-positive cohort where inherited production methods appeared uncalled (WI-hiziz; WI-noham D1; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; awaits bakeoff validation).** The `inherited_calls` linker's Site-2 Step-2 (MRO walk) was a no-op for Python — `python` was absent from `_MRO_WALKERS` — so a typed receiver (`x: Sub`) calling a method defined only on an ancestor (`x.base_method()`; the `receiver_type_hint` already emitted by WI-noham Part A) never resolved, and the ancestor method looked dead. A new `_walk_c3` walker computes Python's **C3 linearization** (`L[C] = [C] + merge(L[bases…], [bases])`) over the in-tree `extends` chain and returns the first ancestor defining the method. C3 is required, not the Ruby/Groovy insertion-order BFS: the two agree on single inheritance and even diamonds but **diverge on uneven-depth diamonds** — for `E(C, D)`, `C(B)`, `B(A)`, `D(A)` where both `B` and `D` define the method, CPython's MRO is `[E, C, B, D, A]` (resolves to `B`), while depth-order BFS would pick `D` (a confidently-wrong ancestor). Registered for `python` in `_MRO_WALKERS` only; deliberately **not** added to `_LEGACY_SITE2_LANGS`, so Python keeps strict Site-2 (no Step-3 `calls→class` fallback — no new runtime_coherence partition — and same-name-class collisions still bias to unresolved), and the resolution is a genuine `calls→method` MRO hit (`ast_call_inherited_method` @ 0.70). Two adversarial-review-caught defects were fixed structurally: (a) **base order** — C3's left-to-right precedence requires source base order, but a *qualified* in-tree base (`class D(mod.Bar, Mixin)`) has its `extends` edge recovered late by the inheritance-linker and arrives out of order, so the linker now reorders each Python class's bases from its `meta["base_classes"]` (source order) before walking, correcting `D(base.Bar, Baz).m()` from `Baz.m` (wrong) to `Bar.m`; (b) **depth/cycle** — an un-linearizable hierarchy (inheritance cycle, or a chain past the depth cap where a truncated branch would silently reorder ancestors) now **biases to unresolved** (`_linearize_c3` returns `None`) rather than emitting a best-effort, possibly-wrong order. Known scope caveat (shared with every intra-repo-only walker, tracked as INV-guviv): an EXTERNAL base (`dict`/`Enum`/3rd-party) ahead of the in-tree ancestor in the real MRO is invisible to the walk and can yield a wrong-positive resolution. Behavioral evidence: `class Sub(Base)` with `Base.save` and `def f(x: Sub): x.save()` now emits a resolved `calls` edge to `Base.save` (was `:unresolved`); the uneven-diamond and reversed-qualified-base cases resolve to the C3-correct ancestor end-to-end. Scope — this is D1 (the walker + Site-2 inherited); py.py `self.method()` (Site-1) and `self.field.method()` (Site-3) inherited-hint emission are the remaining WI-hiziz pieces, so WI-noham stays open. Both packages remain at 100% coverage. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Python `self.method()` calls to a cross-file INHERITED method now resolve to the ancestor's method via a Site-1 `enclosing_class` hint the inherited_calls linker walks up the class's C3 MRO — closing the dead-code false positives where a production method inherited from a cross-file base looked unreachable (WI-hiziz D1 / PR-2; WI-noham; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** A `self.method()` call whose short name py.py's Case 2a could not resolve in the file — an inherited method whose definition lives on a **cross-file** ancestor (a same-file ancestor's method is fortuitously intercepted by Case 2a's file-global short-name lookup) — fell to the terminal unresolved emit `python:external:0-0:<attr>:unresolved` with **no hint**, so Site-1 resolution (already live for Java/Ruby) stayed inert for Python even after D1/PR-1 activated the Python C3 walker. `py.py`'s final unresolved-emit `else` now *additively* stamps `meta["enclosing_class"]` — the caller method's **direct** enclosing class short name (`caller_symbol.qualified_name.split(".")[-2]`; a nested `Inner.method` yields `Inner`) — for a `self.method()` call, as a **leading mutually-exclusive `elif`** ahead of the `receiver_type_hint` branches so an annotated `def m(self: Base)` dispatches to Site-1 (`enclosing_class`) rather than double-stamping and mis-routing to Site-2 (which `_try_resolve` checks first). Four load-bearing guards keep the hint sound: `caller_symbol.kind == "method"` (a **crash guard** — guarantees the dotted `qualified_name` so the split cannot `IndexError` on a module-level function or a nested-function `self`, whose class is unrecoverable); `receiver_name not in var_types` (an explicit `def m(self: T)` annotation is a deliberate static-type declaration whose methods may live **off** the enclosing class's MRO — the mixin/host idiom — so it routes to Site-2 on `T` via the demoted `elif`, not Site-1, avoiding a lost edge or a confidently-wrong 0.90 namesake bind); `"self" in _caller_locals` (excludes a classmethod referencing `self` and a `@staticmethod` with no `self` param — an undefined receiver); and `"staticmethod" not in caller decorators` (excludes a `@staticmethod` that *declares* a `self` param, whose `self` is an arbitrary argument, not an instance). The edge stays `is_resolved=False` with an unchanged dst (**taint-safe by construction** — the linker is the sole minter). The linker's Site-1 resolver (`_resolve_site1`) gains an **INV-fahub ambiguity guard** scoped to a dedicated `_SITE1_STRICT_LANGS = {python}` set (NOT "all but Java"): for Python, whose module namespaces make two in-tree classes of the same short name genuinely **distinct**, a `len(start_class_ids) > 1` collision **biases to unresolved** rather than walking whichever namesake the loop hits first — a recall sacrifice (including cross-language namesakes, since `class_ids_by_name` is language-agnostic) that WI-supat (D3) recovers by threading a concrete enclosing-class id. **Ruby/Groovy are deliberately excluded** from the guard: in a single-global-namespace language two same-short-name classes are a class *reopening* (one logical class — ubiquitous in Rails), so guarding them would suppress the INV-nilud-validated `X.new → inherited #initialize` Site-1 resolution this linker was built to preserve; they keep loop-all-first-match, as does legacy-permissive Java. Known scope caveat (shared with every intra-repo-only walker, tracked as INV-guviv, now **more reachable** because PR-2 aims Site-1 at the common framework-subclass `self.method()` shape): an EXTERNAL base ahead of the in-tree ancestor in the real MRO is invisible to the walk and can yield a wrong-positive. Behavioral evidence: two files where `class Sub(Base)` calls `self.helper()` and `Base.helper` lives cross-file now emit a resolved `calls` edge `Sub.run → Base.helper` (was `:unresolved`); an annotated `def run(self: Widget)` resolves `self.render()` to `Widget.render` via Site-2 (not the empty Site-1 walk of the enclosing class); a `@staticmethod` (with or without a `self` param) calling `self.x()`, a classmethod referencing `self`, a module-level `def f(self): self.g()`, a nested-function `self.x()`, and two same-named Python classes all bias to unresolved; a reopened Ruby class still resolves. Scope — this is D1's Site-1 piece (PR-2); `self.field.method()` (Site-3) and concrete-class-id precision (WI-supat/D3) remain, so WI-noham stays open. Both packages remain at 100% coverage. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Python `self.field.method()` calls where `field` is an INHERITED (cross-file parent) instance field now resolve to the field type's method — the dependency-injection dead-code false positives where a service injected on a base class appeared unused (WI-hiziz D1 / PR-3; WI-noham; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** A `self.svc.process()` call whose field `svc` is declared in a **parent's** `__init__` (not the child's) was absent from the child method's `var_types` (only a class's OWN typed fields are merged), so Case 2f missed it and — because `func.value` is an `ast.Attribute` (`self.svc`), not a `Name` — **no edge was emitted at all**. Three coordinated changes land Site-3 for Python: **(a)** each class symbol now carries `meta["fields"] = {field: type_short_name}` (from the existing `__init__` field-type scan, mirroring `java.py`) — the single source of truth the linker's `_walk_parents_for_field` reads; **(b)** `py.py`'s unresolved-emit adds a `self.field.method()` branch (a dedicated `elif` ahead of the WI-zigah multi-segment path) stamping `meta["inherited_field_receiver"]` + `meta["enclosing_class"]`, which `inherited_calls._resolve_site3` walks the enclosing class's **parents** for the field's type and resolves the method on it (`ast_call_inherited_field` @ 0.80); **(c)** `_resolve_site3` gains the two **INV-fahub ambiguity guards** (on the enclosing-class name and the field-type name) that Site-1/Site-2 carry, scoped to `_SITE1_STRICT_LANGS = {python}` (Java/Ruby stay permissive). The emit is guarded to bias to unresolved for every under-determined receiver: the four Site-1 guards (`kind == "method"`; unannotated `self`; `self` a bound local; not a `@staticmethod`), plus an **own-field exclusion** — `field_name` in neither `var_types` **nor** a new `class_own_field_names` set (every `__init__` `self.X` target name, including an untyped/factory `self.f = make_conn()` that the typed-only `var_types` misses). The own-field exclusion prevents the shadow false positive where a child re-declares a parent field name with a **different** type (an own field is never inherited). The edge stays `is_resolved=False` (taint-safe; the linker is the sole minter). Designed + adversarially reviewed via Workflow; the review caught and fixed the own-field under-exclusion (a factory-assigned own field would else mint a confidently-wrong edge to a same-named parent field's type), a vacuous shadow-safety test, and a spurious `meta["fields"]` attach to a same-name method (now gated on `kind == "class"`). Known residuals (tracked follow-ups): an uneven-depth Python diamond with the same field name declared at different depths with divergent types (the parent-field walk is BFS, not C3-ordered), a same-name-class `meta["fields"]` clobber (recall loss), and `AnnAssign` (`self.x: T`) own fields not yet in the own-field set. Behavioral evidence: cross-file `class Sub(Base)` where `Base.__init__(self, log: Logger)` sets `self.log` and `Sub.run` calls `self.log.info()` now emits a resolved `calls` edge `Sub.run → Logger.info` (was no edge); a child re-declaring `self.handle = make_conn()` over an inherited `handle: Logger` emits no wrong edge. Both packages remain at 100% coverage; taint merge-gate + runtime_coherence ratchet unaffected. Scope — this closes WI-hiziz's Site-3 piece; WI-noham's remaining residual is WI-supat/D3 (concrete-class-id precision). Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Python inherited-call resolution now threads a concrete, authoritative class id through the `inherited_calls` Site meta contract, recovering the recall the same-short-name ambiguity guards deliberately sacrificed — a same-short-name or cross-language namesake collision that previously biased to unresolved now resolves precisely (WI-supat / D3 part A; WI-noham; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** The Site-1/2/3 hints carried only a class *name*, which the linker re-globalized via the language-agnostic `class_ids_by_name`; for Python (`_SITE1_STRICT_LANGS`/strict Site-2) a `len(...) > 1` namesake collision — two in-tree classes sharing a short name, **or** a Python class and a same-named class in another language — biased to unresolved (INV-fahub-airtight but recall-lossy). The producer already holds the concrete receiver/enclosing `Symbol`, so `py.py` now *additively* stamps a concrete id alongside each name hint: `meta["enclosing_class_id"]` (Site-1 `self.method()` + Site-3 `self.field.method()`) and `meta["receiver_type_id"]` (the three Site-2 producers — `var_types`-typed receiver, bare local class `Foo.bar()`, and `@property` read). `inherited_calls` then *prefers* a concrete id — validated as a real `class_symbols` key **of the caller's own language** (`class_symbols[id].language == src_lang`) — as the single candidate, **skipping the `len(...) > 1` guard**; an absent / stale / foreign-language id falls back to the exact pre-WI-supat name+guard path (byte-identical, so every existing `*_biases_unresolved` test stays green). **The enclosing-class id is sound by construction, not by luck:** the design workflow's adversarial audit caught that sourcing it from the bare-name `local_symbols.get(short_name)` is **unsound** — that table is last-write-wins across all classes including nested ones, so a top-level `Inner` shadowed by a nested `Outer.Inner` (or sibling nested `A.Shared`/`B.Shared`) resolves to the **wrong** namesake, and since a wrong-but-real id passes `id in class_symbols`, the guard-skip would mint a *confidently-wrong* 0.90/0.80 edge up the wrong class's MRO — **strictly worse** than the safe bias-to-unresolved it replaced. So `enclosing_class_id` instead comes from a new **authoritative `method_symbol.id → enclosing_class_symbol.id` map** built at method creation (where both symbols are lexically in hand — clobber-immune, exact for nested/duplicate/sibling names), threaded through `FileAnalysis`; and the `receiver_type_id` (whose value traces back to the same clobber-prone name inference) is stamped **only when it is trustworthy** — the type's short name is unique among in-file `ClassDef`s (a per-file name count) **and** the resolved type is not import-shadowed (an in-file `class Foo` rebound by a later `from x import Foo` — Python last-binding-wins — would make the local-first-resolved type the wrong class; the adversarial review reproduced a confidently-wrong `f → LocalBase.compute` from exactly this self-shadow before the guard). The import check is *precise*, not blanket: a correctly cross-file-resolved imported type keeps its id, preserving the cross-file collision-recovery. Otherwise the id is omitted so the linker keeps the safe name path. The genuine recall win — the **cross-file** namesake (two files each defining `Foo`, project-wide `len == 2`) — is recovered unambiguously because a file-local concrete id names exactly one class. Taint-safe (the producer only adds meta keys; the edge stays `is_resolved=False`, the linker is the sole minter); the Site-2 Step-3 `calls→class` type-symbol fallback stays gated off for Python (the id selects *which* type, never re-opens the runtime_coherence partition). Behavioral evidence: two same-short-name Python `Worker` classes where one inherits `run` from a cross-file `Base` — a `self.run()` carrying `enclosing_class_id` now resolves to `Base.run` (was `:unresolved` under the guard); a Python `Config` + a Java `Config` no longer over-suppress the Python call; a `receiver_type_id` pointing at the wrong same-name twin (or a foreign-language id, or a stale id) stays unresolved. Scope — **this is part A** (edge-meta enclosing/receiver ids, Site-1/Site-2/Site-3-enclosing); the Site-3 field-**type** id (recovering the field-type-name ambiguity guard via a parallel `meta["field_type_ids"]` on the parent class symbol) is part B, so WI-supat and WI-noham stay open. Both packages remain at 100% coverage; taint merge-gate + runtime_coherence ratchet unaffected. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **The Site-3 inherited-field resolver now disambiguates a same-short-name field *TYPE* precisely via a concrete field-type id, completing WI-supat and closing WI-noham's dispatch:F3 residual (WI-supat / D3 part B; WI-noham; dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas residual; INV-fahub umbrella; awaits bakeoff validation).** Part A recovered the enclosing-class and receiver-type ambiguity guards; the third Site-3 guard — the *field-type* name (`self.field.method()` where the field's type name resolves to two in-tree classes) — remained a name-only re-globalization that biased to unresolved. Because the field's type is discovered *inside* the linker (by `_walk_parents_for_field` reading the parent class's `meta["fields"]`), the concrete id must ride the **parent class symbol's meta**, not the call edge: `py.py`'s `__init__` field-type scan now attaches a parallel `meta["field_type_ids"] = {field: type_id}` alongside the existing `meta["fields"] = {field: type_name}`, per-field gated by the **same** `_receiver_type_id_trustworthy` check as the Site-2 receiver id (file-unique type name **and** not import-shadowed — the field-type inference is the same bare-name local-first resolution, so it carries the same clobber and self-shadow hazards). `_walk_parents_for_field` now returns a `(type_name, type_id_or_None)` 2-tuple on a hit (bare `None` on a miss, preserving the depth-cap / cycle negatives), and `_resolve_site3` prefers a validated, same-language `field_type_id` as the single candidate — **skipping the field-type `len(...) > 1` guard** — falling back to the name+guard path when the id is absent (a **java / legacy** parent carries only `meta["fields"]`, so its `(name, None)` keeps Java Site-3 byte-identical), stale, or foreign-language. Behavioral evidence: a `class Sub(Base)` where `Base` declares `log: Logger` and **two** in-tree `Logger` classes exist — `self.log.info()` now resolves to the correct `Logger.info` via `field_type_ids` (was `:unresolved` under the guard); the wrong-namesake id, a stale id, and a foreign-language id all stay unresolved; a two-`Database`-in-one-file collision omits the ambiguous `field_type_ids` entry at the producer. With WI-hiziz (D1) + WI-gubar (D2) + WI-gulot + WI-supat (D3, parts A+B) all landed, **WI-noham's receiver-blind dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas 283-twin residual is now closed** (pending a bakeoff re-measure). Both packages remain at 100% coverage; taint merge-gate + runtime_coherence ratchet unaffected. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Path relativization now also rewrites ID-embedding values in `Edge.meta` and `dict`-shaped `Symbol.meta` values — the fix that actually makes WI-supat's concrete-class-id collision-recovery fire end-to-end (dispatch:F3 / INV-nuzas; INV-pohik symptom-2 family).** `_relativize_ir_paths` (finalize sub-step 1) rewrites absolute `repo_root`-prefixed paths to repo-relative in symbol ids, edge `src`/`dst`, and string `Symbol.meta` values (the route `handler_ref` case) — but it **ran before the linkers and did NOT touch `Edge.meta` at all, nor `dict`-shaped `Symbol.meta` values.** WI-supat threads concrete class ids as `enclosing_class_id`/`receiver_type_id` on **edge** meta and `field_type_ids` (`{field: id}`) as a **dict** on class-symbol meta; the `inherited_calls` linker compares them against the *relativized* `class_symbols` index. So every WI-supat id kept its stale absolute form, missed the index, and **silently fell back to the name path — the concrete-id recovery never fired in the full pipeline** (only in linker unit tests with synthetic matching ids; the negative e2e tests passed regardless via that same name-path fallback, masking it). `_relativize_ir_paths` now relativizes edge-meta strings and one level of meta-`dict` values via a shared helper, exactly as it already did for symbol-meta strings. Behavioral evidence (end-to-end via `run_behavior_map`, the regression these tests now lock): with two same-name `Logger` classes, `Sub.run`'s `self.log.info()` resolves to the correct `log1.Logger.info` (`field_type_ids` recovered); with two same-name `Worker` classes, `w1.Worker.run`'s `self.greet()` resolves to the cross-file `BaseA.greet` (`enclosing_class_id` recovered) — both were unresolved before the fix. This also **retroactively activates the already-merged WI-supat part A ids** (whose e2e recall had been inert for the same reason). Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **A Python module-level function alias (`f = g`) now resolves calls through to the real function body instead of dead-ending at the `kind=variable` alias node (WI-gulot; dispatch:F3 / INV-pohik).** A module-level rebinding like `_is_test_path = is_test_path` is extracted as a `kind=variable` symbol, so a caller of `_is_test_path(...)` produced a `calls` edge to that variable — a 0-out-degree dead-end — and the real function appeared *uncalled*, silently stopping reachability, impact analysis, and slice expansion at the alias. `py.py` now builds a module-scope alias map (`f -> target function`, resolving the RHS via local symbols **or imports**, so the common cross-file `from x import f; _f = f` form works) and, when a bare call's callee resolves to such an alias variable, retargets the `calls` edge to the real function (so the target is genuinely `calls`-reachable — the filed repro's expectation). Scoped to **module-level** assignments (`tree.body`), not `ast.walk`, so a function-local `f = g` cannot pollute a same-named module variable's resolution (verified: a caller of a module `p == 5` is never misresolved to a function-local `p = g`). Built additively on the scope-stack substrate (identity:F1/F4a). Behavioral evidence: for `_is_test_path = is_test_path` with a `caller()` calling it, the `calls` edge now targets `is_test_path` (was the variable) and `is_test_path` is genuinely called; the cross-file imported-alias form resolves through the full pipeline. Both packages remain at 100% coverage.
- **Taint analysis reads the resolution verdict from `Edge.is_resolved`, not the `:unresolved` dst-string suffix — ADR-0037 ruling 4 (WI-jopil), fixing a latent regression WI-pubiv introduced.** WI-pubiv's boundary-id remap rewrote unresolved external edges' dst suffix from `:unresolved` → `:external_symbol` on the final graph. Three consumers re-derived resolution status by string-matching `:unresolved` — `taint._match_propagation_entry`, `taint_refine._maybe_rewrite_edge`, and the `cgo` linker — the "consumer re-derives the producer verdict" anti-pattern (ADR-0037 cross-family mechanism #3). Post-remap that suffix is gone, so `verify-claims` taint treated **every** unresolved external edge as *resolved*: `_match_propagation_entry` short-circuited to an exact-name match, **bypassing the WI-razol/INV-tapat ambiguous-name + method-kind collision guard** (a false-positive `host_fs`/`network` taint flow on any ambiguous bare call such as `replace`/`get`), and `_maybe_rewrite_edge` became a silent no-op (its `# pragma: no cover` "external edges always carry the unresolved kind" assumption was falsified). All three now read `Edge.is_resolved` (threaded into `_match_propagation_entry` from the edge dict; `make_unresolved_edge` already sets `is_resolved=False`, and the cgo func-name extraction is now robust to either suffix). The unit tests passed pre-fix only because their fixtures used the pre-remap `:unresolved` dst; the production post-remap path was broken — a new regression test locks the `:external_symbol`-suffix routing. **This completes ADR-0037** (rulings 1/2/3/5 shipped earlier; the id-slot *output* fold was discharged by WI-pubiv + the remap, so the producer suffix is retained deliberately as an internal pre-remap convention).
- **Python route `usage_contexts` now carry a single `http_method` string per context (one context per declared method), matching every other language and spec §9 (WI-kohav).** The Flask/FastAPI `add_url_rule` / `add_api_route` and Starlette `Route` extractors emitted one `UsageContext` with `metadata["methods"] = [list]`, while the spec (`usage_contexts[].metadata`) documents a single `http_method` **string** and every other language's route extractor (rust/ruby/elixir/php/swift/js) already emits that form — so Python was the sole deviant, and a consumer reading the documented `metadata["http_method"]` got a `KeyError` (0/N route contexts carried the key). The two producers now emit **one `UsageContext` per declared method** with `metadata["http_method"] = <method>` (the `methods` list key is dropped); the sole internal consumer (the Starlette route-symbol minter, the only reader of `metadata["methods"]` in the tree) reads the string. A multi-method route (`methods=["POST","PUT"]`) now yields **two** usage_contexts (one per method) instead of one with a two-element list — matching the ruby/elixir convention; the minted route symbols (already one per method) are unchanged. `framework_patterns` extracts the HTTP method from the decorator/call AST (`extract_method`), not the usage_context, so enrichment is unaffected. Behavioral evidence: `add_url_rule('/users', ..., methods=['POST','PUT'])` → 2 usage_contexts with `http_method` `POST`/`PUT` (was 1 with `methods=['POST','PUT']`); a default route → `http_method='GET'` (was `methods=['GET']`).
- **`urllib.request.urlretrieve` is now flagged high-risk, consistent with `urlopen`/`Request` (WI-tijos, part A).** `io_boundary.is_high_risk` listed `urllib.request.urlopen` and `urllib.request.Request` but omitted `urlretrieve` — even though the `io_primitives/python.yaml` catalog already classifies all three as `net_send` — so a `urlretrieve()` network egress was silently treated as low-risk. Added it to `HIGH_RISK_PRIMITIVES`. Behavioral evidence: `is_high_risk("urllib.request.urlretrieve")` is now `True` (was `False`). The item's part B — extending the high-risk *completeness ratchet* from subprocess to net/fs — is **deferred as decision-gated**: unlike subprocess (where `subprocess == arbitrary execution == high risk` is a clean invariant), net/fs has no clean high-risk invariant (`stat`/`open` are benign, `urlopen`/`rmtree` are not), so classifying the ~1,400 uncensused net/fs primitives needs an ADR on the risk taxonomy first.
- **The event-sourcing linker's framework-blind Django-signal patterns are gated on Django detection, so `sqlite3.connect` / `socket.send` no longer produce phantom `framework='django'` nodes (WI-pitit).** The `.send` / `.connect` / `@receiver` regexes match bare identifiers (`sqlite3.connect(db)`, `sock.send(x)`) and hardcode `framework="django"`, so a repo with *no* Django — e.g. a plain `sqlite3` + `socket` module — produced phantom `framework='django'` event publisher/subscriber symbols (the producer-side root of the WI-babun surface contradiction). `detected_frameworks` (already carried on `LinkerContext`) is now threaded `event_sourcing_linker` → `link_events` → `_scan_file` → `_scan_python_events`, and the three Django sub-scans only fire when `"django"` is in the detected set — mirroring the `LinkerActivation(frameworks=["django"])` gate the sibling Django linkers already use. `detected_frameworks=None` stays **permissive** (unit callers testing the raw patterns are unaffected); the production linker passes `ctx.detected_frameworks` (a real, possibly-empty set). The generic event-bus / emitter / channel patterns (which don't hardcode a real framework) are untouched. Behavioral evidence (production path `event_sourcing_linker`): a `sqlite3.connect(db)` + `sock.send(x)` module with `detected_frameworks=set()` now emits **0** `framework='django'` symbols (was 2 — a `sqlite3` subscriber and a `sock` publisher); with `detected_frameworks={'django'}` a genuine `post_save.connect(...)` still emits its Django symbol (the gate is not over-tight).
- **Bare builtin `open()` calls now emit a `calls` edge to `builtins.open`, making `open()` a visible I/O call in every syntactic form (WI-mitul; awaits bakeoff validation).** The Python analyzer's terminal bare-`Name` call branch (`_process_call`) only emitted an edge when the callee was **imported** — so `open()` (a builtin, never imported) fell through and emitted **nothing** in every form (`fh = open(p)`, `with open(p) as f`, `open(p).read()`, `return open(p)`), leaving the `io_primitives/python.yaml` `builtins` catalog rows (fs_read / fs_write, `functions: [open]`) **dead** — no edge ever targeted them, so `open()`-based file I/O was invisible to `io-boundaries` at the call site. `_process_call` now recognizes a bare builtin I/O constructor via the existing `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` set and emits an unresolved `calls` edge `python:builtins:0-0:open:unresolved` (`dst_ref` module `builtins`), uniformly across all syntactic forms (they all route the bare name through the same terminal branch). This is orthogonal to the receiver-method edges — WI-fuvuj already emits `.read()`/`.write()` → `file.read`/`file.write`; WI-mitul is the edge for the `open()` **call itself**. Behavioral evidence: `extract_nodes` on a module with `open()` in each of the four forms now yields exactly one `calls` edge to `python:builtins:0-0:open:unresolved` per call (was zero). Tagged for bakeoff validation (adds an edge on every Python `open()` call corpus-wide, shifting edge / io-boundary / dead-code counts).
- **Co-located Python `test_*.py`/`*_test.py` and bash `test_*.sh` files now set `Symbol.supply_chain.is_test_file=True` (WI-mozum).** `supply_chain.TEST_FILE_PATTERNS` — the suffix conventions that mark a co-located test file (one sitting next to source, outside a `tests/` dir) as tier-1 first-party *test* code — covered Go / JS/TS / Ruby / C++ / Rust but omitted **Python and bash**, so a `mymod/sub/test_thing.py`, `mymod/sub/thing_test.py`, or `scripts/test_hooks.sh` classified with `is_test_file=False` (reason "default (no matching pattern)") — mislabeling out-of-tree tests as production for any consumer keying on the canonical `Symbol.supply_chain.is_test_file` field. Added `test_[^/]+\.py$` / `_test\.py$` / `test_[^/]+\.sh$` to the list (additive — the derived / external / generated precedence checks still run first, so a generated `v1alpha1_*.py` still wins `DERIVED`). This mirrors the convention already in `paths.is_test_file` (`startswith("test_")` / `"_test."`), closing the gap between the two is-test surfaces for Python/bash (the co-located, outside-`tests/` case; the in-`tests/`-dir case was already covered by `TEST_DIR_PATTERNS`). Behavioral evidence: `classify_file` on `mymod/sub/test_thing.py`, `mymod/sub/thing_test.py`, and `scripts/test_hooks.sh` → all now `tier=FIRST_PARTY, is_test=True` (were `is_test=False`).
- **Synthesized route-marker symbols (go/js/java) are now validator-clean — the id kind-slot round-trips against `Symbol.kind`, a real `origin_run_id` joins a real `AnalysisRun`, and `Symbol.origin` names a registered pass-id (WI-tufil).** When a web route surfaces via a standalone route-*marker* Symbol (`meta.framework_role='route'`) rather than a `concept=route` tag on the handler function, that marker tripped three validators at once: (1) **id_format** (advisory) — the id's kind-slot was the literal `route` while `Symbol.kind` is `function` (the ADR-0027 Phase-3 route→function fold set the kind but left the id-slot a `route` *fossil*), and `route` is not a registered symbol-kind; (2) **cross_field** (error) — the `framework_patterns` route materializer (js/java) minted markers via a direct `Symbol(...)` constructor that never stamped `origin_run_id` (empty → broken node→AnalysisRun provenance join, a WI-mosil-class regression); (3) **axis_conformance** (error) — those markers named an unregistered `route-materializer` / `django-cbv-method-expander` pass-id in `Symbol.origin`. The fix needs **no new symbol-kind axis** (ADR-0027 is not reopened) because nothing consumes the id kind-slot — all six route consumers (entrypoint detection, the HTTP/route_handler linker, `cmd_routes`, handler-slice keying, tier-4 route promotion, materializer dedup) key on `meta.framework_role`: the two materializers and the go analyzer's route pass now build the id via `make_symbol_id(..., kind="function")` so it round-trips against `Symbol.kind`; `cli.run_behavior_map` mints a real `AnalysisRun` for each materialization post-pass (mirroring boundary synthesis) and threads its `execution_id` into the markers' `origin_run_id`; and the two post-pass pass-ids are registered in `catalog._BUILTIN_PIPELINE_PASS_IDS` + `pass_metadata.GAP_PASSES`. (The tracker's premise that "go and js emit the identical triple" was **refuted on re-measure** — the go analyzer already stamped `origin`/`origin_run_id` correctly, so go carried only the advisory id-slot warning; the two errors were js/java-materializer-only.) Behavioral evidence (`fixtures/route-parity/{go,java,javascript}`, full pipeline): each language's route marker now has id kind-slot == `Symbol.kind` == `function`, a non-empty `origin_run_id` matching an `AnalysisRun.execution_id`, and every `origin` element a registered pass-id — locked by a new `test_route_concept_parity::test_route_marker_symbols_are_validator_clean` gate. Scope is the route-parity trio's producers; the remaining route-marker producers that still emit the `route` id-slot fossil (js_ts / py.py direct synthesis, ruby/php/elixir/swift/scala, grpc, and go `route_mount`) are the structural fleet tail tracked as a follow-up. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (route-marker id strings + provenance fields change; no structural schema change, and the id-slot is unconsumed).
- **Spring Boot web routes are now detected on Java controllers whose only Spring import is `org.springframework.web.*` (WI-tolap).** `refine_frameworks`' AUTO-mode demote phase validates a manifest-detected framework by requiring a production (non-test) import that matches one of the framework's registered import-module patterns; spring-boot's patterns were `spring-boot` / `org.springframework.boot` only. A canonical `@RestController` + `@GetMapping("/users")` controller imports its annotations from `org.springframework.web.bind.annotation` — a *sibling* namespace of `org.springframework.boot` that prefix-matches neither pattern — so a real Spring app with no direct `org.springframework.boot` import was demoted from `profile.frameworks` to `dev_frameworks`; and because `enrich_symbols` loads a framework's YAML only for `profile.frameworks`, `spring-boot.yaml` never loaded and no `concept=route`/`concept=controller` fired. Java was the sole top-5 language whose canonical web route was fully dark (the gap the WI-tosul route-parity re-measure surfaced). spring-boot's import patterns now also include the Spring annotation namespaces `org.springframework.web` / `.stereotype` / `.context`, so the demote phase recognizes production Spring usage and keeps the framework confirmed. These are *import* namespaces, not Maven group coordinates (spring-web's coordinate is `org.springframework:spring-web`), so `_pattern_matches_deps` never matches them against a declared dependency — manifest detection is unchanged and no manifest false positive is introduced; only import-edge matching (shared by the demote check and the promote phase) widens. This is deliberately *not* the autoload-by-convention exemption used for Rails: Spring is imported in app code (like Django), so import validation stays active rather than being bypassed. Behavioral evidence (`fixtures/route-parity/java/`, full pipeline): a `@RestController` class with an `@GetMapping("/users")` method → before, `profile.frameworks=[]` and the method carried no concept; after, `profile.frameworks=['spring-boot']`, the class gets `concept=controller`, and the method gets `concept=route`. The `test_route_concept_parity` gate's Java case moves from strict-xfail into `ROUTE_GREEN`.
- **`inherits` (Solidity-style class inheritance) is now treated as structurally as `extends` by slice and ranking, via a registry-owned `INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES` set (WI-lobif).** Two consumers hardcoded a structural set of `{extends, implements}` that silently omitted the registered `inherits` edge type — so a class hierarchy expressed with `inherits` (Solidity, and any language where that reads more naturally) leaked into **forward slices** as a normal edge (followed to the parent, unlike `extends` which is skipped) and was dropped from **ranking's** structural-edge preservation (excluded when its source was a test file, whereas `extends`/`implements` survive). Both consumers now query a new `edge_types.INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES = {extends, inherits, implements}` (slice unions `contains`); the registry drift linter (`find_axis_drift`) validates the set's members stay registry-coherent. Method-level `overrides` is deliberately excluded — it is a distinct method→parent relationship neither consumer treated as structural. Behavioral evidence: a forward slice from a contract that `inherits` a parent no longer pulls in the parent (matching `extends`); an `inherits` edge from a test file now survives `filter_edges_for_ranking` (was dropped).
- **Class fields are now rooted at their class by the containment linker, and `kind=file` anchors are exempt from the orphan ratchet — restoring the self-analysis orphan audit (WI-zajaz, WI-logon).** Two coupled orphan-ratchet fixes: (1) **WI-zajaz** — `field` was missing from `linkers/containment.py::CONTAINABLE_KINDS`, so class-body attributes (emitted as `kind=field` with dotted names like `Widget.size`) got no `contains` edge and were orphans; adding `field` roots them at their class via the same dotted-name Phase-1 path methods use (self-analysis: 1862 field orphans, 99.6% of all field symbols, → ~0). Per the containment module's note the dead-code reachability BFS does not traverse `contains`, so this does not move the dead-code-maybe FP rate; it gives fields their class-containment edge (affecting centrality and reverse-slice class-expansion). (2) **WI-logon** — the file-anchor work (WI-dagif/WI-rajod) mints one `kind=file` anchor per discovered path, and the nodeless config/doc cohort is legitimately edgeless, so `kind=file` is now exempt from both the per-kind orphan ceilings and the ratio (numerator + denominator) in the env-gated `test_orphan_baseline_ratchet` — the ratio now measures the orphan rate among *connectable* nodes. Together the two restore the ratchet from ~6.2% (breached) to within its 2.5% ceiling. Behavioral evidence: a fixture class with `name`/`size` attributes → before, both `field` symbols orphaned; after, `Widget contains Widget.name` / `Widget contains Widget.size`.
- **The Lit framework's bare-decorator patterns (`@property`, `@state`, `@query`) no longer fire on non-JavaScript symbols (WI-vuzur).** `lit.yaml` declares a file-level `language: javascript`, but the framework-pattern matcher only consults *per-pattern* `language` filters (never `FrameworkPatternDef.language`), so the file-level gate was inert — the `reactive_property` pattern keyed on `decorator: "^property$"` fired on Python's builtin `@property`, and `@state`/`@query` were ungated too. The three common-word-decorator patterns now carry `language: "^(javascript|typescript)$"`; the camelCase Lit decorators (`@queryAll`, `@queryAsync`, `@eventOptions`, `@customElement`) are ecosystem-unique and need no gate. (Enforcing the file-level gate globally across all 107 framework YAMLs — which carry `multi`, list, and comment-suffixed language forms — remains deferred.) Behavioral evidence: a Python method with `@property` matched against `lit` → before `[reactive_property]`, after `[]`; a JavaScript `@property` still matches.
- **`cargo_dependency` and `poetry_dependency` (and their `_dev`/`_build` variants) no longer both fire on every TOML dependency (WI-vifun).** The cargo and poetry dependency patterns in `config-conventions.yaml` were byte-identical (`symbol_kind: "^dependency$"` + `language: "^toml$"`), but TOML is a file *format*, not an ecosystem — so every `pyproject.toml` dependency was labeled both `poetry_dependency` *and* `cargo_dependency` (100% cargo false-positive on a pure-Python repo), and symmetrically for `Cargo.toml`. Each pattern now carries a `symbol_path` discriminator: `cargo_*` → `Cargo\.toml$`, `poetry_*` → `pyproject\.toml$`. The fix covers the whole family (bare + `dev` + `build`), since the `toml_config` producer routes both ecosystems' dev tables through the same `kind + dependency_scope` shape so they co-fired too. Behavioral evidence: a `pyproject.toml` dependency matched against `config-conventions` → before `[cargo_dependency, poetry_dependency]`, after `[poetry_dependency]`; a `Cargo.toml` dependency → after `[cargo_dependency]`.
- **Naming-convention framework concepts (`service_by_name`, `controller_by_name`, `handler_by_name`) are no longer attached to test-code classes (WI-bosab).** These heuristics fire on a class purely by its name (`^[A-Z]\w*Service$` etc.), so a test fixture like `class MyService` or a pytest `class TestUserController` in `tests/` was labeled a production service/controller — despite the YAML comment claiming test exclusion the regex never delivered. The fix honors the canonical `Symbol.is_test_file` verdict, but that verdict is set by supply-chain classification which runs *after* concept enrichment (enrichment precedes the linkers, whose symbols must also be classified), so the matcher itself cannot see it. A new post-classification pass `strip_test_file_only_concepts` removes any concept whose producing pattern declared `is_test_file: false` from a symbol the classifier marked `is_test_file` — the three naming-convention patterns now carry that marker. This consumes the canonical verdict (per the META-jotih "one is_test_file source" family), not a separate path heuristic. Behavioral evidence (editable install): a repo with `src/app.py` (`UserService`, `UserController`) + `tests/test_app.py` (`MyService`, `TestUserController`) → before, all four carried their `*_by_name` concept; after, the two production classes keep theirs and the two test classes carry `concepts=[]`.
- **The `io-boundaries` and `verify-claims` CLI commands now honor `Edge.is_resolved` + `Edge.dst_ref` from the loaded map, so the consumer-time boundary classification matches the in-process gate (WI-kumol).** Both commands rehydrated a 4-field `_Edge(src, dst, edge_type, meta)` facade from the persisted behavior_map, **dropping** `is_resolved` and `dst_ref`. `compute_boundary_map` reads `edge.is_resolved` (the ADR-0028 unresolved-receiver skip in `_compute_external_potential`) and `edge.dst_ref` (the WI-tihup structured external-target lookup) via `getattr(..., True/None)`, so the facade's omission defaulted `is_resolved=True` — the receiver gate never fired on the CLI path and every unresolved bare-name method match (a duck-typed `x.read()` / `x.write()` on an unknown receiver) was misclassified into the `external_potential` bucket, re-inflating exactly the noise `io-boundary:F3` (INV-tapat / INV-maluk) suppresses in-process. Both facades now route through one shared `_rehydrate_io_boundary_edges` helper that reconstructs `is_resolved` (default `True`) and `dst_ref` (`ExternalRef.from_dict`, default `None`) so a pre-ADR-0028 / pre-WI-tihup legacy map (lacking the keys) classifies exactly as before. Effect on self-analysis: `external_potential_edges` **26338 → 0** (the spurious unresolved-receiver matches); `total_io_edges` (the real, verified boundary set) **unchanged at 754** — honoring the structured `dst_ref` is byte-identical to the legacy colon-split module-hint heuristic on this corpus, so no genuine I/O boundary is lost (measured: 0 real chains dropped, 0 added). The `slice --io-boundary` path was already correct — it operates on real `Edge` objects, not this facade. Behavioral evidence (editable install): a fixture with `os.remove(path)` (`is_resolved=False` but resolves via `dst_ref` → `fs_write`) plus duck-typed `obj.read()` / `bus.write()` (`is_resolved=False`, no `dst_ref`) → before, `external_potential_edges=2`; after, `0`, with the `os.remove` `fs_write` boundary preserved (`total_io_edges=1`).
- **A nested private closure matching the `library_export` concept is no longer emitted as a `library_export` entrypoint (WI-pikib).** The entrypoint detector fired `LIBRARY_EXPORT` on any symbol carrying a `library_export` concept without checking whether that symbol is actually part of the package's public API — so a private inner closure such as `fold_string_interpolation._sub` (the callback passed to `re.sub`, `visibility=private`, `is_exported=False`) was handed to consumers enumerating the public-API surface and seeded reachability BFS from a non-importable helper. The `library_export` branch now gates on INV-jusot's canonical `Symbol.visibility` axis — computed in `finalize` (step 7b), which runs before `detect_entrypoints`, so `visibility` is populated at detection time — skipping the entrypoint when `visibility` is affirmatively non-public; a `None` visibility (uncomputed / legacy map) stays permissive so a genuine export is never suppressed on a missing signal. This is the Wave-3 §E-5 "exportedness gate keys on the new `visibility` axis" constraint, now unblocked by INV-jusot; the item's original "cannot use `is_exported` — None for 100% of nodes" caveat is stale (visibility and the reconciled `is_exported` are populated post-INV-jusot). `serialization_callback` (Go reflective encoding callbacks, WI-pimig) is deliberately left ungated. Behavioral evidence (editable install): a package `__init__.py` exposing public `fold_string_interpolation` with a nested `_sub` closure → before, both `fold_string_interpolation` (public, conf 1.0) and `fold_string_interpolation._sub` (private, conf 0.95) emitted as `library_export`; after, only the public `fold_string_interpolation` remains.
- **The Python `if __name__ == "__main__"` module guard now classifies as a distinct `main_guard` entrypoint kind instead of overloading `main_function` (WI-tuvun).** `kind=main_function` was emitted for two structurally different targets — a **function** node (a `def main()`, label "Python main()") and a **file** node (a guard-only `__main__.py` with no `main()`, label "Python script (if __name__ == '__main__')") — both at confidence 1.0, so a consumer keying on `kind=main_function` to jump to a callable had to handle a file target too, disambiguated only by the free-text label. The `main_guard` concept now maps to a new `EntrypointKind.MAIN_GUARD` (auto-registered into the `entrypoint-kind` catalog axis and the generated `Entrypoint.kind` schema enum), so `kind` alone names the target type (`main_function` → function, `main_guard` → file). The INV-hosuh dedup is preserved: when a script has both a `def main()` and the guard, the function-level `main_function` supersedes the redundant same-path `main_guard` (the guard-only `__main__.py` keeps its lone `main_guard`). Ripples: `sketch` groups `main_guard` under "CLI & Scripts" (not "Other"), and `slice`'s canonical-root boost (`_MAIN_KINDS`) includes it so a guard-only script is still preferred as an auto-selected entry. Dead-code seeding is kind-agnostic (seeds every entrypoint's `symbol_id`), so no reachability regression. Behavioral evidence (editable install): a `__main__.py` with a guard and no `main()` → `kind=main_guard` target_kind=file; a module with `def main()` + guard → single `kind=main_function` target_kind=function (guard deduped). The secondary confidence-drift note (spec §890 says 0.85 Structural, substrate shows ~0.96/1.0) is out of scope — it's the WI-suvil hardcoded-confidence family.
- **`explain` and sketch's Entry Points / Source Files sections now surface the captured `Symbol.docstring` — the human-written intent that was previously never displayed by the two commands an agent runs to orient itself (WI-kipod).** Docstrings are captured for 74–90% of function/class/method symbols and (per WI-kazob) 845/908 file-anchor nodes, but `explain` read location/metrics/callers/callees and never `.docstring`, and sketch's Entry Points rendered the symbol's **path** after the dash rather than its summary while Source Files was a bare path list. Now `explain` prints a `Docstring:` line when the symbol carries one; the Entry Points section renders the docstring after the dash when present (falling back to the path when absent — the path stays recoverable via `explain`); and the Source Files section gained a relative-path→module-summary map (`_file_docstrings`, threaded into `_format_source_files`) so each file line reads `` - `mod.py` — <module summary> `` when its file node carries a docstring. (sketch's Key Symbols section already rendered docstrings — that was not part of the gap.) Behavioral evidence: on the `hypergumbo-tracker` subpackage, Source Files now reads `` - `store.py` — Store for the hypergumbo tracker — YAML I/O, compile, CRUD, Lamport clock. `` etc. (34/34 file nodes summarized, was a bare path list); `explain greet` prints `Docstring: Return a friendly greeting for the given name.`; Entry Points renders `` - `main` (Python main()) — Run the demo… `` (was `` — `mod_b.py` ``). Non-Python file anchors (whose languages lack a uniform module-docstring construct) keep the bare path line.
- **The Python file-kind node now carries the module docstring's one-line summary (WI-kazob).** Every source file carries a substantive module docstring (a project requirement), but the file/module node never received it — 0 of 902 file-kind nodes carried a docstring, so an agent asking "what is this module responsible for?" got the path + a symbol list but not the module's one-line statement of purpose. The Python analyzer attached docstrings to function/class/method symbols but never to the file node. py.py now stamps `ast.get_docstring(module)`'s first line (≤80 chars, matching the `Symbol.docstring` contract) onto the `kind="file"` Symbol. To reach the dominant case — library modules whose only top-level content is imports + defs + a docstring (so `_has_module_level_code` is false and the file node was previously emitted by the language-agnostic orchestrator synthesizer, which can't read a Python docstring) — the file-node emission condition is broadened to "module has executable code **or** a docstring"; the existing INV-hojus dedup means this only changes *which* producer emits the single file node, never doubles it. Python-scoped (the language whose module docstrings the project mandates; 845/908 file nodes); other languages lack a uniform module-docstring construct.
- **`edge.meta.channel` is de-overloaded to a single referent (the dataflow conduit / topic) and registered in MetaKeySpec (WI-pozom).** `channel` (a first-class `Edge.create` kwarg that lands flat in `edge.meta.channel`) meant a message **topic** on `event_publishes` edges but a WebSocket **route path** / Django-Channels consumer class on the WS `references`/`calls` bridge edges, and a dispatch **target name** on annotation `dispatches_to` edges — one field, three unrelated referents, no discriminator (one fixture even carried a full external URL). The non-conduit referents are removed at their 3 edge emit sites: the WS endpoint identifier (route path or consumer class) is already the `references` edge's `dst` endpoint symbol (`websocket.py` — `channel` dropped, not relocated, since it is not reliably a URL); the cross-language WS route already lives on `meta.url_path` (`websocket.py` `calls` edge — redundant `channel` dropped); the annotation dispatch target is the `dispatches_to` edge `dst` (`annotation_convention.py` — redundant `channel` dropped). `edge.meta.channel` now carries **only** the dataflow conduit (message topic / IPC channel / CRDT document / crypto primitive), disambiguated by the co-occurring `channel_kind`. `channel` and `url_path` are now registered in `axis_meta_keys` MetaKeySpec with the conduit-vs-route contract. No `Edge.create` signature change (the kwarg stays for the conduit emitters). `Symbol.meta.channel` on the synthetic annotated-dispatcher node is unchanged (this is an edge-meta finding).
- **OTP `otp_call`/`otp_cast` edges fold to the canonical `dispatches_to` edge_type + a registered `meta['mechanism']` key (WI-rorul).** The Elixir/Erlang OTP linker emitted two edge_type values — `otp_call`/`otp_cast` — that were NOT in the canonical `edge_types` registry, shipped via dict-indirection (`CALL_TYPE_MAP`/`ERLANG_CALL_TYPE_MAP` tuple-unpack: `handler_suffix, edge_type = CALL_TYPE_MAP[call_type]`) that the static L3 producer-coherence walker cannot resolve — so the gate stayed green while 2 unregistered edge_types shipped on any Erlang/Elixir OTP analysis (a consumer enumerating edge types from the registry missed them). The two values now fold to the registered `dispatches_to` edge_type, with the synchronous-call vs asynchronous-cast distinction carried on a new registered `meta['mechanism']` key (values `otp_call`/`otp_cast`); `meta['framework_dispatch']='otp_genserver'` and `meta['call_type']` are unchanged. No in-tree consumer branched on the old edge_types. The latent L3-walker blind-spot itself (static resolution of dict-indirection / tuple-unpack edge_type emit sites) has **no remaining exploiter** post-fold (the scope-sweep's claimed SCIP `has_type`/`defined_by` could not be reproduced) and is split to a focused preventive follow-up, WI-zigih.
- **The reference-family edge-meta key `construct` is renamed `ref_construct` to disambiguate it from the sibling `call_construct` (INV-lajov).** `edge.meta` carried two name-confusable keys with disjoint scopes and vocabularies: `construct` (reference-family / non-call edges — values `jsx`, `script_src`, `websocket_endpoint`, `event_emit`, `dispatch_table`; emitted by the react/html/websocket/solidity/c/cpp analyzers, 31 self-corpus edges) and `call_construct` (`calls` edges — values `method`/`function`/`cross_file`, ~21k edges). Zero overlap in populated rows, but a schema reader would reasonably read them as related (one a generalization of the other). Per the keep-distinct ruling (WI-dinih precedent), `construct` is renamed `ref_construct` at all 6 emission sites + the `axis_meta_keys` MetaKeySpec registry; the two now read as parallel-but-distinct per-edge-family construct axes (reference-family construct vs call-edge SHAPE), not a sub/superset pair. `call_construct` is unchanged. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump — `edge.meta` is a free-form dict and no in-tree consumer branches on the key (it is a display/disambiguation key). The `edge_types.py` and `docs/concept-axes.md` fold-residue docstrings (`likely fold to … meta['construct']=X`) are updated to the new key.
- **Starlette `WebSocketRoute` now classifies as a `websocket_handler` entrypoint instead of `http_route` + `meta.method=WS`; the usage_contexts WS concept vocabulary is aligned to `websocket_handler`; the synthetic `WS` method is retained on the route node (WI-kuvig / WI-lomoz).** WebSocket routes were filed under `entrypoint.kind='http_route'` with the synthetic method `WS` (label `"HTTP WS /ws"`), so a consumer filtering `kind=='http_route'` for HTTP routes accidentally swept in WebSocket handlers and `kind=='websocket_handler'` returned nothing for them — the documented `websocket_handler` kind was structurally unreachable for Starlette. Premise was **partially stale**: every framework *except* Starlette already emitted `concept: websocket_handler` (→ `WEBSOCKET_HANDLER`); Starlette was the lone holdout, funneling both the handler (via `starlette.yaml` `concept: route`) and the inline-minted route symbol (`meta.framework_role='route'`, `http_method='WS'`) into the `http_route` branch (the latter via the direct-route-symbol pass, which the WI body misattributed to the route-handler linker).
