Fix: (1) `starlette.yaml` WebSocketRoute → `concept: websocket_handler`; (2) `_detect_from_concepts` (both the concept-route pass and the direct-route-symbol pass) now classifies a route whose method is the `WS` marker as `EntrypointKind.WEBSOCKET_HANDLER` — a general structural guard, not a Starlette special-case — keeping `framework_role='route'` so the route→handler `dispatches_to` edge still lands; (3) path-bearing WS routes get a `"WS <path>"` label so the handler entrypoint and the route-symbol entrypoint dedup to one (mirroring HTTP route dedup), while pathless concept WebSocket handlers (NestJS gateways) keep their `"<Framework> WebSocket handler"` label and are not collapsed. `meta.http_method='WS'` / the `UsageContext.metadata.methods=['WS']` value are unchanged (RETAIN); `context_name` stays the verbatim source class (`WebSocketRoute`) since it is a free-text display axis. WI-lomoz resolution: entrypoints (`websocket_handler`) and usage_contexts (`WebSocketRoute`) now consistently indicate WebSocket rather than disagreeing (`http_route` vs `WebSocketRoute`). Spec §13 entrypoint-kind catalog updated.
- **In-repo *role* files (examples, documentation bundles, `.ipynb` notebooks, fuzz/bench harnesses) now classify as tier 1 (`first_party`) with their role on a flag/reason, not tier 2; in-repo generated *routes* promote tier 4→1, not tier 2 (INV-naduh; ADR-0041 §1).** `FileClassification` independently routed these role files to `Tier.INTERNAL_DEP`, violating the distance-only axiom — *tier names supply-chain distance and nothing else*, and these files are the project's own code (distance 0). `supply_chain.classify_file` now returns `Tier.FIRST_PARTY` for `EXAMPLE_PATTERNS` (keeping `is_example=True`), `DOCUMENTATION_PATTERNS`, `.ipynb` notebooks, and `FUZZ_BENCH_PATTERNS` (role carried by the reason string); config-declared `internal_package_roots` is now the **sole** tier-2 producer (no path heuristic yields tier 2). `cli.py` route-promotion of generated/derived routes now lands **tier 1** (was tier 2) — in-repo routes are distance 0, and the `route promoted from derived` reason records the generated origin. Behavioral evidence (production-path fixture tests): `examples/app.js`, `demos/*`, `samples/*`, `Documentation.docc/*.swift`, `*.ipynb`, `fuzz/*`, `benches/*` now classify **tier 1** (were tier 2) with `is_example`/role intact, and the end-to-end `run_behavior_map` route-promotion test asserts the generated Flask route lands tier 1. Latent on the hypergumbo self-corpus (0 role-file tier-2 nodes), so no published self-analysis number moves; the change surfaces on any repo with `examples/`, docs, notebooks, or fuzz/bench dirs. Spec §14 updated: the scope-note it carried as the open follow-up is resolved, and the tier-2 table row + "internal dependency detection" section now describe config `internal_package_roots` as the only tier-2 producer (workspace members and role files at tier 1).
- **Spec §9 io-boundary vocabulary aligned to the actually-emitted set; `unknown_dynamic` flagged reserved-but-unimplemented (WI-datoz).** The original finding (CLI emits 2 spec-extending categories + misses 5) was largely overtaken — §840 now enumerates exactly `CATALOG_BOUNDARY_TYPES` (15) + `external_potential`, matching `io_boundary.KNOWN_IO_BOUNDARIES`, which is what `io-boundaries` emits and `verify-claims` boundary constraints accept. The one residual was `unknown_dynamic`: ADR-0016 reserves it for runtime-computed call targets (`getattr(obj, name)()`), but no producer emits it and it is absent from `KNOWN_IO_BOUNDARIES`. §840 now states it is reserved/not-currently-emitted rather than implying it is a live value, so the documented vocabulary matches the emitted/derivable vocabulary exactly. Documentation-only.
- **Spec §9 no longer claims `io_boundary` / `io_primitive` are "stamped on every call edge" — the contract is reframed to consumer-time derivation (WI-fakuv / WI-puvun).** No producer ever stamped `meta.io_boundary` (0 of 110,533 self-corpus edges carried the key); the classification is computed on demand from the `io_primitives/*.yaml` catalogs by `io_boundary.compute_boundary_map`, which `hypergumbo io-boundaries` invokes. The spec now states the behavior map's `calls` edges carry no `io_boundary` key and names the consumer-time `io-boundaries` CLI canonical (the key still appears on the *derived* edges consumer-time views emit). Documentation-only; `axis_meta_keys` already described the key as set by `compute_boundary_map`.
- **The per-symbol `Symbol.lines_of_code` field is renamed `Symbol.line_span`, and `profile.languages[*]` is reconciled to its real `{files, loc}` shape (WI-bozid).** One term — "lines of code" — named two different counting conventions: `Symbol.lines_of_code` was the *physical* line span (`end_line - start_line + 1`, including blanks/comments; verified pure span — 0/29853 nodes deviated), while the spec's "lines of code" and the file-level `profile.languages[*].loc` are *source*-lines-of-code (SLOC). The per-symbol field is renamed `line_span` across all ~150 producer/consumer sites + the schema; its docstring now states it is physical span, NOT SLOC, and points to `profile.languages[*].loc` for the file-level SLOC convention. `Symbol.from_dict` reads the legacy `lines_of_code` key as a fallback so pre-rename behavior maps still deserialize (the new `line_span` key wins when both are present). Separately, the `profile.languages[*]` **schema was drifted**: it declared `{files, lines, percentage}` but the producer (`profile.LanguageStats.to_dict`) only ever emits `{files, loc}` — `lines`/`percentage` were phantom keys no code produced; the schema now documents the real `{files, loc}` shape with `loc` described as file-level SLOC (distinct from per-symbol `line_span`).
- **`sketch_precomputed.additional_file_centrality_scores` is relabeled as a symbol-mention *density* (unbounded ≥ 0), not a `[0,1]`-normalized centrality — the schema, the producer comment, and the `CentralityResult` docstring now disclose the real semantics (WI-sigof).** The values are `Σ(in-degree of the graph symbols whose names appear in the file) ÷ file length in characters` — normalized *by file size*, never to a `[0,1]` range — but the schema described them as a "symbol-mention centrality score" and the producing dataclass field is named `normalized_scores`, so a consumer thresholding (`> 0.8 = hub`), reading the value as a probability/fraction, or comparing across repos on a common scale mis-scales it (a short file naming high-in-degree symbols routinely exceeds 1.0). No value change — this is a labeling/disclosure fix per the WI-sigof ruling: the `additional_file_centrality_scores` schema description now states the exact formula and that the score is unbounded, repo-relative, and a ranking key only; the `cli` producer comment and `CentralityResult.normalized_scores` docstring carry the same disclosure (the field name is documented as historical — normalized by size, not range). A characterization test locks the recharacterization (a 3-char file naming an in-degree-30 symbol scores 10.0), so any future re-normalization to `[0,1]` fails the test and forces the label to be revisited. (The WI-sigof filing's "un-normalized sum-of-top-3, range 0–7.42" evidence predates the WI-rajod/WI-ribag rework that replaced the old `centrality_scores` field with this in-degree-density computation; the mislabel survived the rework, which is why it stayed open.)
- **`supply_chain_summary.derived_skipped` now enumerates the derived-artifact directories discovery excluded, instead of a silent `{files: 0, paths: []}` (WI-jafoz).** The tier-4 derived classifier (`supply_chain.classify_file` / `DERIVED_PATH_PATTERNS`) only ever sees files that survive discovery's gitignore-style `DEFAULT_EXCLUDES` prune — but that prune drops `dist/`, `build/`, `out/`, `target/`, `__pycache__/`, `.next/`, … *before* classification, so derived dirs never produced a tier-4 node and the call site passed `derived_paths=[]` as a stub (commented "would be tracked during file discovery in a full implementation"). On the self-corpus `derived_skipped` therefore read `{files: 0, paths: []}` despite the repo plainly containing `__pycache__`, `scripts/__pycache__`, and `packages/htrac-frontend/node_modules` — a silent lie about what was ignored. A new `_find_derived_skipped(repo_root)` walk recovers them directly: it matches the same `DERIVED_PATH_PATTERNS` the classifier uses, records every file beneath a derived directory, and prunes the dependency/VCS excludes (node_modules, .git, venv, …) so a *dependency's own* build output is never misattributed as the project's. `derived_skipped.files` is now the true skipped-file count and `derived_skipped.paths` enumerates them (capped at 10 per the existing spec §metrics contract). Behavioral evidence: a repo whose only artifacts live in a `build/` dir now reports `derived_skipped.files ≥ 2` with `build/artifact.{o,js}` in `paths`, where it previously reported `{files: 0, paths: []}`.
- **`metrics.by_supply_chain_tier` exposes a new `edges_incident` count so external-dependency tiers' graph contribution is visible instead of a misleading 0 (WI-modom).** The existing per-tier `edges` counts each edge once by its **source** node's tier; because external-dependency tiers (2/3) are graph *sinks* (referenced, not referencing), their `edges` read ~0 — on self-analysis, 0 despite 2,881 / 27,272 edges incident on tier-2 / tier-3 nodes — which reads as "no contribution." A new `edges_incident` counts each edge once per **distinct endpoint tier** (either-endpoint), exposing each tier's real contribution; it double-counts cross-tier edges by design and so does **not** sum to `total_edges` (the source-tier `edges` view remains the reconciling one — its per-tier sum equals the source-resolved edge total). Both views are documented in spec §metrics. INV-jukok preserved: an unresolved endpoint is skipped, never minting a phantom `unknown` tier.
- **Cross-module calls/imports to a symbol *defined in* a package's `__init__.py` now resolve to the in-tree symbol instead of leaking to an `external_symbol` twin (INV-nuzas residual).** `_module_name_from_path` keys a package `__init__.py`'s symbols under `pkg.__init__`, but callers and imports reference `pkg` — so the `(module, name)` registry lookup missed every symbol *defined directly* in a package `__init__` (the existing re-export aliasing only covered names *imported into* the `__init__`, and `module_to_file_id` only resolved import edges to the file node, not calls to the symbol). On the self-corpus this leaked **342 first-party `calls` edges** to phantom external nodes — e.g. `hypergumbo_core.url_folding.fold_array_join` (defined in `url_folding/__init__.py`) resolved 0 of 6 callers and was mis-stamped `ecosystem=third_party`. The global symbol registry now also aliases `__init__`-defined symbols under the importable package name (mirroring the re-export and `module_to_file_id` package-alias patterns); strictly additive — the dead `pkg.__init__` key is retained. Behavioral evidence: in-tree-module `calls`→external twins **342 → 283** on the self-corpus (59 recovered; `fold_array_join` 0 → 6 resolved). The 283 residual is *other* INV-nuzas facets (receiver-typed method calls — `regex.match`, `dict.items` — needing type inference; cross-package non-`__init__` cases), so INV-nuzas stays open with reduced scope.
- **Stdlib *submodule* imports (`unittest.mock`, `importlib.util`, `urllib.request`, …) are now recognized as `ecosystem=stdlib` instead of mis-stamped `third_party` (WI-bifih; ADR-0041 §3).** `IoBoundaryCatalog.is_stdlib_module` matched only exact names, and `python.yaml` enumerates only *top-level* stdlib modules (`unittest`, `urllib`, …) with no `stdlib_prefixes` — so every dotted stdlib submodule fell through to `third_party` (355 import-edge boundary nodes on the self-corpus, dominated by `unittest.mock` ×251). `is_stdlib_module` now falls back to the first dotted/slashed segment: a submodule of an enumerated top-level stdlib package is itself stdlib (a third-party `requests.sessions` whose head `requests` is not enumerated correctly stays non-stdlib). This corrects the `ecosystem` axis (the only behavioral consumer of `is_stdlib_module`) and feeds the `supply_chain_summary` tier-3 ecosystem sub-buckets. Per ADR-0041 §3, stdlib stays tier 3 / `external_symbol` / `is_resolved=False` — only the `meta.ecosystem` stamp changes, so the ADR-0037 edge-resolution FK is untouched. **Also closes the §3 single-source gap in WI-bifih's own package:** `py_deps.parse_python_dependencies` recognized stdlib via a *second* source (the live interpreter's `sys.stdlib_module_names`); it now uses the same `python.yaml` catalog (`load_catalog("python").is_stdlib_module`), so the two recognizers cannot version-drift. (WI-bifih's original framing — "wire the catalog into import *resolution* to cut dead-code FPs" — was overtaken: the ecosystem stamping already landed with ADR-0041 §3, and `imports` edges feed neither the dead-code candidate set nor its reachability graph; the real residual was this submodule mis-stamp + the second-source consolidation.)
- **`hypergumbo sketch -t N` for large budgets no longer dies with exit 141 (SIGPIPE) and zero output (INV-vopuh).** Every budget ≥16146 on hypergumbo's own self-analysis reproducibly returned exit 141 with stdout=0 bytes. A 12-pass investigation pinned the death to *inside* `ranking._compute_centrality_with_python` — but that path spawns no pipes (pure-Python regex over a `ThreadPoolExecutor` of *threads*), so it could not itself raise SIGPIPE. The real cause was one layer up and structural: `main()` reset the **process-wide** SIGPIPE disposition to `SIG_DFL`, so an `EPIPE` on *any* pipe in the process was delivered as a fatal signal 13 to the whole interpreter regardless of which thread provoked it. The `transformers` safetensors auto-conversion fires a *background thread* during `model.encode()` (see `sketch_embeddings._load_st_model_offline_first`); when that thread hit a broken pipe it killed the process, and pass-10 saw it "die in centrality" only because that is where the *main* thread happened to be when the async signal arrived (budget sensitivity = more text to encode = a wider race window; `> file` redirect did not help because the broken pipe was never stdout). The fix leaves SIGPIPE at Python's default disposition (`SIG_IGN`), under which a write to a closed pipe raises a *catchable* `BrokenPipeError` confined to the provoking thread, so the sketch completes. The single case the old `SIG_DFL` was installed for — `hypergumbo explain | head` exiting quietly — is handled explicitly by catching `BrokenPipeError` around command dispatch in `main()` and redirecting the stdout fd to `/dev/null` (suppressing the shutdown-flush re-raise) before exiting non-zero. Reproduced deterministically through the real `cli.main()` entry point: driving `main()`'s prologue then writing to a closed pipe from a worker thread exits -13 (SIGPIPE) on the old code and 0 (survives, full output) on the fix.
- **Warm `hypergumbo sketch` reads the 4x/16x comparison sketches from cache instead of regenerating them every run (WI-ribag; INV-papuj sketch-read-path cluster).** The representativeness table compares the requested-budget sketch against 4x and 16x budget sketches; those two extra `generate_sketch` calls dominated warm-cache wall-clock (~83% on the self-corpus — characterized at 155s total vs 27s with `--no-comparison-sketches`) because, although each comparison sketch's *text* was `cache_write`-ten to the per-(repo, state, analyzer-identity) cache dir, nothing read it back — they regenerated on every invocation (an INV-papuj cache-read miss). Each comparison sketch's `SketchStats` (the only data the table needs) is now serialized to a `.stats.json` sidecar alongside its cached text, and the new `_get_or_generate_comparison_sketch` helper reads both back when present for the current state — skipping the regeneration while keeping the table always-on. A present file in the state-scoped cache dir is fresh by construction (any source change rotates the dir); a pre-existing text-only cache (no sidecar) regenerates once and writes the sidecar. Live repro: a fully-warm `hypergumbo . -t 8000` *with* the representativeness table dropped from ~155s to ~28s (matching the `--no-comparison-sketches` baseline, i.e. the comparison sketches are now ~free on warm cache). New `SketchStats.to_dict`/`from_dict` (schema-drift tolerant). The per-file embedding cost (`additional_file_centrality_scores`) was already cached in `sketch_precomputed`; the symbol-centrality recompute *within* a single generation is a small residual deferred to a follow-up.
- **`hypergumbo sketch --input <map>` now summarizes the MAP's file universe instead of re-walking the working tree (INV-jumim; INV-papuj sketch-read-path cluster).** `generate_sketch` sourced its file universe from `repo_root` filesystem walks regardless of `--input`: `_analyze_test_files` → `find_files` → `repo_root.rglob()` (the documented 60+s hang on populated repos), `_collect_source_files`/`_get_repo_languages`/config discovery (all `FileIndex`-aware but with no index set on the sketch path → rglob fallback), and the Structure tree (`iterdir`-based). So a package-scoped map fed from a larger root produced a sketch of the *entire surrounding repo* (e.g. a 902-node package map → "1,218 files" whole-repo structure) and the test-file count came from a fresh disk walk that could diverge from the map's own `supply_chain.is_test_file` population. The fix is one structural seam: `generate_sketch` is now a thin wrapper that, when a map is provided (or a warm on-disk map is found), synthesizes a `FileIndex` from the map's node paths (new `FileIndex.from_paths`) and installs it as the process-global index for the duration of the call — so `find_files` (hence `_analyze_test_files`), language detection, config discovery, and source-file collection all see the MAP's universe with **zero change to those functions** — then restores the prior index. The Structure helpers (`_format_structure_tree`, `_format_structure_tree_fallback`, `_count_root_items`, and `_collect_important_files`'s root-level source-file scan) were made index-aware via a shared `_count_dir_items`/`_dir_children_from_index` so the directory listing and "(N items)"/"[and N other items]" counts also reflect the map. Non-`--input` sketches with no index are unchanged (the helpers fall back to `iterdir`). The freshness-warning scan in `cmd_sketch` deliberately still walks the tree — its job is to detect source files added or changed since the map was generated, and a new file is by definition not in the map. Advances INV-jumim (the read-path 60s + wrong-universe defect) under the INV-papuj umbrella.
- **`access_mode` no longer mislabels reads as `write` on assignment lines (INV-kudug, symptoms 1&2).** `annotate_dataflow_ast` derived every edge's `access_mode` from the line's statement kind (`x = …` → `write`), with a downgrade-to-`read` that only covered `calls` edges. So a `references` edge (`evidence_type=ast_name_read`), a `function_reference`, or a `module_attr_ref` (`module_attribute_reference`) sitting on an assignment line — the RHS *being read* — wrongly inherited `write`, contradicting the edge's own evidence within a single `meta` (on self-analysis ~49% of `references` edges and ~95% of populated `module_attr_ref` carried `ast_name_read`/`module_attribute_reference` + `access_mode=write` simultaneously). Read-evidence-type edges now derive `access_mode=read` from their evidence type *before* the line-statement heuristic (the same spirit as the existing calls-on-write-line downgrade, generalised). **Symptom 3 (also fixed):** in-place collection-mutator calls (`.append`/`.add`/`.extend`/`.insert`/`.setdefault`/`.update`) now classify as `mutate` rather than `write` in `python.yaml` `library_patterns`; external-sink writes (`.write`/`.send`/`.put`/…) remain `write` and the element-removal family (`.pop`/`.remove`/`.clear`/`.discard`/`.popitem`) remains `delete`, so the four-cell `read`/`write`/`mutate`/`delete` vocabulary is now used as intended instead of `write` being overloaded with `mutate`.
- **`hypergumbo run` per-handler slice files no longer collide — the framework route marker no longer overwrites the function handler's slice on disk (INV-nubub).** `_emit_handler_slices` grouped candidates by symbol id, but the framework-materialized route marker (`meta.framework_role="route"`, ~0 outbound edges) and the concept-enriched function handler for the same `(method, path)` are *distinct ids that produce the same* `slice.handler.<METHOD>.<path>.json` filename — so the second writer silently clobbered the first, leaving only the degenerate 1-node marker slice (7/8 emitted files on self-analysis showed `n=1/e=0` instead of the function's `n=5–17`). Emission now collapses distinct ids that resolve to the same filename, keeping one slice per file — **preferring the non-marker function symbol** so the rich call-graph slice wins — and merges every colliding sibling's route metadata. The same-id-under-multiple-routes case (one handler serving GET+POST) still emits one file with a merged routes list, and a marker-only route (e.g. Go `kind="route"`) still emits. Each `slice.handler.index.json` entry's `node_count` now matches its on-disk file's `feature.node_ids` length, and no two emitted entries share a `file`.
- **`sketch --require-section` and `search --language`/`--kind` reject unknown values instead of silently accepting them (WI-furop; INV-fabov family).** `--require-section NONEXISTENT` previously exited 0 with a full sketch and no required section — so a typo (`Key Sympols`) or wrong case (`key symbols`) silently bought no budget guarantee — and `search --language klingon` / `--kind nonexistent_kind` returned "No symbols found" (exit 0), indistinguishable from a real empty result. All three now validate against their canonical enumerable set (the new `VALID_SKETCH_SECTIONS` constant / `all_known_languages()` / `all_symbol_kind_names()`) **before** running analysis, erroring with exit 2 and a difflib did-you-mean suggestion — mirroring the `config <language>` validation (INV-gufod). A shared `_reject_unknown_choice` helper embodies the fix-class, and the section-name vocabulary that was triplicated across the CLI help text, the sketch docstring, and an analysis-gate set is now a single source of truth.
- **`hypergumbo compact` re-projects feature slices onto the compacted graph (INV-titid).** The compact pass selected a budget-limited subset of nodes/edges but copied the source map's `features[]` wholesale, so the majority of each feature's `node_ids`/`edge_ids` referenced content that had been pruned (~85% dangling even at 10× the default budget — the feature claimed to describe a slice of the compact graph yet pointed at absent nodes/edges; the dangling-*out* twin of INV-tarol's dangling-*in* slice leak). The compact output now rewrites each surviving feature's `entry_nodes`/`node_ids`/`edge_ids`/`node_depths`/`node_tiers` to the retained sets and **drops any feature whose every entry node was pruned** — mirroring the existing entrypoint-filtering precedent (an entrypoint whose symbol was pruned is already dropped). Both selection branches (connectivity-aware and coverage) are covered. Compact's `features` section is now self-contained: every referenced id exists in the compact's own `nodes`/`edges`.
- **`hypergumbo slice` from a function no longer leaks its containing file's imports (INV-tarol).** A function-entry slice seeded `add_file_imports(entry.path)` regardless of entry kind, so it absorbed the *whole containing file's* import edges (`src`=file-node) — edges not reachable from the function. The symptom was starkest at `--max-hops 0`, where the slice was reduced to *only* the file's imports and contained **none** of the function's own edges. Both `add_file_imports` sites (entry-init + BFS-loop) are now gated on `kind in _CONTAINER_KINDS`, so **file/container entries still include their imports, but function entries do not** — restoring subgraph closure (every emitted edge's endpoints are in the slice's node set). This **reverses the prior "function slice includes containing-file imports" contract** (the three tests asserting it were rewritten to the new contract — a deliberate, human-ratified decision since no ADR/decision-register ruling covered it); `--exclude-imports` and file-entry slices are unaffected.
- **`depends_on` / `depends_on_manifest` edge-type descriptions corrected; the pair is documented as distinct *relationships*, not aliases (WI-dinih).** The registry descriptions were mislabeled — `depends_on` read "Generic dependency relationship" and `depends_on_manifest` read "Dependency declared in a package or build manifest," which made them look like synonyms (and the strategy's vocab:F1 prose called them an alias *pair* to fold). The code shows they are genuinely distinct: `depends_on` is a package/build manifest **declaring** its dependency (`json_config`/`make`/`meson`/… → dep, `src`=the manifest), while `depends_on_manifest` is the dependency linker **resolving** an importing source file to a manifest-declared dependency (`src`=the file, `evidence_type=import_to_manifest`) — different `src` kinds, evidence_type, and producer pass; folding them would conflate declaration with resolution. The descriptions now make declaration-vs-resolution legible and cross-reference each other, and a property test locks the keep-distinct verdict so a future fold is a deliberate decision rather than a silent re-litigation. (The broader edge_type `endpoint_shape` cluster retirement — 25 values incl. the deprecated bare `depends` — remains the separate ADR-0023 §6 migration.)
- **The `ast_call_method` evidence-type leak now folds to the `ast_call` apex + `meta["call_construct"]="method"` (vocab:F1, WI-nibis; audit-findings 0012 Cluster 28D).** The original Cluster 28D apex/peer collapse (audit-findings 0012) folded 28 call-construct `evidence_type` values to `ast_call` + structured `meta`, but the Python analyzer's method-call path carried a separate `evidence_type = "ast_call_method"` *default* (`py.py:3811`) that reached the **resolved** call edge unmigrated — ~5,000 edges on self-analysis, 97.9% of the parked `pending_classification` call-construct axis, which is why WI-nibis reopened. Resolved method calls now emit `evidence_type="ast_call"` + `meta["call_construct"]="method"` — matching the already-canonical *unresolved* method-call edges (`py.py:3926`+) that the original migration did fix — and `ast_call_method` is removed from the `EVIDENCE_TYPES` registry (regenerating `docs/schema.json` / `docs/concept-axes.md`, shrinking the schema-coverage baseline). Apex is `ast_call` per ADR-0028 Open Question 1 (decided in 0012), **not** the back-compat `ast_call_direct` a stale registry/spec note named — `ast_call_direct` denotes a *non-method* call. The L3 producer-coherence linter is the substrate-wide negative close-check (no producer may emit a non-registry value). Consumer-safe: no consumer branches on the `ast_call_method` string. The two remaining `AXIS_PENDING` call-construct peers (`ast_call_namespace`, `qualified_call`, both ternary producers, 0 self-corpus edges) are deferred to a follow-up.
- **IO-boundary classification now verifies the receiver before matching a method-kind primitive — a bare method name with no receiver evidence no longer phantom-matches (io-boundary:F3; INV-tapat, INV-maluk; cross-language + taint).** The catalog matcher's no-module-context fallback returned the first primitive registered under a short name, so an untyped `something.replace(...)` matched `pathlib.Path.replace` as `fs_write`, `x.read_text()` matched a filesystem read, etc. — the false-positive class INV-tapat (no receiver verification) and INV-maluk (`str.replace` ≠ `Path.replace`) describe. The previous band-aid (a hand-maintained `ambiguous_names` blocklist) only covered enumerated names and missed `read_text`/`exists`/`executeQuery`/… The fix is structural: a shared kind-aware gate (`io_boundary.gate_named_entry`) keys on the unused `IoPrimitive.kind` (`function`/`method`/`attribute`) plus the edge's `call_construct` — when there is no module hint, an untyped **method** call never matches (it has a receiver whose type is unknown), while a free-**function** call may still match a function-kind hit. Module-qualified calls (`os.listdir()`) are unaffected — they take the module-filter branch and never reach the gate; WI-fuvuj-typed receivers (`open()`→file, `socket.socket()`→socket) classify correctly. The gate is applied at **all three consumers** via one shared function: `IoBoundaryCatalog.lookup_with_module` (io-boundaries) and taint.py's `_lookup_named_entry`/propagation path (both `propagate_taint_structural` and `propagate_taint_ddg`) — closing the INV-maluk reopen over taint's `match_sink` third consumer (the ~5541-violation cascade). `ambiguous_names` is retained as the meta-absent / non-Python safety net (not removed this PR). Two catalog kind-mismarks the gate exposed were corrected (Kotlin top-level `println`/`print` and Scala companion-object factories `Source.fromFile`/… are function-style at the AST level → re-marked `functions:`). Behavioral evidence (`lookup_with_module` through the real path): `replace`/`read_text` with `call_construct=method` and no module → not tagged; the same with a `pathlib`/`pathlib.Path` hint → `fs_write`/`fs_read`; `os.listdir` bare → `fs_read` (function-kind preserved); `file.read` (WI-fuvuj) → `fs_read`. Tagged for bakeoff validation. Does **not** recover bare `builtins.open()` (blocked upstream — no edge is emitted for it today; WI-higaj/producer work).
- **Python method-style I/O on constructor-typed local variables now classifies into the correct boundary instead of the undifferentiated `external_potential` bucket (WI-fuvuj).** A call like `f.read()` / `s.send()` where the receiver is a local variable assigned from a known I/O constructor (`f = open(p)`, `s = socket.socket()`, including the idiomatic `with open(p) as f:` form) used to emit `dst = python:external:0-0:<name>:unresolved` with no module qualifier — the analyzer couldn't type the receiver, so the bare method name hit the catalog's `ambiguous_names` suppression (`read`/`write`/`send`/`recv` are deliberately ambiguous due to Python duck-typing) and the edge fell into `external_potential` rather than `fs_read`/`fs_write`/`net_send`/`net_recv`. The Python analyzer now tracks I/O-constructor-typed locals in a new `external_var_types` map (parallel to the existing in-repo-class `var_types`, same per-scope lifecycle) and emits a **module-qualified** dst + structured `dst_ref` (`python:file:0-0:read:unresolved` for file objects via a new synthetic `file` catalog module; `python:socket.socket:0-0:send:unresolved` against the existing socket entries) — carried in **both** the dst-id module slot and `dst_ref` because the io-boundaries CLI consumer drops `dst_ref` on serialize/reload. The qualified hint takes the catalog's module-filter path, bypassing `ambiguous_names` for *typed* receivers while **untyped** receivers stay suppressed (the safety net is untouched). Scope is deliberately bounded to `open`→file and `socket.socket`→socket plus the `with`/`async with` form (other stdlib constructors, aliased constructors, and binary/text-mode precision are deferred). End-to-end on a fixture: `read`→fs_read, `write`→fs_write, `connect`/`send`→net_send, `recv`→net_recv (previously all `external_potential`). This is the producer-side receiver evidence that the io-boundary:F3 receiver-tuple gate (INV-tapat/INV-maluk) consumes — sequenced first per the 2026-06-26 ruling so F3 closes those invariants *and* recovers real I/O recall (WI-ropob) rather than only suppressing.
- **The four framework-dispatch linkers now use the canonical test-file vocabulary instead of four divergent private copies (supply-verdict:F1, META-jotih).** `middleware_chain`, `router_routes`, `controller_routes` and `decorator_dispatch` each carried their own `_is_test_path` / `_TEST_PATH_SEGMENTS` to skip test symbols, but the copies were narrower than — and inconsistent with — the canonical `paths.is_test_file`: all four knew only `test_`-prefix / `_test.py` (router/controller added `_test.rb`/`_test.exs`; decorator even omitted `__tests__`), while none handled `spec_` / `.test.` / `.spec.` / `mock_` / `fake_` / `tests.rs` or the wider test-directory set. A test class in a `_spec.py` or `mock_*` file therefore leaked through as a production service/controller/route/dispatch concept. All four now consume the single canonical `paths.is_test_file(sym.path)` (the path-derivation form — deliberately *not* the runtime `Symbol.is_test_file` field, which is uniformly `False` at linker time since classification runs after the linkers). Strict superset: every pattern the private copies caught is still caught (`_test.rb`/`_test.exs` via canonical's generic `_test.` check). One source of truth for the test-file verdict these consumers were each re-deriving. Advances the META-jotih family (consumers ignoring the canonical `is_test_file`).
- **Content-scanning linkers no longer synthesize phantom production edges from pattern strings sitting in TEST files — a shared test-file scan gate (linker-evidence-gating:F1; WI-nitob, WI-rilin).** Fifteen Tier-2 edge-recovery linkers (message-queue, database-query, http, graphql, graphql-resolver, grpc, websocket, orm, di-resolution, openapi, ipc, otp, phoenix-ipc, go-cobra, go-memberlist) self-scan the filesystem via `discovery.find_files` and regex-match framework patterns in file *content*, but none gated on whether the scanned file is a test — so a Kafka topic / SQL table / RPC name appearing as a string literal in a fixture or assertion produced a phantom *production* edge. WI-nitob: the message-queue linker synthesized **103 `event_publishes`** from MQ pattern strings in three test-file bodies, cross-linking 26 unrelated test files by topic; WI-rilin: the database-query linker emitted phantom cross-package table `references` sourced entirely from its own test files. A single shared `discovery.find_non_test_files` wrapper now drops test-classified paths (via the canonical `paths.is_test_file`) at the one discovery chokepoint, and every self-scanning linker consumes it instead of `find_files` — so the gate lives in one place rather than as N divergent (or absent) private copies. `event_sourcing` (which already gated inline) was migrated onto the shared helper for a single source of truth. `grpc`'s `.proto`-existence count keeps `find_files` (it emits nothing per file), and `subprocess-cli` is deliberately excluded — a `subprocess.run([...])` in an integration test is a *real* CLI invocation, not fixture data, and detecting test-suite invocations of the project's own CLI is that linker's intended behavior. Because the skip decision lives in the wrapper, wiring each linker is a no-new-branch change. WI-nitob closed; WI-rilin's test-sourced face drained (its missing cross-package scoping is a separate residual).
- **Monorepo workspace siblings are no longer mislabelled as third-party tier-2 "direct dependencies" in the Python dependency manifest (INV-nuzas, first half / supply-verdict:F3, ADR-0041 D8a).** `parse_python_dependencies` collected every `pyproject.toml`'s `[project].dependencies` and stamped each resolved import name `{"direct": True}` (→ tier 2). In a monorepo, sibling packages declare each other as dependencies, so a package's own first-party code (`hypergumbo_core`, `hypergumbo_lang_mainstream`, …) landed in the manifest and the boundary classifier stamped it as an *external* tier-2 "direct dependency" — the "tier-2 direct-dependency lie" (leverage #27). The parser now also reads each package's **own** distribution name (PEP 621 `[project].name`, Poetry `[tool.poetry].name`, via the new `_extract_distribution_name` helper), resolves those names through the same dist→import path, and subtracts them from the resolved import-name set right after the existing stdlib carve-out — so workspace members are removed from the tier-2 set rather than mislabelled as externals. Third-party deps are untouched (a `click`/`rich` with no matching workspace package stays tier-2 direct). This is the workspace-subtraction half of INV-nuzas; the in-tree module-resolution half (so the imports resolve to real in-repo nodes instead of becoming boundary nodes at all) is the separate supply-verdict:F4. Orthogonal to the tier-2-vs-tier-3 reassignment of genuine third-party deps (supply-verdict:F5 / ADR-0041 §1) — F3 only removes the first-party rows.
- **Python imports of in-tree workspace-sibling modules now resolve to the real first-party file node instead of dangling to a phantom `external_symbol` boundary (INV-nuzas, second half / supply-verdict:F4).** The Python analyzer emits one `imports` edge per import statement, but two forms produced a dangling dst that `create_boundary_nodes` then minted as an `external_symbol` (`path='<external>'`, mis-tiered): (A) `import pkg.sub.mod` (`ast.Import`) did **zero** symbol lookup — every plain-import dst was an unconditional external module reference; (B) `from pkg import submod` (`ast.ImportFrom`) where `submod` is a *submodule* (not a symbol re-exported from `__init__`) failed the symbol lookup and fell through to an external `:symbol` ref. Both reduce to "the import names an in-tree **module**, but no module→node resolution existed." The analyzer (which already builds `global_symbols` across the whole repo) now also builds a `module_to_file_id` map — every in-tree dotted module name → its first-party file-anchor id, plus the package name for each `__init__.py` — and `_extract_import_edges` consults it: `ast.Import` resolves the full dotted name; `ast.ImportFrom`'s external fallback tries `{module}.{name}` (the submodule case) then `{module}` (an in-tree module whose symbol wasn't pinned), pointing the edge at the real file node (`is_resolved=True`, no `ExternalRef`). The existing symbol lookup still wins first, so `from pkg import Symbol` keeps its precise symbol-node target. Genuine third-party modules are absent from the map, so `import os` / `from numpy import array` stay external (verified). **Behavioral evidence (hypergumbo self-analysis, before→after):** `imports` edges mis-routed to `python:hypergumbo_*` phantom externals **366 → 0**; `hypergumbo_*` modules mislabelled `internal_dep` **144 → 0** (internal_dep symbol count 480 → 176); phantom `hypergumbo_*` external nodes **331 → 116**. The residual 116 are reached **only** by `calls` (339) and `module_attr_ref` (106) edges — a distinct cross-module call/reference-resolution gap (dispatch:F3 / WI-noham, currently blocked), **not** import resolution; INV-nuzas's import facet is closed but the invariant stays open on that call-edge residual. Documented scope-out: a PEP-420 namespace package (no `__init__.py`) bare `import nspkg` has no file anchor for the package name and stays external (a submodule `import nspkg.mod` of a real file still resolves); cross-language analogues (Go/Java/Kotlin in-tree module imports) are a separate follow-up. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Supply-chain tier now names distance only — declared third-party deps drop from tier 2 to tier 3, and the direct/transitive/undeclared distinction moves to a `directness` meta stamp (supply-verdict:F5; ADR-0041 §1/§2).** `DependencyManifest.classify_import` returned `INTERNAL_DEP` (tier 2) for any import declared in a project manifest, and the boundary-node synthesizer relabeled those external nodes to tier 2 with `"direct dependency (…)"` reason strings — so a third-party PyPI/Go/Maven package got tier 2 *simply for being declared*, while the standard library (declared nowhere) sat at tier 3. The `TIER_WEIGHTS` ranking (`{1: 2.0, 2: 1.5, 3: 1.0}`) therefore weighted an arbitrary declared dependency **above** the stdlib — a trust inversion. Per ADR-0041 §1, `tier` now names supply-chain *distance* and nothing else: `classify_import` returns `EXTERNAL_DEP` for every third-party import (direct, transitive, undeclared, stdlib alike), and the boundary tier-min relabel + its reason strings are retired (boundary nodes are uniformly tier 3, reason `"unresolved external reference"`). The declaration relationship the classifier still knows is re-emitted (§2) as a new registered `directness` meta key — `direct` (declared in a manifest), `transitive` (in the manifest but not direct), or `undeclared` (imported but declared nowhere; also where stdlib lands) — stamped once at the classification chokepoint (`create_boundary_nodes`) via the new `DependencyManifest.classify_directness`. **Self-analysis evidence:** the 176 tier-2 `internal_dep` boundary symbols (all genuine third-party — `yaml`, `rich`, `pytest`, `tree_sitter_*`, `starlette`, `uvicorn`, `bcrypt`, …) reclassify to tier 3, so no `TIER_WEIGHTS` consumer can weight a PyPI package above the stdlib again. Scope: this retires only the *manifest* direct-dep → tier-2 mapping; file classification independently still routes in-repo example/documentation/config-internal role files to tier 2 (a separate axiom-conformance follow-up), and the `ecosystem` (stdlib vs third_party) axis is supply-verdict:F6. spec §14 tier table rewritten. Tagged for bakeoff validation.
- **Tier-3 external boundary nodes now carry an `ecosystem` provenance stamp (stdlib vs third_party), and `supply_chain_summary` sub-buckets externals by it (supply-verdict:F6; ADR-0041 §3).** Completing the ADR-0041 tier trilogy (§1 distance-only tier + §2 directness, both supply:F5): with all third-party at tier 3, the residual stdlib-vs-third_party distinction that supply-chain triage actually needs is now a registered `ecosystem` meta key on boundary nodes. It is sourced from the **single mandated catalog** — `io_boundary.IoBoundaryCatalog.is_stdlib_module` (the same per-language stdlib enumeration the io-boundary closed-world gates consume), injected into `create_boundary_nodes` as an `ecosystem_classifier` so `ir.py` stays free of an `io_boundary` dependency (mirroring the existing `dependency_manifest` injection). A boundary node stamps `ecosystem=stdlib` when the module is in the language's enumerated stdlib, `third_party` when it is not **and** the language has an enumerated stdlib (so "not stdlib" cannot be misread as third_party on a language whose stdlib set is unknown), and nothing when there is no enumeration. `_compute_supply_chain_summary` adds an `ecosystem` sub-object under `external_dep` counting tier-3 symbols by class (`stdlib`/`third_party`/`unknown`). The key is deliberately distinct from the pre-existing `package_ecosystem` (which names the package-*manager* registry — npm/composer — of package-shaped symbols); this one names provenance *class*. Self-analysis: tier-3 boundary nodes split 1172 `undeclared`-directness / mostly stdlib+third_party ecosystem; the triage payoff is that a `third_party` vuln (pin the package) is now distinguishable from a `stdlib` one (upgrade the runtime). spec §14 updated.
- **Solidity `inherits` / `overrides` / event-`emit` references edges no longer inherit the call-specific `ast_call_direct` evidence_type default (vocab:F2 / WI-lojug).** `Edge.create`'s `evidence_type` defaults to `ast_call_direct`; three Solidity analyzer producers built non-call edges without passing the kwarg, so a contract inheritance (`contract Token is IToken`), a function `override`, and an `emit Event(...)` reference were all stamped as direct function calls — corrupting the evidence_type axis (a consumer selecting "all call sites" by `evidence_type == ast_call_direct` would wrongly pull in inheritance/override/reference edges, and the forthcoming evidence→confidence derivation layer would assign call-band confidence to them). Each now passes the accurate **registered** inference-pathway value: `inherits` → `ast_extends` (AST `is`/inheritance clause), `overrides` → `type_hierarchy` (resolved by name-matching against the inheritance hierarchy), event-emit `references` → `reference` (a name-resolved reference to the event symbol). Investigation found WI-lojug's original 326-edge count was **stale**: the decorator-dispatch (292) and rust-trait-dispatch (1) `dispatches_to` edges now explicitly carry `ast_call_direct` + `meta.framework_dispatch` — the canonical post-fold form per ADR-0028 Phase 3 / audit-findings 0014, not a leak — leaving these 3 Solidity edges as the only genuine *default* leaks (self-corpus `Contract.sol`: 1 inherits + 1 overrides + 1 event-emit reference). The remaining explicit `ast_call_direct` on websocket-endpoint `references` edges is a distinct (non-default) producer-correctness question filed separately; the structural "make evidence_type a required Edge.create param" hardening is a tracked follow-up.
- **Closure-factory decorators' returned inner closures are now reachable in the call graph instead of being false-positive "dead" (INV-pohik symptom 3, closure-factory leg / dispatch:F8 traversal half).** A Python function that returns its own directly-nested function (the `register_analyzer`-style `def register(...): def decorator(func): ...; return decorator`) left the nested `decorator` with **zero** reachability in-edges (`calls`/`dispatches_to`/`wraps`), so `dead-code-maybe` flagged it dead and `slice`/features couldn't reach it — even though it's invoked wherever the factory's result is applied. The Python analyzer now detects `return <bare-name>` where the name resolves (via the existing INV-mofav `nested_by_parent_id` index, keyed on the factory's symbol id) to one of the function's **directly-nested** defs, and emits a `dispatches_to` edge factory→closure with `meta["dispatch_kind"]="closure_factory"` (evidence_type `function_reference`), so the closure inherits the factory's reachability. Scope is deliberately narrow — only a bare-`Name` return of a directly-nested def counts (a returned parameter, call, attribute, import, or sibling top-level function emits nothing), and returns inside the factory's own `if`/`try` blocks are collected while nested function/class scopes are skipped. Self-analysis (`analyze/` scope): 3 closure factories de-orphaned (`register_analyzer`→`decorator`, `make_file_finder`→`finder`, `as_registered_analyzer`→`analyze_fn`); `register_analyzer.decorator` reachability in-edges 0→1. This is the **traversal half** of dispatch:F8; the descriptor-protocol leg (no self-repro — synthetic fixtures) and the umbrella **closure-gate property test** that flips INV-pohik to `satisfied` land in the F8 follow-up. F8 is one of the two structural fixes in INV-pohik's `resolves_ids` (with F1).
- **`dead-code-maybe` now consumes `cross_language_hits` as a false-positive demoter instead of leaving it purely a rank signal (WI-gavub / dispatch:F7).** Each dead candidate already carried a `cross_language_hits` count (how many *other-language* files mention its name as a string), but it was never used to *remove* candidates — so framework-dispatched symbols like Lit's `*.render` (called from HTML/CSS, CLH≈94) or an HTTP/RPC/FFI name referenced from another language stayed in the "potentially dead" list as noise. A candidate whose name appears in **≥ `--cross-lang-threshold`** (default 3) other-language files is now excluded from the dead set as a near-certain cross-language-dispatch / missing-edge false positive, and the count is reported as `demoted_cross_language` in the JSON summary (mirroring `demoted_view_func` / `demoted_dispatch_inherited`). The threshold stays above 1 so a single coincidental hit does not exclude — some genuine dead code has a low CLH (WI-gavub's calibration concern); `--cross-lang-threshold 0` disables the demoter entirely. Implemented as a hard exclusion mirroring the existing `dispatch_inherited_ids` demoter (no per-candidate dead-confidence score exists yet to demote proportionally). Symptom-FP-reduction that advances INV-pohik's default-seed-FP gate without traversing new edges; not in INV-pohik's `resolves_ids`.
- **`dead-code-maybe` now defaults to `--seeds production` (entrypoints + exported public API) and discloses the cohorts the headline used to silently fold away — retiring the ~89%-false-positive default (WI-lirob / WI-jufih / dispatch:F2, per the 2026-06-10 ruling).** The documented default was `--seeds entrypoints`, which seeds only ~100 declared entrypoints and so flagged ~89% of production functions as "potentially dead" on a plugin-registration-heavy codebase — a single `--seeds all` flip dropped that ~55pp, yet the help text never marked the flag load-bearing. The new default seed set is **`production`** = entrypoints ∪ exported public API (`supply_chain.is_exported`), so externally-callable code is no longer reported dead. The strict entrypoint-only view "survives as a labeled cohort, not the default": the JSON/text summary now reports two disclosure buckets — `entrypoint_only_dead` (how many the old strict view would flag) and `test_only_reachable` (production functions dead under the default but reachable once test code is seeded — the WI-jufih dead-code-vs-coverage contradiction, now surfaced rather than absorbed). Omitting `--seeds` prints a one-line stderr note pointing at the buckets; `--seeds` gained a `production` choice and defaults to a sentinel so an *omitted* flag is distinguishable from an explicit `--seeds entrypoints` (which still produces the strict view, unchanged, with no note). The existing `entrypoints`/`tests`/`exports`/`all` modes are unchanged. The inline production/test/export symbol classification was extracted into a reusable `production_callables()` helper (docs-prose:F4, seam g). This is symptom-exposure reduction, not a call-graph traversal fix — INV-pohik's structural symptoms (route edges, closure-factory dispatch) are F1/F8; F2 is excluded from INV-pohik's `resolves_ids`.
- **Top-level functions and module-level variables are now rooted at their file anchor via `contains` edges, instead of being containment-tree orphans (INV-pohik de-orphan / dispatch:F4).** `CONTAINABLE_KINDS` (the containment linker's set of kinds that can be *contained*) held only `method`/`getter`/`setter`/`message`, so a top-level `def` or a module-level `var` — which carries a bare name that Phase-1 naming-convention can't match — got no `contains` edge at all, leaving the file's containment tree rootless and `slice` unable to expand "this file's symbols". `function` and `variable` are added to the set; the linker's Phase-2 span_overlap fallback now attaches each to the `kind="file"` anchor (file-anchor:F1) whose span encloses it. Whole-repo self-analysis: ~8.8k new file→member `contains` edges (7,062 to functions, 1,758 to variables); orphan functions 93 → 14 and orphan variables 130 → 0 (orphan ceilings ratcheted 240→30 / 120→15). A *dotted* function name (the JS `userController.createUser` route-handler idiom) can still match a same-file container via Phase 1 — accepted, since the dotted name genuinely qualifies a real container when one exists and emits nothing when none does. The `dead-code-maybe` false-positive rate is **unaffected** — its reachability BFS traverses `calls`/`dispatches_to`/`wraps`, never `contains`. (A separate, pre-existing breach surfaced while measuring this — the env-gated orphan ratchet's `file` ceiling and overall ratio are exceeded by last session's nodeless file anchors — is tracked under WI-logon, not adjusted here.)
- **Route → handler dispatch now lands as a traversable `dispatches_to` edge instead of being stranded in metadata — `:route` feature graphs are no longer empty (INV-pohik symptom 2 / dispatch:F1).** A route symbol records its handler as `meta["handler_ref"]` (a full symbol id, which the route_handler linker resolves by id for the "direct" case). But `_relativize_ir_paths` rewrote `Symbol.id`/`path`, `Edge.src`/`dst`, and `UsageContext` fields while never touching `Symbol.meta` — so after the repo-root prefix was stripped from every symbol id, the route's `handler_ref` still held the *absolute* handler id. The linker (which runs after relativization) builds its `symbol_by_id` index from the relativized ids, so `symbol_by_id.get(handler_ref)` always missed and the `dispatches_to` edge was never emitted; a BFS/feature-slice from a route seed returned an empty edge set (7 of 8 `:route` feature graphs empty on self-analysis). `_relativize_ir_paths` now also relativizes string values in `Symbol.meta` that carry the repo-root prefix (the canonical case being `handler_ref`), so the linker resolves the handler by id and emits the edge. Whole-repo self-analysis: route→handler `dispatches_to` edges 0 → 8, empty `:route` feature graphs 7/8 → 0/8 (each now carries 5–21 edges). Short-name refs (Express `handler_ref`, `view_name`) and non-string meta values never carry the prefix and are left untouched. This is one of the two structural fixes in INV-pohik's `resolves_ids` (with dispatch:F8); the `dead-code-maybe` false-positive headline is unaffected (that is dispatch:F2).
- **`hypergumbo explain` now delivers its documented contract — `--provenance` has a visible effect and section headers match edge semantics (INV-rarol / cli-output:F3).** Two defects fixed: (1) `--provenance` was byte-identical to the no-flag form for the analyzer-produced edges that dominate most symbols (`derived_from` is recorded only on linker-inferred edges, so most edges showed nothing) — it now prints a marker (`(no derivation chain — analyzer-produced edge)`) so the flag is never invisible; extending `derived_from` to every edge remains the deferred structural half (declared-fields:F5). (2) The `Called by` / `Calls` headers summed *mixed* edge types — a `Called by (N)` count lumped containers, instantiators, and references in with actual callers — so explain now partitions each direction into **per-type sections** with relationship-accurate labels (`Called by` counts only `calls`; `Instantiated by` lists `instantiates`; `Contained by` lists `contains`; unmapped types fall back to a direction-qualified canonical-name label), making each section's count match its entries' semantics.
- **`cross_language` removed as a stored edge.meta field — it's derived from endpoint node languages, not stored (INV-zigup / ADR-0023 derivable-from-endpoints rule).** The flag was set inline by 7 bridge linkers (http / websocket / graphql / graphql_resolver / event_sourcing / database_query / message_queue), yet it was simultaneously under-populated (the dependency and html linkers never set it), unreliable (synthetic destination nodes with `language=None` made `src.lang != dst.lang` meaningless), and noisy (redundant `False` on same-language edges). `cross_language` is *purely derivable* from the two endpoints' languages — both already in the output — and has **zero internal consumers**, so per ADR-0023's rule (a property *not* derivable from endpoints earns a field; derivable ones are folded out) it is removed rather than centrally stamped. The 7 linkers no longer write it (the local cross-language check that legitimately adjusts confidence at link time in http/message_queue is kept); consumers compute cross-language-ness on demand from node languages. The `language=None` synthetic-node gap is now cleanly just a node-language-completeness concern (ADR-0031), not a `cross_language` reliability bug.
- **`<script src>` migrates from the endpoint-shape `script_src` edge_type to `references` + `meta["construct"]="script_src"`; `inherits`/`overrides` confirmed canonical, not vestigial (INV-vavat / ADR-0023).** INV-vavat filed three single-occurrence edge.type values as "vestigial singletons to fold," but a producer audit showed the singleton-ness was a *corpus artifact*: `inherits` (Solidity `is` / BitBake `inherit`) and `overrides` (Solidity `override`) are genuinely-distinct relationships that barely appear because the Python-heavy dogfood corpus has ~no Solidity/BitBake — they're **kept** (ADR-0023 protects them; `overrides` was additionally *added* to ADR-0023's named not-deprecate list for clarity). Only `script_src` was a real issue, and it's a known migration not a fold: it sat on `AXIS_ENDPOINT_SHAPE` (a construct, not a relationship). The html analyzer now emits `edge_type="references"` with the construct on a new registered `construct` edge.meta key (replacing the prior `framework_dispatch="html_script_src"` misuse). `script_src` stays registered-as-deprecated per the established endpoint-shape migration convention (cf. `http_calls`/`grpc_calls`).
- **`call_construct` no longer smuggles file-locality — bash/objc cross-file calls move to a new `call_locality` edge.meta key (INV-vakaf).** The bash and Objective-C analyzers set `edge.meta["call_construct"]="cross_file"` on cross-file calls, but `call_construct` names the *syntactic* call shape (method/function/pipe/…), not *where the endpoints live* — two unrelated axes through one field. File-locality now lives on its own registered `edge.meta` key **`call_locality`** (values `same_file`/`cross_file`, declared per ADR-0024), stamped sparsely (`cross_file` on boundary-crossing calls; absent = same-file, the common case). The two producer sites no longer write `call_construct` at all.
- **`hypergumbo config <X>` now rejects non-language names instead of silently returning an all-null config (INV-gufod).** `config` reads per-language config files, but accepted ANY string — passing a framework or linker name (the repro: `config airflow-framework-dispatch-linker`) or a typo returned all-null sections with only a stderr warning and exit 0, indistinguishable to a script from a genuinely empty config. It now validates the argument against the language catalog (`all_known_languages()`): an unknown name errors with exit 2 and a difflib "Did you mean" suggestion for near-misses. A real language that merely has no config yaml stays valid (exit 0 + the existing "no configuration" warning) — only non-languages are rejected.
- **`hypergumbo slice` JSON now echoes `min_confidence` in `feature.query`, closing the last reproducibility gap (INV-rakot).** The `feature.query` field exists so a slice can be reproduced from its JSON alone, but `min_confidence` was the one influential flag never echoed (`max_tier`/`language`/`hub_threshold`/`exclude_imports` were backfilled earlier). A non-zero `--min-confidence` is now included in the query echo (omitted at the `0.0` default, where it does not filter — keeping the `feature_id` hash stable for confidence-unfiltered slices). A round-trip guard test (`test_query_to_dict_echoes_all_influential_params_invrakot`) asserts every CLI-influential param appears in the echo, catching the drift class where a newly-added flag is forgotten in `to_dict()`.
- **`hypergumbo search` now reports the TOTAL match count and rejects non-positive `--limit` values (INV-toniv).** The result header read `len(matches)` *after* the `matches[:limit]` slice, so it always printed `min(total, limit)` — a search for a common term reported "Found 20" when 1468 matched, hiding the real total. And a negative `--limit` was silently interpreted as Python tail-drop slicing (`--limit -5` → `matches[:-5]`, dropping the last 5), while `--limit 0` fell through the falsy guard as "no limit". Now the header reports the total and discloses truncation (`Found 1468 symbol(s) matching 'Symbol' (showing 20):`), and `--limit` uses a positive-integer argparse type factory (`_positive_result_limit`) that rejects `<= 0` and non-numeric values with `exit 2` and a clear message instead of mis-slicing.
- **`hypergumbo symbols` no longer truncates multi-digit In/Deg column values to `"10…"` on a default terminal (INV-ripoh, incl. the pass-31 In-column face).** The numeric columns (In/Out/Deg) carried no `min_width`, and a fixed `_SYMBOLS_INNER_COLUMNS_OVERHEAD = 30` under-budgeted the console width once In/Deg hit 4 digits — so Rich proportionally squeezed Deg into `"19…"`, silently destroying the connectivity rank the command exists to surface (a 1939-degree symbol read as `19`). The width is now DATA-DRIVEN: each inner column's width is computed from its widest cell (floored by the header label), that width both sets the column's `min_width` (so Rich can't squeeze it) and feeds the forced console `required_width` (so honoring the min-widths doesn't overflow Symbol/File). Verified across 4- and 5-digit degrees and long kind names; the workaround `--col-width 1000` is no longer needed for connectivity-heavy symbols.
- **The nodeless config/doc cohort now gets `kind="file"` anchors too, and every additional-file centrality key is a real node path — closing the split file universe (WI-rajod; file-anchor:F1+F4 co-release).** WI-dagif anchored the *node-bearing* cohort (source files with content nodes); this closes the other half. Config/doc files that carry **zero** content nodes (a `README.md`, a `config.yaml`, a `package.json` with no analyzable symbols) previously had no node of any kind in the behavior map, yet they *were* keys in the sketch's additional-file centrality surface — so the two file universes (centrality-key paths vs file-node paths) shared **0 intersection** (the WI-rajod invariant violation). **F1** mints a `kind="file"` anchor for each Additional-File candidate at the `all_analyzers` orchestrator chokepoint (the only pre-`finalize()` site that can both see every discovered file via `FileIndex.all_files()` and reach the symbol list before nodes are serialized), reusing the WI-dagif generic minter (`synthesize_file_anchors_for_paths`) and a new shared selector `taxonomy.additional_file_candidates` (CONFIG/DOC role, resolvable language, not a content-source path, not hidden, not excluded). **F4** splits the centrality surface: the candidate-selection that feeds the scores now subtracts only *content* paths (`content_source_paths` = symbol paths with `kind != "file"`), NOT the file anchors F1 just minted — without this carve-out, F1's anchors would re-key as content and empty the Additional-Files surface. Because both the anchor minter and the score producer call the *same* `additional_file_candidates` selector, every centrality key is now guaranteed to be a real file-anchor node path (the subset invariant holds by construction). HIGH-blast-radius (adds file anchors for config/doc files corpus-wide) — bakeoff validation pending (WI-rajod).
- **Every path that carries content nodes now gets a `kind="file"` anchor — closing the rootless-`contains`-tree gap for node-bearing paths (WI-dagif; file-anchor:F1, node-bearing slice).** A source file whose analyzer emits content symbols but no `make_file_id`-shaped edge (e.g. a Java/C#/Rust file with only a class + fields and no imports/calls, or a generated module) previously got no file-kind anchor node, so its `contains` tree was rootless (content nodes with no file root) and `slice` could not expand "this file's symbols". This is one face of the split file universe (centrality-key paths vs file-node paths share 0 intersection — WI-rajod). A new orchestrator-chokepoint pass `synthesize_file_anchors_for_node_bearing_paths` (sibling to the WI-ramuv dangling-edge synthesizer, run right after it) mints one `kind="file"` anchor per node-bearing path lacking one, with a real `span.end_line` so the containment linker's span-based pass roots the file's top-level members at it (no separate `contains`-edge emission needed; the two synthesizers now share one `_read_file_end_line` helper and one synthesis `AnalysisRun`). **Scope is deliberately the node-bearing cohort**: those paths already carry content nodes, so they are already in `source_paths` and minting their anchors cannot empty the Additional-Files surface — which makes this slice safe to land *independently* of file-anchor:F4 (the centrality surface split). The nodeless cohort (yaml/markdown/config files with zero nodes) and the full file-universe unification remain the F1+F4 co-release, gated on the WI-rajod-invariant human decision (correctness-strategy §6.6).
- **The Java analyzer now emits an `imports` edge for every import declaration — the last imports-emission holdout among mainstream analyzers (INV-gojit).** Java extracted import declarations only into name-resolution dicts (`_extract_imports` and siblings build simple-name → FQN maps for call canonicalization) and never surfaced them as graph edges, so a Java file emitted `edge_types == [calls]` while every other mainstream analyzer (python/js/ts/go/rust/csharp/swift) emitted ≥1 `imports` edge — verified general, not stdlib-specific (zero edges on both `import java.util.List` and `import com.example.helper.Formatter`). The new `_extract_import_edges` emits one `imports` edge per declaration from the file node to a structured `ExternalRef` (aligned with ADR-0037's push to populate `dst_ref`). The module slot carries the **full import specifier** — a Java `import` names a fully-qualified type or static member, not a package, so the specifier as written IS the module path (at parity with Go/C#, whose import edges also carry the full path; the structured `ExternalRef` and the simple name in the name slot are richer than Go/C#'s literal `package`/`namespace`). This matters downstream: `refine_frameworks` prefix-matches import modules against framework patterns, and the full path resolves both package-level patterns (`org.springframework.boot` ← `import org.springframework.boot.X`) and type-level patterns (`android.app.activity` ← exact). regular `import com.example.Foo;` → module `com.example.Foo`/name `Foo`; `import static a.b.C.member;` → module `a.b.C.member`/name `member` (registered `import_static` evidence type); wildcard `import a.b.*;` → module `a.b`/name `*`. Confidence 0.95 at sibling parity; `src` is the `make_file_id` file node (backstopped by the WI-ramuv dangling-endpoint synthesizer). Because Java now emits import edges, Java/Kotlin frameworks also begin participating in `refine_frameworks`' prod-vs-dev demotion (previously exempt via the "languages without import edges" carve-out) — the full-path module representation is what keeps a real Spring app's `spring-boot` confirmed rather than demoted. **Strips the last `(java, edge_imports)` strict-xfail hole** from the emission-parity matrix gate (now a hard lock) — the gap the gate itself surfaced when it landed.
- **Go module-variable emission no longer leaks function-/block-local `var` declarations (INV-sidab).** The symbol walk visits every AST node, so a `var x = …` inside a function or block body (parent `statement_list`) was emitted as `kind="variable"` exactly like a package-level `var` — despite the emit block's own comment claiming "package-level var aliases". Surfaced by the F5 DEEP-bakeoff adversarial ground-truth audit (caddy: a function-local `var handler` in a `_test.go` body classified as a module variable). Emission is now gated on the `var_declaration`'s direct parent being `source_file` (package/file scope); interface-assertion detection (`var _ I = &S{}`) still runs for every var_spec regardless of scope (unchanged). Verified on caddy: go `variable` 98→89 — the 9 dropped are all genuine function-locals (`var body io.Reader = resp.Body` in a method, `var ptr any = rawCfg`, and test-body `handler`/`next`/`w`/`unhealthy`); 0 package-level vars dropped. Pre-existing (predates the WI-jusus F5 Go struct-field slice); same scope-conflation class as the swift INV-lanaz fix.
- **Swift field emission no longer misclassifies method-/init-/closure-local `let`/`var` as type fields (INV-lanaz; regression in the WI-jusus F5 swift slice).** The non-computed `property_declaration` branch decided field-eligibility from `_get_enclosing_type(node)` being truthy — but a local binding inside a *method* of a type also parses as `property_declaration` and *does* have an enclosing type, so it was emitted as `kind="field"` named `Type.local`. A DEEP-bakeoff adversarial ground-truth audit found **383 of 407** emitted swift "fields" on SwiftyJSON were XCTest method-body locals (e.g. `let json = JSON(array)` inside `func testSingleDimensionalArraysGetter()`), with only ~10 genuine stored properties; the 100%-coverage unit tests and the emission-parity matrix gate missed it because their fixtures used only clean stored properties declared directly in a type body. A stored property is now recognized by its **direct** parent being a type-body node (`class_body` for class/struct/actor/extension, `enum_class_body` for enum) — `enclosing_type` is computed only in that case; top-level (`source_file`-parent) bindings stay `kind="variable"`, and method/init/closure-local bindings (parent `statements`) are skipped (the module-level-only contract the other variable emitters already honor). Behavior is otherwise unchanged (the field/variable split, naming, qualified name, and stable_id are identical for genuine stored properties and top-level bindings). Verified on SwiftyJSON: swift `field` 407→19 (all 19 genuine stored properties — the `JSON` struct's `rawArray`/`rawDictionary`/`type`/… plus test-class fixtures and a `CodableModel` struct), `variable` unchanged at 46, and the two dropped `Source/` entries (`let object` in `init(data:)`, `var merged` in `merged(with:)`) confirmed method-locals.
- **The 11 doc/markup/template analyzers now mint `node.id` and `stable_id` together via one shared `make_doc_symbol_ids` helper, ending the drift class WI-rijup exposed and fixing a latent same-name-sibling id collision (INV-dulah DRY refactor).** WI-rijup canonicalized `stable_id` across these analyzers but left each one's near-duplicate per-analyzer `_make_symbol_id` raw-id builder in place, with `stable_id` minted by a *separate* `make_doc_stable_id` call — exactly the two-places-for-one-identity split that let nine of them ship `stable_id == raw id` in the first place. New `make_doc_symbol_ids(language, path, kind, name, start_line, end_line) -> (node_id, stable_id)` in `analyze/base.py` mints both from one argument set (`node_id = "{language}:{path}:{kind}:{start_line}:{name}"`; `stable_id` via the existing `make_doc_stable_id`), and the eleven copies (`rst`, `robot`, `scss`, `vue`, `svelte`, `puppet`, `astro`, `pony`, `twig`, `sparql`, `kdl`) are deleted in favor of it. For the **six line-bearing** analyzers plus `kdl` the `node.id` is **byte-identical** (their builders already emitted `{lang}:{path}:{kind}:{start_line}:{name}` with `line == span.start_line`, verified per call site). For the **four line-less** analyzers (`rst`, `robot`, `pony`, `sparql`) `node.id` **gains the `start_line` segment** — a deliberate change that both moves the id toward the documented grammar and resolves their latent collision (two same-name same-kind siblings in one file, e.g. two `section`s, previously shared a `node.id`). `stable_id` is unchanged at every site. The sparql `uses_vocabulary` edge `dst` now references the prefix node's own (now line-bearing) `id` directly — the prefix Symbol is indexed by name in Pass 1 so Pass 2 resolves it — instead of re-minting a line-less stand-in that would no longer match. The markdown and gitignore analyzers are **not** folded in: they already use a different, more grammar-conformant id shape (`{lang}:{path}:{start-end}:{name}:{kind}`), so adopting this helper would be a slot-reorder breaking change out of this refactor's scope. (Separately noted on INV-dulah: `robot`'s `test_case` node has long carried id/stable_id kind-slot `"test_case"` while `Symbol.kind == "test"` — a pre-existing kind-slot/kind discrepancy this refactor faithfully preserves, not a regression.)
- **Four more `stable_id` producers canonicalized — sparql, kdl, the graphql client-call linker, and the SCIP indexer — closing the residual WI-rijup's grep missed (WI-sojop, INV-hunup).** iter-002 validation of WI-rijup (which fixed 9 doc analyzers) confirmed those moved but surfaced a wider residual: a codebase-wide scope-expansion found four producers still emitting non-canonical `stable_id`. **sparql** (3 sites) and **kdl** carried the exact WI-rijup antipattern (`stable_id=symbol_id` over the raw composite) — two analyzers the original grep didn't sweep; both migrated to `make_doc_stable_id`. The **graphql client-call linker** (`linkers/graphql.py`) self-stamped `stable_id=call.operation_name` on its Class-B (`language=None`, `protocol_origin="graphql"`) `graphql_client` stand-in — both non-canonical *and* collision-prone (GraphQL operation names repeat across files, and bare `operation_name` is exactly the "wrong, colliding value" the `make_synthetic_symbol_identity` chokepoint was built to prevent). Fixed by emitting `stable_id=None` so the existing post-linker `populate_synthetic_class_b_identity` pass stamps a canonical injective id keyed on `(protocol_origin, kind, path, name, occurrence)` — the same path the graphql *resolver* linker already used (Phase 6 PR1); the operation name is preserved in `meta`. The **SCIP indexer** (`scip/index.py`) set `stable_id=sym_info.symbol` (the raw SCIP moniker); since the moniker is a globally-stable identity, it is now hashed via `_short_sha256` to the canonical `sha256:<16hex>` form, with the moniker preserved in `meta["scip_symbol"]`. A parallel investigation (5 read-only facet probes) settled the design hash-to-canonical over document-exempt: `stable_id` is a foreign key with a HARD per-file uniqueness check (unlike `fingerprint`, which is never a key and *can* safely exempt `language=None`); 18+/20 synthetic/external/linker producers already hash; and the SCIP node carries a real language (so a `language=None` exemption wouldn't even cover it). `Symbol.id` is unchanged at every site. iter-002 also confirmed the WI-rijup fix moved for the right reason (pretix 1223→0, chatwoot 12664→0 nonconforming `stable_id`; node counts byte-identical; node.id unchanged; 0 new collisions).
- **Nine doc/markup/template analyzers now emit canonical `sha256:<16hex>` `stable_id`s instead of reusing the raw composite `Symbol.id` (WI-rijup, INV-hunup).** The `rst`, `scss`, `vue`, `svelte`, `puppet`, `robot`, `astro`, `twig` and `pony` analyzers each built a raw composite node id (`{lang}:{path}:{kind}[:{line}]:{name}`) via a per-analyzer `_make_symbol_id` helper and then set `stable_id=<that raw id>` — so every symbol they emitted carried a `stable_id` that violated the documented `^sha256:[0-9a-f]{16}$` contract (`stable_id_scheme: hypergumbo-stableid-v8`). The bug escaped the existing `_check_stable_id_format` validator because self-analysis (the validator's gate substrate) contains no rst/vue/scss/etc. nodes; a DEEP cohort surfaced it as **1,223 nonconforming stable_ids on pretix** (rst directive/target, vue prop/slot, scss mixin) and **12,664 on chatwoot** (vue directive/prop/slot/style_block). Fixed by routing each emit site through the shared `make_doc_stable_id(language, path, kind, name, start_line, end_line)` factory (the same one markdown and gitignore already use — `sha256("doc:{language}:{path}:{kind}:{name}:{start_line}-{end_line}")[:16]`), folding `kind`+span so same-`name` siblings stay distinct. `Symbol.id` (the consumer-visible identifier / edge endpoint) is deliberately **left unchanged** at every site — only `stable_id` is canonicalized; the raw composite still serves as the node id (the node-id-format question is the separate INV-dulah invariant). Pre-existing per-analyzer tests that asserted `id == stable_id` were updated to assert the now-correct divergence (`id` retains the raw composite, `stable_id` matches the canonical regex). Scope discovery: the root-cause grep found exactly these nine `stable_id=<raw-id>` producers (markdown/gitignore were already canonical); the DRY follow-up that mints `id`+`stable_id` together so they cannot drift again — and unifies the three line-less analyzers' (`rst`/`robot`/`pony`) node-id format — is tracked on INV-dulah.
- **JavaScript/TypeScript class inheritance from external/library bases is no longer dropped — `extends LitElement` / `implements OnInit` now emit unresolved-external edges (WI-dutov, emission-parity F4).** The JS/TS inheritance-edge extractor emitted `extends`/`implements` edges only for bases that resolved intra-repo; any external/library base (`extends LitElement`, `extends React.Component`, Angular's `implements OnInit`) was **dropped entirely**, leaving the class an inheritance-graph orphan indistinguishable from a base-less class. When a base resolves to neither a project class nor a project interface, the extractor now mints an **unresolved-external** edge — `{lang}:{module_hint}:0-0:{base}:unresolved`, `is_resolved=False`, with a structured `dst_ref` when the import module is known — mirroring the unresolved-CALL convention (WI-banaf/WI-vurop). The edge type is **precise**: a new `_extract_implements_names` recovers the `implements`-clause origin so an external interface (`implements OnInit`) emits `implements`, not a mislabeled `extends`. The module hint resolves through the file's `named_imports`, then `namespace_imports` and the qualifier of a dotted base (so default imports `import Component from 'preact'` and namespace bases `import * as React; extends React.Component` recover `preact`/`react`, not the `external` sentinel), falling back to the honest `external` sentinel (no `dst_ref`) for non-imported globals. **Aliased** imports (`import { Base as B }`) are re-resolved on their canonical name first, so a local class imported under an alias resolves intra-repo instead of being mislabeled external; an aliased *external* base emits under its canonical export name. A base imported from a **relative** path that fails to resolve is treated as intra-repo (dropped) rather than mislabeled as a library. `_normalize_import_module_hint` now also strips URL/Deno schemes (`https://`, `npm:`, `jsr:`) and sanitizes residual `:` so a URL/Deno base specifier can't corrupt the colon-delimited symbol-id grammar (this also hardens the unresolved-call path that shares the helper). The core inheritance-linker's existing dedup means no duplicate edges. Scoped to the JS/TS analyzer (the highest-value, import-aware locus); the identical external-base drop in the Python/Kotlin/Ruby analyzers and the framework-agnostic core inheritance-linker, and the unhandled TS interface-extends-interface case, are tracked separately (WI-jubag and siblings), as is the eventual chokepoint form that carries import+clause metadata to the linker for all languages.
- **JavaScript/TypeScript route-handler symbols now populate `signature`, closing the last JS-vs-TS signature gap (INV-golap).** INV-golap recorded JS function/method nodes at `signature: None` on 0/10 (TS 25/26), but that asymmetry was **stale for ordinary constructs** — the `_extract_jsts_signature` code path postdated the measuring pass. A comprehensive re-probe across ~18 construct families (function declarations, arrows, async, class/static/async methods, getters/setters, object methods, named/default/const exports, default/rest params) confirmed JS already reached full TS parity for every *extracted* ordinary construct, and a whole-repo self-analysis measured JS 4/4 and TS 31/31 with **zero** null-signature function/method symbols. The re-probe isolated the one genuine residual: Express-style **route-handler pseudo-symbols** were emitted as `kind="function"` without ever calling `_extract_jsts_signature`, so an inline `app.get('/x', (req, res) => …)` handler — a real arrow/function node with `(req, res)` right there — carried `signature=None`. Fixed by adding `function_expression` to `_extract_jsts_signature`'s handled node types (covering anonymous/named function-expression handlers) and calling it at the inline-handler emit site.
